# Technical Debt Register

Statuses: Open, In Progress, Blocked, Done.

## ARCH-001 Monolithic Route/Domain/Mutation Coupling

- Affected area: Backend `app.py` and route modules.
- Evidence: Route scan still finds most operator workflows in `app.py`, including import review, library, cleanup, deduplication, playlists, Plex, configuration, transactions, and import endpoints. The external-engine branch changed the service boundary: Beets command arrays now flow through `job_engine._beet_run()`/`BeetsClient`, and `_db()` returns `RemoteSQLiteConnection` instead of a local file handle. The monolith still mixes routing, matching, orchestration, legacy SQL-shaped calls, and residual filesystem mutation call sites.
- Current risk: Changes to one workflow can accidentally alter unrelated behavior. Compatibility shims reduce migration blast radius, but they also hide whether a route is using a well-owned service method or a legacy remote-SQL/remote-command shape.
- Desired state: Routes remain thin. Application services own workflows. Domain modules own matching and safety decisions. Provider adapters own external calls. Repository/client methods own Beets-engine access.
- Safe migration approach: Extract one tested service at a time. Prefer replacing legacy SQL-shaped and command-array callers with explicit `BeetsClient` methods while preserving route signatures and responses.
- Priority: P0.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-002 Duplicated Matching And Confidence Rules

- Affected area: Import review, playlist processing, missing-track replacement, duplicate handling, MusicBrainz submission preparation.
- Evidence: Matching logic exists in `app.py` (`_track_ai_*`, import review revalidation, `_best_album_track_match`, playlist matching), `helpers_mb.py`, `backend/track_align.py`, `backend/mb_alignment.py`, and `backend/import_guard.py`.
- Current risk: Release-group candidates can be accepted or rejected differently depending on entry point. AI, AcoustID, tracklist, duration, and title evidence can be weighted inconsistently.
- Desired state: One shared matching result contract and one authoritative backend implementation for confidence, conflicts, warnings, and action eligibility.
- Safe migration approach: Define contract around existing strongest structures, add adapter tests for each entry point, then migrate callers one workflow at a time.
- Priority: P0.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-003 Mutations Do Not All Use One Controlled Boundary

- Affected area: Beets command execution, filesystem cleanup, metadata write, artwork repair, import, playlist placement, deduplication, replacement.
- Evidence: The `beets` control agent now owns Beets CLI execution, direct SQLite compatibility queries, tag writes, and file move/delete endpoints inside the authoritative engine container. `app.py` still contains many legacy command-array call sites and residual direct filesystem operations (`shutil.move`, `shutil.rmtree`, `Path.unlink`, `os.replace`, copies, and directory removals) that are not all planned, audited, and verified through one transaction boundary. `backend/transaction_engine.py` exists but is not universal.
- Current risk: Preview/audit/rollback behavior varies by workflow. Partial failure can be hard to recover or may be reported inconsistently. Residual filesystem call sites also make it easy to accidentally depend on web-manager media mounts that the supported two-service Compose files intentionally do not provide.
- Desired state: Mutating workflows use shared plan/apply/verify/recover semantics with root validation, diffs, audit records, and recovery information, and all media/Beets mutations execute in the `beets` engine boundary.
- Safe migration approach: Mutating workflows use shared plan/apply/verify/recover semantics with root validation, diffs, audit records, and recovery information, and all media/Beets mutations execute in the `beets` engine boundary. Wave 22 (PR #95) added `album_maintenance_v1`, `album_artwork_v1`, `folder_cleanup_v1`, and `playlist_media_cleanup_v1`. Wave 23 (PR #97) performed a mutation surface truth pass and scanner overhaul.   **Wave 25 Docker acceptance round (same PR #100):** building the dedicated Wave-25 two-service Docker acceptance scenarios this pass required actually exercising `import_folder_with_id` (the real, frontend-called `/api/folders/import-with-id` route) end to end, which surfaced two further real, previously-undiscovered bugs the mutation-inventory methodology was structurally blind to (it only flags *mutation* sinks; these are read/validation-path bugs): (1) `import_folder_with_id` validated the import source folder's *existence* via `candidate.exists()`/`is_symlink()` inside the `beets-web-manager` process itself -- but that container has zero filesystem visibility into `/data/media/music` or `/data/torrents` in either documented compose topology (only `/web-manager-data` is mounted, `read_only: true`), so every real call was guaranteed to 404 with "Source path does not exist" regardless of whether the folder was genuinely there. Fixed by dropping the local existence requirement (root-containment, a pure string check, is still enforced locally) and adding a real, fail-closed existence+directory-type check to `create_import_folder_plan()` on the engine side, which actually has the mount. (2) The Control Agent's `/import/plan` and `/import/apply` handlers built their allowed-roots lists from `os.environ.get("MUSIC_ROOT", "/music")` / `os.environ.get("DOWNLOADS_ROOT", "/downloads")` -- but `MUSIC_ROOT`/`DOWNLOADS_ROOT`/`STAGING_ROOT` are never actually set anywhere (neither compose file, nor `Dockerfile.beets`), so these always silently fell back to paths (`/music`, `/downloads`, `/staging`) that do not exist in the engine container at all, instead of the real bind-mount-matching module constants (`MUSIC_ROOT` = `/data/media/music`, `DOWNLOAD_PATH` = `/data/torrents`) already defined and correctly used by roughly half of this file's other handlers. `create_import_folder_plan`'s root-containment check therefore rejected every real source folder with `import_folder_path_out_of_root` regardless of where it actually was. Fixed for `/import/plan`/`/import/apply` specifically (the two handlers this round's acceptance work actually exercises).
  **Not yet fixed, tracked here rather than silently absorbed:** the same wrong-default pattern (`os.environ.get("MUSIC_ROOT", "/music")` instead of the safe `os.environ.get("MUSIC_ROOT") or str(MUSIC_ROOT) or "/music"` fallback already used elsewhere in `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, plus the equivalent `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`/`STAGING_ROOT`/`TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS` cases) still appears in roughly 20-30 other handlers in that file. Each needs individual verification (some may be genuinely unreachable with a real path, dead, or covered by a redundant downstream check) before a blanket fix -- out of scope for this pass; flagged here explicitly so it is not lost. A STATUSES-allowlist bug in `transaction_engine.py` was also found and fixed in this round: `store.update(operation_id, status="Recovery Required")` silently persisted as `"Pending"` instead (the value was never in the `STATUSES` set that `_status()` validates against) at every one of its several pre-existing call sites, not just the new `album_artwork_fetch_v1` family this round added.
  **Wave 25 Round 3 (`confirmed_import_v1`, same PR #100):** running the real Docker acceptance for `fresh-import`/`crash-resume-no-duplicate` (after fixing three unrelated `NameError`s found in the two prior CI iterations) surfaced a genuine architecture mismatch, not a small bug: `import_folder_v1`'s Apply step routed every import -- fresh and reimport alike -- through `reimport_source_atomic()` / `verify_deterministic_identity()`, a deliberate SEC-002 Wave 8 security control requiring the source audio's *own embedded* MusicBrainz tags to already match the target before import runs. That model is correct for genuine reimports of already-tagged library content; it is the wrong model for `import_folder_with_id()`'s actual job of tagging *untagged* downloads for the first time, which have no embedded MB tags by construction -- so it failed closed for essentially every real fresh import, not just this round's test fixture. Fixed by building a second, distinct controlled family, `confirmed_import_v1` (`transaction_engine.create_confirmed_import_plan`/`execute_confirmed_import_apply`, `beets_control_agent.run_confirmed_import_native`, new `/imports/confirmed/plan`+`/imports/confirmed/apply` routes, new `BeetsClient.plan_confirmed_import`/`apply_confirmed_import`), binding authorization to an immutable source-manifest digest + an explicitly reviewed concrete Release ID/RGID + best-effort track/fingerprint alignment (reusing `backend/track_align.py`, no new matcher) instead of pre-existing embedded tags. `reimport_source_atomic()`/`verify_deterministic_identity()` themselves were deliberately left untouched (no bypass flags added) and remain the reimport path's only gate -- see `docs/operations/wave25_import_reconciliation_design.md`'s "Round 3" section for the full before/after and the two trust models' security-model writeup. `import_folder_v1` (the old family) is left in place, untouched and still tested, simply no longer invoked by production code for this workflow. Known, documented limitation: crash-safety is proven for a crash *after* native import succeeds but before `Completed` is recorded (the required acceptance scenario, handled via an `mb_albumid`-uniqueness idempotency check); a crash *mid-subprocess* is a materially harder, pre-existing problem shared with `reimport_source_atomic`'s own design, out of this round's scope.
  **Wave 25 Round 4 (same PR #100):** the first real Docker run of `confirmed_import_v1` (Round 3 had never run against real Docker) found the round-3 blocker was in fact fixed, but two real bugs remained in Round 3's own new code: (1) `run_confirmed_import_native()` copied `--quiet-fallback asis` verbatim from `reimport_source_atomic`, which lets Beets silently import under an unreviewed identity (or none at all) whenever its match confidence for the forced `--search-id` candidate is weak -- fixed by switching to `--quiet-fallback skip`, and by having the native runner report `albums_before`/`albums_after` so `execute_confirmed_import_apply()` can tell a clean, zero-mutation skip apart from a genuine partial mutation under the wrong identity, which it could not do before. (2) the Docker acceptance fixture used flat ~0.3s synthetic files against tracks that are actually minutes long, which alone starved Beets' own match-confidence scoring regardless of tag/filename similarity -- fixed by fetching the real release's tracklist/durations from MusicBrainz once per run and generating duration-accurate fixture audio with basic (non-MusicBrainz) tags, asserted MBID-free before every import request. Also replaced the crash/resume scenario (previously only a restart+idempotency test, not a true crash-after-native-success test) with a deterministic, narrowly-gated failpoint (`confirmed_import_after_native`, `BEETS_ACCEPTANCE_MODE`-gated, inert in every real deployment) that reaches the actual required window without a timing race, and corrected the artwork scenario's plugin config (`fetchart` was never loaded at all) to report transaction-semantics and successful-acquisition as two separate, honestly-scoped claims. See `docs/operations/wave25_import_reconciliation_design.md`'s "Round 4" section for the full diagnosis.
  **Wave 25 Round 4, corrected (same PR #100):** the duration-accurate fixture above still failed the real Docker run with an unconditional `Skipping.` and no match detail, which led to two further hypotheses that were committed but never independently verified before the next Docker run: a `match.strong_rec_thresh: 0.25` relaxation, then a WAV-to-FLAC fixture conversion for tag-reading reliability. Neither was the actual cause. Per the standing instruction to prove rather than guess, the real cause was found by reproducing `beets.autotag.match.tag_album()` directly (outside Docker) against the fixture's own metadata: it returned zero candidates, not a low-confidence one. Root cause: Beets' `plugins` config key is not additive across config sources -- the acceptance engine's `config.yaml` set `plugins: fetchart` (added earlier in this same round to fix the artwork scenario) with no `musicbrainz`, which silently replaced Beets' own packaged default (`plugins: [musicbrainz]`) rather than adding to it, unloading the one plugin that resolves `--search-id` into a real candidate at all. Fixed by listing both plugins explicitly (`plugins: fetchart musicbrainz`); re-running the same local reproduction with the fix applied returns one candidate at distance `0.0` and recommendation `strong` under Beets' *default*, unrelaxed `strong_rec_thresh`, so the earlier `0.25` relaxation was reverted as unneeded (and had been masking, not fixing, the real bug). The WAV-to-FLAC fixture change was kept as a genuine but non-load-bearing reliability improvement. Also fixed in the same pass: the `two-service-docker-acceptance` GitHub Actions job installed no `ffmpeg` of its own, so the FLAC conversion step failed closed with a clear `RuntimeError` before any scenario could run at all on the first attempt after that change landed -- `ffmpeg` is now installed explicitly as its own workflow step rather than assumed present on the runner image.
- Priority: P0.
- Status: In Progress (Album Lifecycle & Import Reconciliation Domains: Complete & Verified; ARCH-003 overall remains In Progress for remaining non-album/non-import domains).

## ARCH-004 Job Persistence And Idempotency Are Uneven

- Affected area: `job_engine.py`, import review jobs, playlist download/sync jobs, AI batch import, acquisition, replacement, maintenance runner.
- Evidence: `JobStore` is in-memory. `PythonJob` supports structured state and cooperative cancellation. Some workflows add checkpoint files and uniqueness checks; others rely on route-local state or result inference.
- Current risk: Process restart, retry, or duplicate starts can repeat completed steps, lose progress, or leave stale active status unless each workflow implemented its own protections correctly.
- Desired state: Shared job requirements for operation identifiers, idempotency, resource locks, bounded retries, checkpoints, heartbeats, cancellation checks, and terminal-state recovery.
- Safe migration approach: Add job contract tests and a reusable idempotency/checkpoint helper. Migrate long-running workflows by risk, starting with import/replacement/playlist mutations.
- Priority: P1.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-005 Frontend Decision Logic Can Drift From Backend Authority

- Affected area: `frontend/src/features/importReview/ImportReviewPage.tsx`, other large feature panels, `frontend/src/api/types.ts`.
- Evidence: Large feature components render data, poll jobs, manage local workflow state, and calculate some block/eligibility display. API types are extensive and frontend panels sometimes adapt backend evidence shapes locally.
- Current risk: UI can enable, hide, or label actions differently than backend eligibility. User-facing explanations can diverge from backend safety decisions.
- Desired state: Backend returns authoritative evidence, conflicts, safety result, and action eligibility. Frontend displays those fields and only handles presentation state.
- Safe migration approach: Extend backend contracts first, then simplify frontend helpers as contract consumers. Add static and UI tests for visible evidence and disabled/destructive actions.
- Priority: P1.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-006 Provider Boundaries Are Inconsistent

- Affected area: MusicBrainz, AcoustID, OpenAI, Discogs, SLSKD, yt-dlp, Plex, Lidarr.
- Evidence: `helpers_mb.py` and `backend/slskd.py` are extracted boundaries, while `app.py` still contains direct OpenAI, Discogs, yt-dlp, Plex, and download orchestration logic.
- Current risk: Retry, rate-limit, secret redaction, and failure representation differ by provider.
- Desired state: Each provider has a small adapter with typed inputs/outputs, explicit transient/permanent failure classification, bounded retries, and redaction.
- Safe migration approach: Extract adapters only when changing a workflow for a real bug. Preserve API responses and add contract tests.
- Priority: P2.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-007 Raw SQL Compatibility Layer Bypasses Repository Boundary

- Affected area: Web-manager legacy `_db()`/`RemoteSQLiteConnection` callers and control-agent SQLite endpoints.
- Evidence: The original local SQLite violation has been removed from the web-manager service boundary: `app.py`'s `_db()` yields `backend.beets_client.RemoteSQLiteConnection`, and the actual `sqlite3.connect(LIB_PATH)` calls are in `backend/beets_control_agent.py` inside the `beets` container. However many routes still express data access as raw SQL-shaped calls over the remote compatibility layer instead of using explicit repository/client methods.
- Current risk: Lock handling, pagination, row factories, path normalization, query allowlisting, and write safety can vary by caller. The service boundary is safer than before, but the repository boundary is still weak.
- Desired state: A small Beets repository/client layer owns common reads/writes and lock retry policy. Web-manager routes call typed methods rather than raw SQL-shaped compatibility calls.
- Safe migration approach: Consolidate repeated read-only queries first by adding explicit `BeetsClient` methods backed by control-agent endpoints. Move write paths only when covered by mutation tests and transaction/audit records.
- Priority: P2.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-008 Agent Instructions Were Too Large And Duplicated

- Affected area: `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/AGENT_WORKFLOW.md`, `docs/AI_ENGINEERING_RULES.md`, and governance tests.
- Evidence: The integrated governance branch adds shared workflow documentation and static tests in `tests/test_engineering_governance_docs.py`. The instructions now distinguish review-and-fix work, push/PR/merge/deploy restrictions, migration-sensitive work, and the difference between implemented, tested, verified, ready to push, ready to merge, and ready to deploy.
- Current risk: Low. Future drift is still possible if agent files are edited without updating the shared docs and governance tests.
- Desired state: Concise agent files reference shared rules, and governance tests catch contradictory terminology or unsafe authority claims.
- Safe migration approach: Keep agent files short, keep project-manager assignments allowed, and update shared docs and tests together for rule changes.
- Priority: P1.
- Status: Done.

## ARCH-009 Release ID And Release-Group Identity Are Inconsistently Modeled

- Affected area: `app.py`, `helpers_mb.py`, `frontend/src/api/client.ts`, `frontend/src/api/types.ts`, `frontend/src/features/importReview/ImportReviewPage.tsx`, import review, folder import, repair, cleanup, playlist placement, and replacement workflows.
- Evidence: ADR-0002 defines MusicBrainz release-group ID as canonical album identity, while runtime code still carries both `mb_albumid` and `mb_releasegroupid` with mixed responsibilities. Examples where `mb_albumid` is treated as the primary operational album candidate include `app.py` functions `_resolve_album_release_for_import`, `_folder_release_preflight`, `_start_reimport_disk_job_internal`, `_album_mb_completeness`, and frontend fallback logic such as `initialMbid`/candidate matching in `ImportReviewPage.tsx`. Examples where release ID is legitimately edition-level evidence include `_fetch_mb_release_tracklist(mb_albumid)`, `_resolve_release_group_to_release`, `_playlist_album_tag_release_placement`, and `helpers_mb.py` release/recording candidate payloads that need concrete release tracklists, dates, country, medium, and track positions. Missing or inconsistent `mb_releasegroupid` propagation remains visible across API types and client payloads where some responses require only `mb_albumid`, while newer import-target preview and selected-match paths expose `release_group_id` or `mb_releasegroupid`.
- Diagnostic snapshot: On 2026-07-20, `git grep -I -o` at commit `1e065a6772bc6eed83e2d7be1c71dc3128285907` found 968 `mb_albumid` and 350 `mb_releasegroupid` occurrences across all tracked files in this branch. A source-focused scan over `app.py`, route modules, `helpers_mb.py`, `backend/`, and `frontend/src/` found 853 and 312 respectively. This is a dated diagnostic snapshot; it is not proof that every occurrence is incorrect and it is not a permanent invariant. The reviewer's approximate 572/172 count likely used a narrower or different search scope.
- Current risk: Matching, folder placement, imports, repairs, cleanup, and replacement can disagree about whether a MusicBrainz release ID or release-group ID is the album identity. A release-level candidate may incorrectly drive canonical folder identity, while valid edition-level release evidence is still required for tracklist and date comparison.
- Desired state: Shared matching and import contracts carry both fields with explicit names and semantics: release-group ID for canonical album identity, optional release ID for edition-level evidence, and recording IDs for track identity. No workflow substitutes a release ID where release-group identity is required.
- Safe migration approach: Do not rename fields globally. First add contract tests and typed result shapes around current entry points. Then update one workflow at a time to require/propagate `mb_releasegroupid` for album identity while retaining `mb_albumid` as representative release evidence. Keep API compatibility by accepting existing fields during transition and emitting warnings when only release ID is present for album-level decisions.
- Required tests: Unit tests for release-vs-release-group normalization, contract tests for MusicBrainz release and release-group candidates, import review tests for selected match propagation, playlist placement tests for representative release evidence, repair/replacement tests that keep release-group folder identity stable, cleanup tests that do not merge distinct release groups, and regression tests proving a release ID is never written where a release-group ID is required.
- Priority: P0.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-010 AI Batch Import State Writes Have No Versioning Or Compare-And-Swap

- Affected area: `app.py` AI Batch Import durable state (`_ai_batch_write_state`, `_ai_batch_commit`, `_AI_BATCH_STATE_DIR/<batch_job_id>.json`) and every route that reads-modifies-writes it outside the active worker thread. Direct writers, confirmed by inspection to call `_ai_batch_commit`/`_ai_batch_write_state` themselves from the route: `ai_batch_pause`, `ai_batch_stop` (also directly kills the JobStore job before its own whole-state write), `ai_batch_import_status`'s reconcile-and-commit path, `ai_batch_import_recover`'s reconcile-and-commit path, and `start_ai_batch_import`'s same-source-path reconnect commit. `ai_batch_skip` is *not* a direct writer -- confirmed by inspection it only mutates the in-memory `_ai_batch_controls` dict and sets `_ai_batch_skip_event`; it never itself calls `_ai_batch_commit`/`_ai_batch_write_state`. It is an indirect trigger: the active worker thread observes the control/event change on its own next iteration and is the one that actually persists the resulting state. An earlier revision of this entry overstated `ai_batch_skip` as a direct writer; corrected here.
- Evidence: `PR #12` fixed the retry/recover *start* path specifically, across three iterations. The first iteration (`_start_ai_batch_job`, `_run_ai_batch_import`, `_ai_batch_recalculate_batch_state` self-heal) added `_ai_batch_try_reserve_start`/`_ai_batch_release_start`, a process-local guard held only while `_start_ai_batch_job` itself was starting a worker. A second, independent review reproduced a remaining duplicate-worker race: that reservation was released as soon as `_start_ai_batch_job()` returned, before the worker it started had made its first commit. A second recover request landing in that post-start/pre-heartbeat window read the still-pre-reconciliation, all-terminal-looking `folder_states` snapshot; `_ai_batch_recalculate_batch_state`'s finalize branch stamped `worker_alive=False` over the correct just-computed value, so the second request didn't recognize the first worker as active and started a second one. Fixed by replacing the startup-only reservation with `_ai_batch_active_workers`, a registry authoritative for the worker's *entire* lifetime (reserve -> promote to job_id -> ownership-safe release in the worker's own `finally`, only after `_run_ai_batch_import` exits), plus making `_ai_batch_reconcile_state` defer entirely to that registry (skip recalculation/finalization) whenever a worker is registered for the batch, instead of trusting a possibly-stale folder snapshot. A third independent review then found the worker-lifetime registry itself had a post-spawn startup-abort gap: the spawned worker thread's `job_id_ready.wait(timeout=10)` ignored its return value, so a failure in `_ai_batch_persist_job_association`/`_ai_batch_promote_worker` *after* the worker thread was already spawned released the registry from the outer function but left the worker blocked, which then proceeded into real batch work unregistered and unvalidated once the 10s wait elapsed. Fixed with an explicit handoff protocol (`handoff_ready`/`startup_aborted` events plus a registry re-check, `_ai_batch_active_worker_job_id(batch_job_id) == owned_job_id`, as the actual proof of a valid handoff) that the worker must positively pass before calling `_run_ai_batch_import`; cleanup responsibility transfers to the worker thread as soon as it is spawned rather than being retained by the outer function. The underlying mechanism all three bugs came from -- `_ai_batch_write_state` does a whole-file JSON replace with no version field, no compare-and-swap, and no check that the writer's in-memory snapshot is still current -- is unchanged and still used by every route listed above. Any of those routes committing a stale snapshot while the worker thread is concurrently committing newer progress can still silently lose data, by the same shape as the bugs PR #12 fixed for the start path.
- Current risk: A pause/stop/status-poll/recover request landing at the wrong moment relative to worker progress can revert folder state, drop a requeue, or misreport batch status -- same failure mode as the original retry-race bug, just via a different route. Risk is currently mitigated only by these routes' commits generally being fast and infrequent relative to worker commits, not by any structural guarantee. `ai_batch_skip` carries a narrower version of this risk indirectly, through the state the worker itself later persists in response to the control/event change, not through a write of its own. The active-worker registry added in this update mitigates the *duplicate-start* instance of this risk (routes now consult `_ai_batch_worker_registered`/`_ai_batch_active_worker_job_id` rather than trusting a raw state-file read for that specific decision), but does not add versioning to the underlying writes themselves.
- Desired state: A single compare-and-swap or optimistic-concurrency primitive for `_AI_BATCH_STATE_DIR` writes (e.g. a monotonic `state["version"]` checked-and-incremented under `_ai_batch_state_lock`, rejecting a stale-based write) that all commit call sites go through, replacing the current unconditional whole-file overwrite.
- Safe migration approach: Add the version field and CAS check to `_ai_batch_write_state`/`_ai_batch_commit` first, in a mode that only logs a warning on a detected stale write (no behavior change), to confirm how often it actually fires against real traffic before making it authoritative. Then migrate call sites incrementally, starting with the routes identified above. Required regression tests for that migration: route-versus-worker stale-write detection (a route's commit using a snapshot older than the worker's latest must be rejected or merged, not silently overwrite), and confirmation that legitimate sequential commits from the same writer are never rejected.
- Note on runtime model: this app's supported deployment is one Waitress process with a thread pool, not multiple worker processes (see `_ai_batch_active_workers`' comment in `app.py`). A version/CAS field is still worth adding for correctness and clarity even in a single-process model, and becomes necessary (not just nice-to-have) if the deployment model ever changes to multiple processes or containers sharing one state directory. The active-worker registry is explicitly process-local only -- it provides no guarantee across multiple Python processes, multiple containers, or multiple hosts sharing the JSON state directory; durable/file-based locking would be required for that, tracked here as future work, not implemented in PR #12.
- Priority: P2.
- Status: Open.

## ARCH-011 Remote Beets Plugin Diagnostics For `/api/setup/status`

- Affected area: `routes_setup.py`, `backend/beets_client.py`, `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, and setup-status tests.
- Evidence: The integrated branch removes setup-status dependency on `_beet_binary()`/`_run_beet_diagnostic()` and normalizes one authenticated Beets control-agent `/status` snapshot into the existing public fields (`available`, `version`, `plugin_loader_ok`, `plugin_loader_error`, `configured_plugins`, `plugin_failures`) plus `loaded_plugins`, `installed_plugins`, `capabilities`, `commands`, and remote path/tool health. The control agent reports Beets version, loaded plugins, plugin failures, `submit` and `mbsubmit` command readiness, Chroma/fingerprinting, AcoustID lookup/submission, MusicBrainz submission, FetchArt, ReplayGain, LastGenre, Discogs, ListenBrainz, and Discpath state. Unit tests cover connection failure, authentication failure, timeout, malformed shapes, FetchArt loaded/failed, Chroma present/missing, independent submit/mbsubmit readiness, ReplayGain backend readiness, Discpath readiness, redaction, no local subprocess lookup, and one-snapshot reuse. Disposable runtime validation passed for the patched engine, Chroma disabled, invalid control-agent credentials, and unreachable control-agent cases.
- Current risk: Low. Future risk is mostly contract drift between `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, `backend/beets_client.py`, and `routes_setup.py` if one side changes without tests.
- Desired state: `/api/setup/status` reports authoritative remote Beets readiness, fails closed on remote errors, never falls back to a local Beets executable or local `find_spec()` as operational proof, and does not leak the internal API token or raw exceptions.
- Safe migration approach: Keep the current compatibility response shape for frontend callers. Extend the control-agent status contract only with additive structured fields. Do not treat setup-status display as a security boundary; command endpoints must continue enforcing capability checks themselves.
- Required tests: Covered by `tests/test_routes_setup.py`, `tests/test_external_beets_architecture.py`, image verification, and disposable Compose runtime probes.
- Priority: P1.
- Status: Done.

## ARCH-012 Library Disk-Walk Loses Real Albums Whose Folder Is Entirely Missing

- Affected area: `app.py` `_build_library_payload()`, specifically the leftover-`missing_by_bucket` injection pass that runs after the per-artist-folder walk (the "Any remaining missing items belong to artists not on disk at all" block).
- Evidence: Live verification against the real production database (3,144 items / 413 albums, confirmed by direct query) found `/api/library` reporting only 368 unique `album_id`s after the `_library_stats_for_artists()` aggregation fix (see the library-summary-aggregation fix on `integration/external-beets-engine-release-v2`) -- 45 real albums are not represented by any disk-walk card at all. Their items are all "missing" (file gone from disk), and the leftover-injection code's artist/album name-based lookup (`beets_album_lk.get((mart.lower(), malb.lower()), {})`) fails to match them back to their real `album_id`, so `ba_info2` stays `{}` and the card is built with `album_id=0` -- landing in the singleton bucket instead of being counted as a real album.
- Current risk: Medium. Read-only display accuracy only (no data mutation risk -- the underlying Beets database is unaffected), but `/api/library`'s `albums`/`tracks` totals undercount by roughly this margin whenever an entire album folder has been removed from disk while the Beets rows remain.
- Desired state: The leftover-missing-item injection should reliably resolve every fully-missing album back to its real `album_id`, most likely by keying off each missing item's own `album_id` (already present on the item row) instead of, or in addition to, the artist/album name-string lookup, which is fragile to normalization differences between the stored `albumartist`/`album` fields and the on-disk folder-derived name.
- Safe migration approach: This is disk-walk traversal logic -- CLAUDE.md's "`/api/library` is high risk, needs side-by-side parity checks before any traversal-logic change" applies. Do not modify `_build_library_payload()` without first building a side-by-side parity harness (old output vs. new output over a real or realistically-shaped fixture) so a fix here can't silently regress a different part of the walk.
- Required tests: A new parity/regression test modeling a real album whose entire folder is missing from disk, asserting it is still counted in `albums`/`tracks`, not `singleton_tracks`.
- Priority: P1.
- Status: Open.

## SEC-001 Retained Plex Credential After Diagnostic Exposure

- Date: 2026-07-29.
- Scope: `PLEX_TOKEN`, used only by `beets-web-manager`.
- Risk: the token appeared in private diagnostic session output during earlier work on this branch and must be treated as potentially exposed.
- Decision: owner reviewed the exposure and explicitly chose to retain the current token rather than revoke/replace it, accepting the associated risk.
- Existing mitigations: `PLEX_TOKEN` is sent via a request header, never a URL query parameter (query-string tokens are far more likely to end up in access logs/proxies). A stable, persisted, installation-specific Plex client identifier (`X-Plex-Client-Identifier`/`X-Plex-Product`/`X-Plex-Device-Name`) is now sent on every request, so any future rotation is cleanly attributable instead of facing the ambiguity that blocked attribution this time (the token has visibility into 35 authorized devices with no way to isolate which one originally produced it). Token values are never logged, printed, or included in reports.
- Future recommended action: rotate `PLEX_TOKEN` when convenient, using Plex Web -> Settings -> Account -> Authorized Devices to remove the specific session, then generate a replacement.
- Status: Owner accepted. Not a release blocker.

## SEC-002 Main-Branch CodeQL Backlog

- Discovery date: 2026-07-30, during final independent review of PR #49. A first query without `--paginate` under-reported the backlog as 30 open alerts; the authoritative paginated query against `main` at `e694a40e597428614b0be888b05ea5cc58ec7db9` found 320.
- Initial exact alert count: 320 open, `state=open`, `ref=refs/heads/main`. By rule: `py/path-injection` 195, `py/stack-trace-exposure` 81, `py/polynomial-redos` 18, `js/incomplete-url-substring-sanitization` 14, `py/incomplete-url-substring-sanitization` 4, `js/xss-through-dom` 2, and one each of `py/full-ssrf` (critical), `py/clear-text-storage-sensitive-data`, `py/bad-tag-filter`, `py/regex-injection`, `py/weak-sensitive-data-hashing`, `js/insecure-randomness`. By severity: 1 critical, 238 high, 81 medium. By file: `app.py` 277, `index.html` 12, `routes_submissions.py` 10, `routes_setup.py` 8, `backend/transaction_engine.py` 5, `frontend/src/views/Playlists.tsx` 5, `routes_lidarr.py` 3. By age: 313 of 320 (98%) were created in a single baseline scan on 2026-07-20; only 7 postdate that scan.
- Affected files: overwhelmingly `app.py` (86% of the backlog), a single ~49,500-line legacy module. See `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`/CLAUDE.md for why `app.py` remains the primary route module.
- Root-cause groups (full backlog, not yet individually triaged beyond this batch): the 195 `py/path-injection` and 81 `py/stack-trace-exposure` alerts are almost certainly dominated by a small number of repeated code shapes across `app.py`'s many route handlers (unsanitized filesystem helper parameters; `except Exception as ex: ... f"{ex}"` returned directly in a JSON response), based on the pattern found in this batch, but this has not been confirmed alert-by-alert for the remaining ~312.
- This batch (critical + 7 non-baseline alerts, owner-selected scope -- see below): 8 alerts individually traced and closed out:
  - `#18` `py/full-ssrf` (critical, `routes_submissions.py:1276`, `_fetch_open_graph_metadata`): **proven false positive**. Triple-layered: route-level `_validate_reference_url()` calls `validate_outbound_url()`; the sink itself re-validates immediately before request; `urllib.request.urlopen` is globally monkey-patched to `backend.security.secure_urlopen`, which re-validates and installs `SafeRedirectHandler` so every redirect hop is independently re-checked (closing the classic SSRF-guard-via-redirect bypass) and strips cross-origin auth headers. New tests: `tests/test_outbound_security.py` (`test_install_secure_urllib_patches_module_level_urlopen`, `test_fetch_open_graph_metadata_blocks_ssrf_before_any_socket_use`, `test_fetch_open_graph_metadata_uses_the_secured_opener_for_redirects`). No code change. Dismissed as `false positive`.
  - `#327`/`#328` `py/path-injection` (`app.py`, `_ai_batch_write_state`'s `os.replace` sink): **proven false positive**. `_ai_batch_state_file()` reduces `batch_job_id` to `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` before it is ever joined onto `_AI_BATCH_STATE_DIR`; no input can introduce a path separator. New test: `tests/test_sec002_codeql_backlog.py::AiBatchStateFileContainmentTests`. No code change. Dismissed as `false positive`.
  - `#331` `py/path-injection` (`app.py:1480`, `_write_plugin_job_beets_config`'s `write_text` sink): **proven false positive**. `temp_path`'s only variable component (`cmd`) is checked with exact-membership against the fixed 4-item `_PLUGIN_CMDS` allowlist (`ytimport`, `ytupdate`, `wlg`, `aisauce` -- none contain path metacharacters) before the call; CodeQL does not model the allowlist check as a taint barrier. New test: `tests/test_sec002_codeql_backlog.py::PluginJobConfigCommandAllowlistTests`. No code change. Dismissed as `false positive`.
  - `#332` `py/clear-text-storage-sensitive-data` (`app.py:1514`, `_write_job_beets_config`'s `write_text` sink; CodeQL's flagged source is `routes_submissions.py:60`, `_acoustid_key()` reading `ACOUSTID_API_KEY`/`ACOUSTID_KEY`): **genuine defense-in-depth weakness, fixed**. The AcoustID key can flow through `_acoustid_submit_config_extra()` into the generated temp beets config, which was written with the process default (world-readable) permissions and never cleaned up. Fixed by `os.chmod(temp_path, 0o600)` immediately after every `_write_job_beets_config()` write, protecting this and any future secret embedded via its `extra` parameter. New test: `tests/test_sec002_codeql_backlog.py::JobBeetsConfigSecretPermissionTests`.
  - `#334`/`#335` `py/path-injection` (`app.py`, `_folder_import_track_count`'s `Path.is_dir()`/`Path.rglob()` sinks): **genuine defense-in-depth weakness, fixed**. `source_folder` reaches this function through several layers of import-matching helpers; at least one traced call path is DB-derived (an existing album's on-disk folder, looked up by ID) but the function itself performed no containment check, so a future or undiscovered caller passing raw request text would have had an unbounded read-only directory walk. Fixed by constraining `source_folder` to `MUSIC_ROOT` or `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` via the existing `_path_is_under()` helper (no new resolver introduced), returning `0` (the function's existing not-available sentinel) for anything outside those roots. New test: `tests/test_sec002_codeql_backlog.py::FolderImportTrackCountRootContainmentTests`.
  - `#401` `py/stack-trace-exposure` (`app.py:10611`, `album_delete_art`): **genuine vulnerability, fixed**. The art-file `p.unlink()` failure path returned the raw exception text (`f"Could not delete {p.name}: {ex}"`) directly in the client-facing JSON error, while the sibling `clear_album_artpath()` failure path a few lines below it in the same function already logged server-side and returned a sanitized message. Made the unlink-failure path match that existing pattern. New test: `tests/test_sec002_codeql_backlog.py::AlbumArtDeleteExceptionSanitizationTests`.
- Alerts dismissed: `#18`, `#327`, `#328`, `#331` at merge time, all `false positive`, individually justified above. **Correction (added during Wave 2 review):** post-merge rescanning of `main` at the PR #52 squash commit found `#332`, `#334`, and `#335` still `open` despite the code fix already being live -- `os.chmod(0o600)` restricts access but does not change what CodeQL's `py/clear-text-storage-sensitive-data` query checks for (the value is still stored unencrypted, which is unavoidable here since beets requires a plaintext YAML value to authenticate with AcoustID), and CodeQL's taint model does not recognize the codebase's `_path_is_under()` helper as a sanitizer for `py/path-injection`. All three were dismissed individually after the fix was verified: `#332` as `won't fix` (architecture decision -- plaintext storage is required by beets, 0600 permissions is the applied mitigation, encryption is not viable since the app must present the credential back to beets in cleartext), `#334`/`#335` as `false positive` (fixed and proven safe by `FolderImportTrackCountRootContainmentTests`; CodeQL simply does not trace through the existing containment helper). This distinction -- code-fixed-and-then-dismissed vs. dismissed-without-any-code-change -- was not previously called out; all 7 of PR #52's dismissed/fixed alerts (`#18`, `#327`, `#328`, `#331`, `#332`, `#334`, `#335`) plus `#401` (auto-transitioned to `fixed` by CodeQL's own rescan, no dismissal needed) are the complete accounting for that PR.
- Security impact of that batch: closed the one critical alert and all 7 non-baseline alerts with either a proven-safe dismissal or a real fix; no user-facing behavior change for the false-positive/won't-fix group, and the three fixes were narrow (file permission, one added containment check reusing an existing helper, one error-message change).

### Wave 2: non-app backend files (routes_submissions.py, routes_setup.py, routes_lidarr.py, backend/transaction_engine.py)

- Starting count (live, paginated, re-verified independently of PR #52's report): 312 open on `main` at `332e12fbda00cedc0d95f9cebcf86baece0fb473` (320 initial minus the 8 alerts PR #52 closed). Confirmed unchanged from the reported figure.
- In-scope count: 25 (`routes_submissions.py` 9, `routes_setup.py` 8, `backend/transaction_engine.py` 5, `routes_lidarr.py` 3) -- one less than the originally estimated maximum of 26, since `#18` (already closed by PR #52) was itself in `routes_submissions.py`.
- Alert numbers: `#128`, `#28`, `#323`, `#30`, `#31`, `#32`, `#33`, `#34`, `#36` (`routes_submissions.py`); `#23`, `#24`, `#25`, `#26`, `#27`, `#102`, `#321`, `#322` (`routes_setup.py`); `#19`, `#20`, `#21` (`routes_lidarr.py`); `#129`, `#130`, `#131`, `#132`, `#133` (`backend/transaction_engine.py`).
- Root-cause groups:
  1. **Unguarded path resolution helper** (`py/path-injection`, `#128`): `routes_submissions._abs_resolved()` performed `Path(path_str).expanduser().resolve(strict=False)` with **no containment check at all**, and its caller chain (`_resolve_folder_submission_target` <- `_resolve_submission_target` <- `GET /api/submissions/target?path=...`) is reachable from an authenticated request query parameter with no other validation in between. This is the most significant finding of this wave -- a genuine arbitrary-directory-read/audio-tag-parse primitive anywhere in the container filesystem. Fixed by requiring the resolved path fall under `MUSIC_ROOT` or `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, reusing `_path_is_under()` and the `_SUBMISSION_ALLOWED_ROOTS` tuple -- both already defined in the same file and already used for a *different* check (cover-art path, line ~509) but never applied to this function.
  2. **Deliberately-raised, safe-message exceptions** (`py/stack-trace-exposure`, `#23`, `#28`, `#30`, `#31`, `#32`, `#33`, `#321`, `#20`, `#21`, plus all 5 `#129`-`#133` `py/path-injection` alerts): proven false positives across all four files. Each traced exception type (`ValueError` from `_write_env_file`/`_draft_target_ref`/`_validate_reference_url`, `KeyError` from `_resolve_submission_target`, `RuntimeError` from `_lidarr_config_error()` via `_http_error_message()`) is raised only with fixed, deliberately-crafted, non-sensitive text (env-var key names, numeric IDs the caller already supplied, or one of two hardcoded config-status strings) -- never a wrapped raw system/library exception. `TransactionStore._path()` requires a `txn_` prefix and rejects `/`, `\`, and NUL before any of its 5 flagged sink lines, and it is the *only* place in the file that builds a transaction path.
  3. **Genuinely broad `except Exception` leaking to a public response** (`py/stack-trace-exposure`, `#24`/`#322` (`_write_env_file` broad except, two call sites), `#25`/`#26`/`#27` (AI/MusicBrainz/Plex connectivity-test broad excepts), `#323`/`#34`/`#36` (submission-target/reference-url/musicbrainz-validate broad excepts)): fixed by logging the real exception server-side (`app.logger.warning`, type name only) and returning a fixed safe message, while preserving the existing `ValueError`/`KeyError` deliberate-message paths unchanged. Also fixed two **unreported** instances of the identical pattern discovered while reviewing the same functions (fpcalc connectivity check, AcoustID lookup check in `routes_setup.setup_test_acoustid`) plus one dormant/currently-unreachable instance (`routes_setup._check_path`, not wired to any route yet but tested directly and left for future use).
  4. **Incomplete URL substring check** (`py/incomplete-url-substring-sanitization`, `#102`): `"openai.com" in base_url` in `setup_test_ai` matched any URL merely *containing* that substring (e.g. `https://evil.example/openai.com`), which would select the wrong request path shape. Fixed with a real parsed-hostname check (`urlsplit(base_url).hostname == "api.openai.com"` or a proper subdomain suffix match). Low severity in practice -- this only picks a URL path shape, not an access-control decision -- but fixed to a correct pattern regardless.
  5. **Out-of-scope root cause surfaced in an in-scope sink** (`#19`): the actual unbounded `str(exc)` originates in `app.py`'s `_acq_fetch_lidarr_wanted()` broad `except Exception as exc: return [], str(exc)`, which is out of scope for this wave (`app.py` is explicitly excluded). Fixed at the in-scope boundary instead: `routes_lidarr.wanted_lidarr()` now only echoes the string when it is the known-safe `"not configured"` message, otherwise logs it server-side and returns a fixed message. `app.py`'s `_acq_fetch_lidarr_wanted` itself remains untouched and is presumably already counted among that file's 270 remaining `py/stack-trace-exposure`/`py/path-injection` alerts for a future `app.py` wave.
- Fixes: `routes_submissions.py` (`_abs_resolved` containment, `submission_target`/`submission_reference_url`/`validate_musicbrainz_release` exception sanitization); `routes_setup.py` (6 exception-sanitization sites + 1 URL-hostname fix, all in `setup_save_env`/`setup_test_ai`/`setup_test_musicbrainz`/`setup_test_acoustid`/`setup_test_plex`/`setup_regenerate_auth_token`/`_check_path`); `routes_lidarr.py` (`wanted_lidarr` boundary sanitization). `backend/transaction_engine.py`: no code change (all 5 alerts proven false positive).
- Dismissals: 14 total, all `false positive`, each individually justified and backed by a passing behavioral test in `tests/test_sec002_nonapp_backend.py`: `#23`, `#28`, `#30`, `#31`, `#32`, `#33`, `#321` (`routes_setup.py`/`routes_submissions.py` deliberate-message exceptions), `#20`, `#21` (`routes_lidarr._http_error_message`), `#129`, `#130`, `#131`, `#132`, `#133` (`backend/transaction_engine.py` `TransactionStore._path()`).
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_nonapp_backend.py`, 19 tests across `TransactionStoreIdContainmentTests`, `SubmissionFolderPathContainmentTests`, `SubmissionExceptionSanitizationTests`, `SetupExceptionSanitizationTests`, `LidarrErrorSanitizationTests`.
- Remaining backlog after this wave: 287 (312 - 25). All remaining alerts are in `app.py` (270), `index.html` (12), and `frontend/src/views/Playlists.tsx` (5).
- Next recommended wave: `app.py` itself, the largest remaining concentration (270 of 287, ~94%). Given this wave confirmed the two dominant repeated shapes (deliberate-safe-message exceptions that are false positives vs. genuinely broad excepts that need sanitizing; unguarded path-resolution helpers vs. already-sanitized ones CodeQL doesn't recognize), a per-rule sweep across `app.py`'s `py/path-injection` and `py/stack-trace-exposure` alerts (the two largest rule groups, ~195 and ~81 respectively before this wave) is likely more efficient than alert-by-alert triage, but `app.py`'s size (~49,500 lines) means this should be split into multiple scoped waves rather than attempted at once. `index.html`/`Playlists.tsx` (frontend, 17 alerts) need a separate frontend-focused wave.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged -- the `_abs_resolved` fix in this wave closed a genuine arbitrary-directory-read primitive; remaining backlog is still high-severity-heavy and concentrated in the customer-facing legacy `app.py`).

### Wave 3: app.py py/stack-trace-exposure (all 62 open alerts)

- Starting main SHA: `c92150a5c2f0275353aba28c5e0c77bcae00cb40` (PR #55 squash).
- Starting total open-alert count (live, paginated, independently re-verified): 287, matching the reported figure exactly. `app.py` `py/stack-trace-exposure`: 62 (also matching exactly).
- Alert numbers: `#37`, `#38`, `#39`, `#324`, `#42` through `#100` (non-contiguous; the full list is `37,38,39,42,43,44,45,46,47,48,49,50,52,53,54,56,57,58,59,60,61,62,63,64,65,66,67,68,69,70,72,73,74,75,76,77,78,79,80,81,82,83,84,85,86,87,88,89,90,91,92,93,94,95,96,97,98,99,100,102(app.py-scoped stack-trace group only)` plus `#324,#325,#326`).
- Root-cause groups:
  1. **Genuinely broad `except Exception`/specific-exception leaking raw text to a public response field** (majority of the 62; ~40 alerts across dozens of routes and helpers): fixed with the established pattern -- log the real exception server-side (`app.logger.warning`, type name only, never the message), return a fixed safe message. Includes several call sites managing the beets `config.yaml` file itself (`/api/config` GET/POST, `/api/config/revert`) -- the single most sensitive file in the container, since it can hold provider credentials, yet previously leaked raw filesystem-operation exception text on any read/write/backup failure.
  2. **Shared helper functions feeding many downstream sinks at once** (`#37` 4 sources / `#43,#44,#45,#46,#48,#56` overlapping sources / `#72-76,78,80` sharing one source): fixing the helper once closed every alert that traced through it. Notable: `_classify_openai_error()`'s fallback branch (`f"AI provider error ({exc})"`, feeding `#324`/`#39` plus 2 already-safe call sites) now returns only the exception type name; `_folder_cleanup_path()`'s except-branch (`f"Invalid path: {exc}"`, feeding 7 alerts across the folder-move/rename/merge maintenance route) now returns a fixed message; `_SpotifyFetchError`'s two raise sites (`#85`/`#86`) used to wrap the underlying urllib/json exception in violation of the "never wrap another exception" rule and now raise fixed messages.
  3. **Deliberately-raised, safe-message exceptions** (`#38`, `#44`, `#61`, `#62`, `#100`): proven false positives. `_start_metadata_apply_transaction()` (`#38`/`#100`, two call sites of the same function), `delete_import_review_folder()`'s `ValueError` branch (`#44`, verified against `_delete_review_source_folder()`'s raise sites), and `_folder_clean_root()` (`#61`/`#62`, after tightening its one path-embedding raise to a fixed string) all raise only fixed, deliberate `ValueError`/`RuntimeError` messages.
  4. **Job-log-only taint, no top-level response field affected** (`#43`, `#46`, `#68`, `#83`; the last with 5 separate `log.append()` sources): **won't fix**, per CLAUDE.md's explicit non-negotiable requirement that jobs expose "raw debug detail." Every one of these confirmed to feed *only* a `"log": log` list in an operation's response, never a top-level `"error"`/similar field, and none are provider/credential-adjacent (all are internal filesystem/DB cleanup operations).
  5. **Existing redaction helper not yet applied at one call site** (source line 1002, part of `#37`): the codebase already has `_redact_security_text()` (used elsewhere for yt-dlp auth-rejection reasons) but a yt-dlp cookie "smoke test" probe error wasn't routed through it. Fixed by applying the existing helper rather than adding a new one.
- Fixes: `app.py` only, ~48 call sites/helper functions across the file (path-cleanup, AI suggestion, Spotify/yt-dlp playlist import, Plex status/playlist-delete, config.yaml management, qBittorrent hardlink repair, transaction settings, library-health/duplicate-resolver/mb-completeness routes, maintenance-report readers, and more). No other files changed except one existing test harness (`tests/test_ai_auth_setup_hardening.py`, updated below).
- Test regression found and fixed mid-wave: `tests/test_ai_auth_setup_hardening.py::ClassifyOpenAiErrorBehaviorTests` used an AST-extraction harness that didn't stub `app` in its exec namespace (the fix added an `app.logger.warning()` call inside `_classify_openai_error`) and explicitly asserted the *old, unsafe* fallback behavior (`assertIn("weird provider response", result)`). Fixed by adding a minimal `app` stub (a `logging.getLogger` shim) to the harness namespace and rewriting the assertion to require the exception type name while explicitly asserting the raw message and an embedded secret are both absent.
- Dismissals: 5 total, all `false positive`, each individually justified and backed by a passing behavioral test in `tests/test_sec002_app_stacktrace.py`: `#38`, `#44`, `#61`, `#62`, `#100`.
- Won't-fix (job-log carve-out, dismissed individually on GitHub as `won't fix` with the CLAUDE.md raw-debug-detail justification -- these are not false positives, they are accepted-by-design and the hard "zero open PR alerts" merge gate requires a real disposition, not just documentation): `#43`, `#46`, `#68`, `#83`. A 5th instance of the identical pattern (`#413`) surfaced after the `delete_import_review_folder` fix itself, once CodeQL re-scanned: fixing that route's "error" field to a static string left its sibling "log" field (same `_delete_review_source_folder` job-log sources) as the sole remaining taint at that exact sink, so CodeQL assigned it a new alert number. Dismissed the same way.
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_app_stacktrace.py`, 16 tests across `ClassifyOpenAiErrorSharedHelperTests`, `FolderCleanupPathContainmentTests`, `FolderCleanRootFalsePositiveTests`, `SpotifyFetchErrorSafeMessageTests`, `ConfigYamlExceptionSanitizationTests`, `PlexStatusExceptionSanitizationTests`, `StartMetadataApplyTransactionFalsePositiveTests`, `PlaylistDeleteExceptionSanitizationTests`.
- Remaining backlog after this wave: 225 (287 - 62; all 62 individually accounted for as fixed, false-positive-dismissed, or won't-fix-dismissed -- zero open alerts remain scoped to this wave's PR head). By file: `app.py` `py/path-injection` 184, `py/polynomial-redos` 18, `py/incomplete-url-substring-sanitization` 2 (1 fixed this wave), `py/bad-tag-filter` 1, `py/regex-injection` 1, `py/weak-sensitive-data-hashing` 1, plus `index.html` 12 and `frontend/src/views/Playlists.tsx` 5.
- Next recommended wave: `app.py` `py/path-injection` (184 alerts, the largest remaining single group by far) -- given this wave's finding that CodeQL does not recognize this codebase's established containment helpers (`_path_is_under`, `_folder_cleanup_path`-style resolve-then-validate) as sanitizers, expect a similar high proportion of already-safe-but-unrecognized findings requiring per-alert trace verification rather than code changes, with a smaller number of genuine unguarded-path-helper fixes like `_abs_resolved` in wave 2. `py/polynomial-redos` (18) is a good second target -- narrow, mechanical, and independent of the path-injection triage. Frontend (`index.html`/`Playlists.tsx`, 17 alerts) needs a separate frontend-focused wave.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged).
- Status: Open. See the "Post-merge correction" note above for wave 3's actual final disposition (225 remaining, not 220 -- this line was a stale draft superseded after post-merge cleanup; corrected here to avoid two conflicting counts in the same document).

### Wave 4 (path-injection wave 1): app.py folder-cleanup/rename/merge cluster

- Starting main SHA: `92a7ea62274eb9de3e3a4aafd4e18152e7235109` (PR #56 squash).
- Starting total open-alert count (live, paginated, independently re-verified): 225, matching the reported figure exactly. `app.py` `py/path-injection`: 184 (also matching exactly, all high severity; 176 from the 2026-07-20 baseline, 8 newly numbered on 2026-07-30 from the post-wave-3 rescan).
- Cluster map (summary; full alert-number breakdown kept as local review evidence, not committed): the 184 path-injection alerts cluster around roughly a dozen functional areas by line-number proximity -- import review/folder import (~9100-12500), library duplicate/cleanup scanning (~15000-19800), AI batch/replacement acquisition (~21500-30250), qBittorrent hardlink repair (~32600), **folder-cleanup/rename/merge maintenance (~35800-36280, 32 alerts)**, artist-folder/root-folder repair (~38300-40650), submission/Plex/playlist paths (~41400-47020). Full per-alert line numbers recorded in the PR description.
- Selected cluster: **folder-cleanup/rename/merge maintenance** (`/api/clean/folder-placeholder/*`), alert numbers `256,257,258,259,260,261,262,263,264,265,266,267,268,269,270,271,272,274,275,276,277,280,281,282,283,284,285,417,418,419,420,421` (32 total).
- Selection rationale: matches cluster-selection priority #1 (request-controlled destructive paths: rmdir, rename, shutil.move) exactly -- `remove_empty_source` (rmdir), `safe_rename` (rename), and `merge_source_files` (shutil.move + rmdir) are all directly reachable from a single authenticated POST endpoint with attacker-supplied `source_path`/`target_path`. Internally coherent: every alert in the cluster traces through the same `_folder_cleanup_path()`/`_path_under()`/`MUSIC_ROOT` containment primitives, already deeply understood from wave 3's stack-trace-exposure review of the same functions. Deferred clusters (import review, library cleanup, AI batch, qBittorrent, artist/root-folder repair, submission/Plex/playlist) are read-heavy or already behind narrower request surfaces; none combine destructive mutation with as direct a request-to-sink path.
- Genuine vulnerability found and fixed: `_path_under()`/`Path.relative_to()` treat `MUSIC_ROOT` as "under" itself (`relative_to` returns `.`), so none of the three destructive actions refused the music library root itself as a source. Each is incidentally slowed by a "DB items tracked under source" or "folder is empty" check, but none of those is a reliable, by-design barrier -- a fresh/out-of-sync beets DB, or an emptied root after a merge, could let any of the three delete, rename, or dissolve the entire music library root folder. Fixed with a new `_folder_cleanup_is_approved_root()` helper (reuses `_path_under()`/`MUSIC_ROOT`, no new resolver), checked once at action dispatch in `apply_folder_placeholder_action_api()` for all three destructive actions.
- Second instance of the same gap, plus a narrower one of its own: `apply_safe_folder_placeholder_renames_job()`'s request-supplied `source_paths` branch computed a destination as `src.parent / clean_name` with no containment re-check -- safe for an ordinary subfolder (whose parent stays under `MUSIC_ROOT`), but with no guard against `src` being the root itself (whose parent falls entirely outside `MUSIC_ROOT`). Fixed with the same root refusal plus an explicit source+destination containment check immediately before every `rename()`, applied uniformly to both the request-supplied and background-scan code paths.
- Proven false positives: the remaining 30 of the 32 original alerts. Full review of `_folder_cleanup_path()`, `_folder_cleanup_file_inventory()`, `_folder_cleanup_db_items()`, and `_folder_cleanup_compare_files()` confirms every path reaching a filesystem sink in this cluster is either already resolved-and-validated via `_folder_cleanup_path()`/`_path_under()` before use, or derived from real filesystem enumeration (`rglob()` + `relative_to()`) that cannot itself reintroduce a traversal segment (a real filesystem walk can never yield a discoverable child literally named `..`). CodeQL does not model this codebase's containment helpers as sanitizers (the same non-recognition pattern confirmed across every prior remediation wave), so it continues to flag the `resolve()`/`rglob()`/`rename()`/`shutil.move()` call sites themselves regardless of the validation that precedes them.
- Architecture boundary decisions: none required. This entire cluster operates on `web-manager-owned state` under the app's own `MUSIC_ROOT` configuration value pointing at the shared Beets media volume -- it does not call into `BeetsClient`/the control agent, and no new engine-side operation was needed or added.
- Equivalent unreported flows: none found. The `rg` sweep across this line range (`open(|Path(|...|rename|...`) surfaced no filesystem sink outside the functions already reviewed above.
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_app_path_folder_cleanup.py`, 12 tests across `FolderCleanupPathContainmentTests`, `ApprovedRootSelfRefusalTests`, `SafeRenamesJobRootAndDestinationContainmentTests`. 7 of the 12 tests independently verified to fail against the pre-fix commit (4 real assertion failures, 3 `AttributeError` since the new helper didn't exist), confirming they exercise the actual vulnerability rather than merely the new helper's existence.
- Remaining backlog after this wave: 193 total (225 - 32; all 32 accounted for as fixed or proven false positive, zero dismissals needed since no alert met the strict false-positive dismissal bar independently of the code fix -- the root-cause fix is what makes the remaining 30 alerts' non-recognition classification provably correct, not a standalone dismissal). By file: `app.py` `py/path-injection` 152, `py/polynomial-redos` 18, `py/incomplete-url-substring-sanitization` 3, `py/bad-tag-filter` 1, `py/regex-injection` 1, `py/weak-sensitive-data-hashing` 1, plus `index.html` 12 and `frontend/src/views/Playlists.tsx` 5.
- Recommended Wave 2 (path-injection) cluster: the import-review/folder-import area (~9100-12500), the next-largest destructive-adjacent cluster (delete/move operations on review-queue source folders), followed by library duplicate/cleanup scanning and AI-batch/acquisition areas.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged -- this wave closed a genuine root-folder-destruction vulnerability).
- Status: Open. PR #52, wave 2, wave 3, and wave 4 (this folder-cleanup cluster) are closed; 193 alerts remain, requiring further dedicated waves.

### Wave 5 (path-injection wave 2): app.py import-review source deletion and per-file cleanup

- Starting main SHA: `cfc95ede8ebabebac0241897bdaaf29c786a779a` (PR #57 squash). Live paginated `main` inventory on this SHA returned 225 total open alerts and 184 `app.py` `py/path-injection` alerts, all high severity. The 32 Wave 1 alert numbers (`256,257,258,259,260,261,262,263,264,265,266,267,268,269,270,271,272,274,275,276,277,280,281,282,283,284,285,417,418,419,420,421`) are still listed open on `main`; excluding that completed cluster leaves the previously reported effective backlog of 193 total and 152 `app.py` path-injection alerts for wave selection.
- Remaining cluster-map summary (excluding Wave 1): folder import/preflight reads (`135-138`); metadata/art cache writes/deletes (`139-149`); shared browse/import path helpers and disk-art serving (`150,152-155`); import-review delete, stats, and per-file cleanup (`156-181`); album-art save/upload (`182-184`); library duplicate/import target scanning (`185-217`); AI batch, acquisition, and replacement staging (`219-252,414-416`); qBittorrent hardlink repair (`253-254`); artist/root-folder repair (`287-296`); playlist, Plex, and staging manifests (`297-320`).
- Selected cluster: **import-review source deletion and per-file cleanup**, alert numbers `156,157,158,159,160,161,162,163,164,165,166,167,168,169,170,171,172,173,174,175,176,177,178,179,180,181` (26 total), spanning `_pending_review_path_key`, `_library_no_mb_album_matches_folder`, `_delete_library_db_rows_under_folder`, `_delete_review_source_folder`, `/api/import/folder-stats`, `_remaining_audio_files`, `_import_review_cleanup_destination`, `_unique_import_review_cleanup_path`, `_prune_empty_review_dirs`, and `/api/import/review-files/cleanup`.
- Selection rationale: highest coherent remaining risk after Wave 1. This route family combines authenticated request-controlled and stored Pending Review paths with recursive scans and destructive sinks (`unlink`, `shutil.move`, `rmdir`, `shutil.rmtree`). The download/temp folder delete path previously accepted any configured cleanup-root subfolder without proving it was the Pending Review source, while per-file cleanup trusted the stored Pending Review folder itself at the filesystem boundary.
- Genuine fixes expected to close after rescan: download/temp source-folder deletion now requires Pending Review membership before `shutil.rmtree`; per-file cleanup now refuses music-library folders, approved roots themselves, outside-root stored paths, symlink folder escapes, symlink parent components, and symlink file escapes before `unlink` or `shutil.move`; folder-stats recursive scanning is now fenced to the configured review roots and music root and rejects root-self scans.
- Defense-in-depth fixes: import-review path text is validated through bounded URL-decoding while preserving literal percent filenames; traversal, dot segments, encoded separators, encoded NUL, decoded absolute paths, Windows drive/UNC/backslash attacks in container-style inputs, and double slash forms are rejected before path construction reaches the sink; quarantine destinations are canonicalized under `IMPORT_REVIEW_QUARANTINE_DIR`, checked for symlink components, and rechecked after uniqueness selection and directory creation so symlinked or replaced destination parents cannot redirect a move outside the quarantine root.
- Equivalent unreported flows fixed: the unreported `/api/import/folder-stats` arbitrary recursive scan and the symlinked quarantine destination-parent escape and destination-parent replacement race in `/api/import/review-files/cleanup` were fixed with the same import-review path contract. No Beets engine-side operation was added; this wave keeps direct destructive cleanup limited to provider download/temp staging and blocks per-file cleanup from mutating Beets/media-owned library paths.
- Proposed false-positive dismissals for Claude: final PR-head CodeQL after the code fixes reported 12 open `py/path-injection` alerts in the new import-review helper chain (`#424`, `#425`, `#426`, `#428`, `#429`, `#430`, `#431`, `#432`, `#433`, `#434`, `#435`, `#436`). Codex trace review classifies these as probable sanitizer-model false positives, not dismissed: CodeQL does not model the bounded path-text validator, fixed-root lexical containment, symlink-component rejection, canonical containment, trusted return values, or destination-parent revalidation as taint barriers. Behavioral coverage now proves literal-percent compatibility, outside/root-self rejection, symlink file and directory escape rejection, symlinked destination-parent rejection, destination-parent replacement after initial validation, and outside sentinels unchanged. Claude must independently decide whether to dismiss them.
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_app_path_import_review_cleanup.py` covers real Flask route requests, real temp roots, real files, literal percent filenames, stored hostile Pending Review paths, root-self refusal, encoded traversal, encoded NUL, raw-relative decoded-absolute input, symlink file and directory escapes, symlinked quarantine destination parents, destination-parent replacement after initial validation, and outside-root sentinel preservation.
- Remaining backlog estimate after rescan: if CodeQL recognizes all 26 selected alerts as fixed, effective backlog becomes 167 total and 126 `app.py` path-injection alerts, while the live count may still include Wave 1 until those already-completed alerts are reconciled. Status remains open; no alerts were dismissed by Codex.
- Recommended Wave 3 (path-injection) cluster: artist-folder/root-folder repair (`287-296`) because it is the next destructive media-owned mutation cluster and likely needs an architecture-boundary decision around Beets/control-agent ownership. qBittorrent hardlink repair (`253-254`) is a smaller high-risk candidate if Claude wants a narrower pre-wave.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged).
- Status: Open. This wave is code-fixed in the draft PR; ordinary CI is passing, aggregate CodeQL is blocked on the 12 proposed sanitizer-model false positives above, and Claude must make the dismissal/final-review decision.

#### Claude final independent review (PR #58)

- Independently re-verified, live and paginated, on `main` at `cfc95ede8ebabebac0241897bdaaf29c786a779a`: 225 total open alerts, 184 `app.py` `py/path-injection`, matching Codex's figures exactly. Also independently confirmed the Wave 1 (32-alert) discrepancy Codex flagged: those 32 alert numbers (`256-285` non-contiguous, `417-421`) are still individually `state=open, dismissed_reason=null` on `main` despite the Wave 1 fix having been live since PR #57's merge -- CodeQL's non-recognition of this codebase's containment helpers persists across rescans, it does not self-resolve. Per Section 30/Section 4 of this review's instructions, the "effective backlog" framing in the Wave 5 entry above is explicitly **not** used as a final count anywhere in this correction; all counts below are the live paginated figures.
- Reviewed the complete diff independently (app.py, the new test file, package-lock.json, TECHNICAL_DEBT.md) against the surrounding helper chain, request transport, and every one of the 12 PR-head alert traces (`#424-#436` minus `#427`). Concurred with Codex's classification that all 12 are CodeQL sanitizer-model non-recognition, not genuine vulnerabilities -- every flagged sink (`is_symlink()`, `resolve()`, `exists()`/`is_dir()`) operates on a value that already passed lexical containment against a trusted, non-request-controlled root (`DOWNLOADS_ROOT`/`_DOWNLOADS_ROOTS`/`MUSIC_ROOT`/`IMPORT_REVIEW_QUARANTINE_DIR`, the last read once from `os.environ` at request time, never from request data) and is canonically re-validated via `_path_is_under()` immediately after.
- Independent adversarial testing (not merely trace review) found one genuine gap Codex's review had not proven safe: the TOCTOU window between the final source revalidation (`resolved.resolve()` + `_path_is_under()` recheck) and the destructive `shutil.move()` call was wider than necessary, since destination-side work (parent resolution, symlink-component checks, `mkdir`) happened in between with no final recheck on the source. A constructed race test (source swapped for a symlink to an outside sentinel file at the exact instant `shutil.move()`/`os.rename()` would fire) additionally proved that `shutil.move()`'s destination-is-a-directory auto-descend behavior (`os.path.isdir(dst)` before deciding rename-vs-copy) would follow an attacker-planted destination-leaf directory symlink and write the quarantined file *inside* the attacker's target directory -- a real, previously-untested escape, independent of any of the 12 CodeQL alerts.
- Fixed both: (1) added a final `resolved.is_symlink()` check immediately before both the `unlink()` and the (now) `os.rename()` call, closing the bulk of the source-side race window; (2) replaced `shutil.move()` with `os.rename()` (falling back to `shutil.copyfile()`/`copystat()`+`unlink()` only on cross-device `errno.EXDEV`), neither of which auto-descends into an existing directory target the way `shutil.move()`/`shutil.copy2()` do, plus an explicit `dest.exists() or dest.is_symlink()` pre-check and a post-move `dest.is_symlink()` verification that undoes (`unlink()`s) and reports `skipped` rather than falsely reporting success if the source was swapped for a symlink at the exact rename instant. The residual race (an attacker who already has concurrent local write access to the downloads volume, winning a kernel-syscall-width window) is accepted and documented, consistent with the TOCTOU doctrine established in prior waves -- no portable application-level code can close a true kernel-level race, and an attacker with that capability already has direct filesystem access equal to or greater than what this endpoint would grant.
- Verified the `/api/import/folder-stats` response-contract change (200 OK with `exists:false` -> 400 with an error for any invalid/nonexistent/outside-root path) against the single frontend caller (`ImportReviewPage.tsx::requestDeleteFolder`): it already treats a thrown/non-2xx `getFolderStats()` response as best-effort (`catch { /* show dialog without stats */ }`), so the change is compatible with no frontend edit required.
- Verified the `frontend/package-lock.json` change: `npm ci` succeeds cleanly with 0 vulnerabilities and a clean working tree afterward; `npm ls brace-expansion` resolves to `5.0.9` (via `eslint -> minimatch`), matching the advisory fix; no unrelated version drift.
- Added 6 new tests to `tests/test_sec002_app_path_import_review_cleanup.py` (19 total, up from 13): a query-string encoded-traversal test against `/api/import/folder-stats` itself (not just the cleanup route), a deep-encoding test (4/5/6 percent-encoding layers, proving payloads beyond the 3-additional-decode budget fail closed because no downstream consumer decodes further), a source-replaced-by-symlink-before-`unlink()` race test, a source-replaced-by-symlink-before-move race test, and a destination-leaf-replaced-by-directory-symlink race test (the one that caught the `shutil.move()` escape above). All 19 pass; the two race tests that initially failed against the pre-fix code are what drove the two fixes in this correction.
- Live Linux-container adversarial validation (disposable, no production mounts) independently confirmed on the actual runtime platform: drive-letter/UNC/double-slash/relative-path rejection, single/double-encoded traversal rejection, real symlink-file escape rejected while a literal-percent filename (`100%25 legit.flac`) and a normal file were correctly quarantined with names preserved verbatim, the outside sentinel file's content was untouched and no symlink ever appeared inside the quarantine directory, and all three `delete-folder` authorization gates (not-pending, approved-root-itself, music-library-root-itself) held.
- Alert disposition (12 of 12, individually justified, see GitHub dismissal comments): dismissed as `false positive` -- `#424`, `#425`, `#426`, `#428`, `#429`, `#430`, `#431`, `#432`, `#433`, `#434`, `#435`, `#436`. Each dismissal comment names the specific runtime barrier at that exact sink (prior lexical containment against a trusted root, or canonical `_path_is_under()` revalidation immediately following).
- Wave 1's 32 still-open alerts (`256-285` non-contiguous, `417-421`) are **not** dismissed by this review -- they are out of scope for PR #58 (a different code cluster, already merged and independently re-verified safe in Wave 4's own review). They are noted here only to keep the live-count reconciliation accurate; a future maintenance pass should dismiss them individually against the Wave 4 fix, the same way this review dismissed Wave 5/PR #58's 12.
- Remaining backlog (live, paginated, post-merge -- see Section 41/42 of the final report for the exact post-merge figures): unchanged in shape from Wave 5's estimate once Wave 1's 32 and this wave's 12 are accounted for; the true `app.py` `py/path-injection` figure will not drop until CodeQL's own rescan re-numbers or closes the original `#156-#181` alerts, which -- per the established pattern -- may require a further individual dismissal pass after the post-merge rescan rather than an automatic closure.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged).
- Status: Open (SEC-002 overall). This wave (PR #58) is merged; see the PR's final report for exact merge SHA and post-merge verification.

### Wave 6 (path-injection wave 3): app.py artist-folder/root-folder repair -- Codex implementation, pending Claude final review

- Starting main SHA: `170650c8432dd22b62dfb8d20f8d17c50e1db123` (PR #58 squash). Live paginated `main` inventory on this SHA: 216 total open alerts, 175 `app.py` `py/path-injection`, all high severity, matching the reported figures exactly. Latest Python CodeQL analysis is on this exact SHA.
- Known fixed-but-open prior-wave alerts (not subtracted from the live totals above): Wave 1's 32 (`256-285` non-contiguous, `417-421`) remain open; of Wave 2/5's 26 original (`#156-#181`), 9 auto-closed as `fixed` on the post-merge rescan and 17 remain open. Both sets are pre-existing debt, out of scope for this wave.
- Wave 3 original alert inventory (confirmed live, unchanged, one coherent cluster): `#287` (app.py:38582), `#288`/`#289` (38769), `#290`/`#291` (38811), `#292` (38984), `#293` (39179), `#294` (39195), `#295`/`#296` (39335) -- 10 alerts total, all `py/path-injection`, all still open, all tracing to the same root cause.
- Functions/routes: `_apply_artist_folder_groups()`, `_scan_artist_folder_groups()`, `_stamp_artist_folder_scan()`, `_stamp_artist_folder_album_mbid_counts()`, `_auto_merge_case_duplicate_artist_folder()`, `POST /api/clean/artist-folders/scan`, `POST /api/clean/artist-folders/merge`, `POST /api/clean/artist-folders/stamp-mbid`.
- Genuine vulnerability found and fixed: all three routes accepted a `root` request value (defaulting to `MUSIC_ROOT` but freely overridable -- the frontend exposes a free-text root field) with only an `exists()`/`is_dir()` check, no containment against `MUSIC_ROOT` at all. `_apply_artist_folder_groups()` and the `_stamp_artist_folder_*` chain then perform destructive filesystem operations (`shutil.move`, `rmdir`-equivalent removal, `Path.rename`) and direct Beets-DB path rewrites (via the already-architecture-compliant `RemoteSQLiteConnection`) driven entirely by that unrestricted root. An authenticated caller could point `root` at any directory on the container filesystem reachable from the web-manager process and have "duplicate artist folder" merge logic move, merge, or remove files there.
