Fixed with a new `_artist_folder_repair_root()` helper (reuses the wave 2 `_import_review_path_text_error`/`_path_lexically_under`/`_path_has_symlink_component_under`/`_path_is_under` helpers -- no new resolver), applied at all three route entry points and defensively inside `_apply_artist_folder_groups()` itself (the function's own alert-#287 sink). `root` must resolve to `MUSIC_ROOT` itself or a real subdirectory of it; internal trusted callers (`_auto_merge_case_duplicate_artist_folder`, always invoked with `MUSIC_ROOT` directly) are unaffected since they already satisfy the new constraint.
- Defense-in-depth / matching-policy fix (found during review, not itself a CodeQL alert): `_apply_artist_folder_groups()`'s core "duplicate artist folder" grouping (`_scan_artist_folder_groups()`) groups folders by normalized-name equality alone -- the `use_musicbrainz` flag only affects which name/casing is chosen as canonical, never whether folders are considered duplicates. This meant the merge path could commingle two different artists' catalogs purely because their folder names happened to normalize the same way (case/punctuation variants), violating the project's non-negotiable "do not merge on fuzzy name matching alone" rule -- and inconsistent with the sibling `stamp-mbid` feature's plain-name-duplicate path, which already AcoustID-fingerprint-verifies before merging. Fixed by requiring the same `_artist_folder_fingerprint_confirms()` check (reused, not duplicated) before merging any group with no MusicBrainz artist ID evidence; a confirmed mismatch skips that source folder instead of merging it. Groups with real MB Artist ID evidence are unaffected and skip the (network-dependent) fingerprint check entirely.
- Architecture boundary decision: none required, consistent with Wave 1's precedent for the folder-cleanup cluster. The direct filesystem mutation and Beets-DB writes in this cluster are the same class of residual `app.py` direct-mutation debt already tracked as ARCH-003 in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`; the DB access itself already goes through `backend.beets_client.RemoteSQLiteConnection` (confirmed by reading `_db()`'s definition), not a local SQLite handle, so no architecture-compliance regression exists. No new Beets/control-agent engine capability was added or judged necessary for this wave.
- Equivalent unreported flows: none found beyond the two fixes above. Every filesystem sink in `_merge_artist_dir_contents()`, `_replace_stamp_db_path_prefixes()`/`_replace_stamp_db_exact_paths()`, and the candidate-construction code in `_stamp_artist_folder_scan()` operates on values structurally guaranteed to be direct children of (or renamed variants within) the now-validated `root_path`, by construction of `_scan_artist_folder_groups()`'s and `_stamp_artist_folder_scan()`'s own iteration logic (`root_path.iterdir()`), so no additional containment gap was found. `/api/clean/album-folders/apply-issue` (a neighboring but functionally distinct route) was reviewed only enough to confirm it is a separate cluster, not touched here.
- Proposed false-positive dismissals: superseded by Claude's final review below (the root contract change renumbered these to `#454`-`#456`; all three dismissed after independent verification -- see the Claude final review subsection for the exact justification).
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_app_path_artist_root_repair.py`, 18 tests across `ArtistFolderRepairRootBoundaryTests` (outside-root/prefix-collision/symlink/encoded-traversal/Windows-UNC rejection and music-root/subfolder acceptance, via real Flask requests and a synchronous job-body run proving the background job captures the validated root, not raw request text), `ArtistFolderRepairRootHelperTests` (literal-percent preservation, deep-encoding, NUL rejection), and `ArtistFolderMergeIdentityTests` (fingerprint-mismatch blocks a name-only merge, fingerprint-confirm allows it, MB-ID-evidenced groups skip the fingerprint check entirely). 11 of 18 tests independently verified to fail (8 failures + 3 errors) against the pre-fix commit via `git stash`/`git stash pop`, confirming they exercise the actual vulnerabilities.
- Remaining live-count estimate: not claimed here -- see the draft PR's CodeQL results for the exact live count on the pushed head; per the task's own live-inventory discipline, no reduced "effective" total is reported.
- Recommended Wave 4 cluster: album-folder relocation/duplicate-cleanup (`/api/clean/album-folders/*`) and the remaining library duplicate/import-target scanning area, both still-open path-injection concentrations noted in Wave 5's cluster map.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged -- this wave closes a genuine unrestricted-root arbitrary-directory read/write/delete primitive reachable from three authenticated destructive routes).

#### Claude final independent review (PR #59)

- Independently re-verified, live and paginated, on `main` at `170650c8432dd22b62dfb8d20f8d17c50e1db123`: 216 total open, 175 `app.py` `py/path-injection`, matching Codex's figures exactly. Independently re-traced `#287`/`#292`/`#293` via the alert-instances API against this exact SHA and confirmed the same root cause and line numbers Codex reported.
- **Genuine defect found and fixed -- accepted-root contract was too permissive.** Codex's `_artist_folder_repair_root()` accepted `MUSIC_ROOT` *or any real descendant of it*. The frontend labels this field "Library root" (`ArtistFoldersPanel.tsx`) with no supported concept of scanning an individual artist or album folder -- there is only one `MUSIC_ROOT`, no multi-section library concept anywhere in the codebase. Accepting a descendant (e.g. an artist folder) as `root` would make `_scan_artist_folder_groups()` misinterpret that folder's own children (album folders) as "duplicate artist folders," destructively merging two unrelated albums together and rewriting the Beets DB's `albumartist` column with an album folder's name. Fixed by requiring `root` to resolve to *exactly* `MUSIC_ROOT`, not a descendant. Also made `MUSIC_ROOT.resolve()` failure fail closed (reject) instead of silently falling back to an unresolved comparison basis, per the review's explicit instruction.
- **Genuine defect found and fixed -- fingerprint tri-state fail-open bug.** `_artist_folder_fingerprint_confirms()` returns a real tri-state `Optional[bool]`: `True` (confirmed match), `False` (confirmed mismatch), or `None` (no usable evidence -- empty folder, fpcalc/AcoustID unreachable, provider error, or no candidate had a usable artist field). Both Codex's new check in `_apply_artist_folder_groups()` and the **pre-existing** (not introduced this wave) plain-name-duplicate check in `_stamp_artist_folder_scan()` only blocked on `fp_result is False`, silently letting `None` fall through and authorize the merge -- i.e. whenever the fingerprint service simply failed to produce a result, the safety gate did nothing. Fixed both call sites to require `fp_result is True` explicitly; both `False` and `None` now block and log a distinct reason. This is the single most safety-critical finding in this review: the previous code's *entire point* was to prevent silently commingling two different artists' catalogs, and an unavailable AcoustID/fpcalc service (a very plausible real-world condition) defeated it completely.
- **Genuine defect found and fixed -- weak MB Artist ID evidence bypassed fingerprint verification.** `_apply_artist_folder_groups()` skipped the fingerprint check entirely whenever the group had any non-empty `mb_artistid`. That ID is derived from `_mb_canonical_for_artist_entries()`, which performs a MusicBrainz *text search on each folder's name* -- not per-folder audio-content evidence. Two different real artists who happen to share a folder name would produce the same (or a similarly ambiguous) name-search result, so this "evidence" is vulnerable to exactly the collision scenario the fingerprint check exists to catch. Fixed by requiring fingerprint confirmation (`is True`) for every source unconditionally, regardless of `mb_artistid` presence; the ID is still used for canonical-name selection and DB tagging (a separate, lower-severity, pre-existing concern noted below), just no longer trusted to authorize the file merge itself.
- **Pre-existing bug found and fixed while reviewing the above (not itself a CodeQL alert or a Wave 3 regression):** `_stamp_artist_folder_scan()`'s plain-name-duplicate skip branch added the skipped folder's own path to `candidate_paths`, a set whose sole purpose elsewhere in the function is tracking paths that became *real candidates* (used to drop now-stale `skipped` entries from earlier loops). Adding a skip-only path there caused the function's own end-of-scan filter to erroneously discard that exact skip entry, silently hiding the fingerprint-mismatch/unavailable reason from the operator -- directly undermining the visibility the fix above depends on for "leave for review" to mean anything. Fixed by removing that erroneous line; confirmed via a direct unit test that the skip reason now survives to the response.
- MB Artist ID evidence quality (task's "album identity"/"MB Artist ID Evidence Decision" sections): given fingerprint verification is now unconditional for every merge, `mb_artistid` consistency across sources is no longer the operative safety gate for the file-merge decision. It remains used only for canonical-name choice and DB `mb_albumartistid` tagging on the canonical entry's *existing* rows (a pre-existing behavior, not part of Wave 3's diff) -- writing a name-search-derived MBID onto already-imported catalog metadata independent of whether any merge actually occurred. This is a real, narrower, lower-severity data-quality concern (mistagging, not destructive file mutation) that was consciously **not** fixed in this PR to avoid scope creep into `_mb_canonical_for_artist_entries()`'s broader design; recorded here as a specific, actionable follow-up rather than left implicit.
- Architecture boundary decision (independently re-examined, not accepted from Codex's "Wave 1 precedent" framing alone): **Option A, narrowly secured legacy flow**, for the reviewed cluster's actual filesystem/DB operations -- DB writes already go through `RemoteSQLiteConnection` (verified by reading `_db()`), and direct filesystem mutation is the same already-tracked ARCH-003 debt Wave 1 accepted for an equivalent cluster. However, independent review found a **more specific, previously-undocumented** architecture violation in the same function: `_apply_artist_folder_groups()`'s post-merge tag-write step calls `subprocess.run([BEET_BIN, "-c", cfg, "write", ...])` -- a **local subprocess shell-out**, not `backend.beets_client.BeetsClient.run_command()` (an existing remote-execution method already used elsewhere for exactly this purpose). Per `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own text, Beets command arrays "must not shell out in the web-manager container." Since the web-manager image ships zero Beets binaries, this call fails every time in the actual deployed topology (caught by a broad `except Exception`, logged as a `WARN`), meaning the "write tags for N albums" step is a silent no-op in production today. `BEET_BIN`/local `subprocess.run` appears at ~47 sites across `app.py`, not unique to this cluster -- fixing it here would mean auditing and testing dozens of unrelated call sites, far outside a path-injection wave's narrow scope. **Not fixed in this PR; documented instead** as a new, more specific, actionable architecture item (broken functionality, not just style debt) recommended for its own dedicated wave -- see Recommended Wave 4 below. This does not block the path-injection/identity fixes in this PR, which are independent of whether the tag-write step itself functions.
- Test quality: added 5 new tests (23 total in the wave-3 file, up from 18) covering the corrected root contract (`test_scan_rejects_subfolder_of_music_root`, `test_subfolder_of_music_root_is_rejected`, literal-percent re-targeted at `MUSIC_ROOT` itself), the fingerprint fail-open fix (`test_name_only_group_blocked_when_fingerprint_unavailable`, `test_group_with_musicbrainz_artist_id_blocked_on_fingerprint_mismatch`, `test_group_with_musicbrainz_artist_id_still_requires_fingerprint_confirmation`), and a new `StampMbidPlainNameDuplicateFingerprintTests` class covering the pre-existing `_stamp_artist_folder_scan()` path's identical tri-state contract and the `candidate_paths` visibility bug. 6 of the (now) 23 tests independently verified to fail against Codex's pre-review head via `git stash`/`git stash pop`. Also corrected a pre-existing, unrelated source-text test (`tests/test_dedup_acoustid_fingerprint.py::test_artist_folder_name_merge_fingerprint_verified`) whose fixed-offset extraction window no longer reached the relevant code after this review's edits added an explanatory comment; widened the window and strengthened its assertion to check for `if fp_result is not True:` explicitly.
- Final reviewed head: `f2442f4d5ab115aea4d4ee0e024d021a162a35b4`. PR-head CodeQL after the review's corrections reported 3 open `py/path-injection` alerts (renumbered from the original `#452`-`#454` to `#454`-`#456` by the root-contract fix): `#454` (app.py:38609, `candidate.resolve(strict=False)`), `#455`/`#456` (app.py:38606, `candidate.exists()`/`candidate.is_dir()`). All three sit after an exact-equality proof (`candidate != music_root -> reject`, two-to-three lines above each sink) that `candidate` literally equals the trusted, canonically-resolved `MUSIC_ROOT` constant -- a strictly stronger barrier than the original wave's lexical-containment check, and still not recognized by CodeQL's taint model. All three individually dismissed as `false positive` with sink-specific justifications. 0 open PR alerts; aggregate CodeQL check green.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged).
- Status: Open (SEC-002 overall). Reviewed, corrected, and merged by Claude -- see the final report for exact squash SHA and post-merge verification.

### Path-Injection Wave 4: album-folder relocation, duplicate cleanup, and import-target boundaries -- Codex implementation, pending Claude final review

- Starting main SHA: `15e1068a1fc1770684205f3c72fffe2f928572ea` (PR #59 squash). Live paginated `main` inventory on this SHA: 209 total open alerts, 192 `app.py` alerts, 168 `app.py` `py/path-injection`, all high severity. Latest Python CodeQL analysis: `1567867616` on commit `15e1068a1fc1770684205f3c72fffe2f928572ea`.
- Fixed-but-open prior-wave accounting, not subtracted from the live totals: Wave 1's 32 (`#256`-`#272`, `#274`-`#277`, `#280`-`#285`, `#417`-`#421`); Wave 2's 17 still-open original alerts (`#156`-`#163`, `#166`-`#170`, `#174`-`#176`, `#178`); Wave 3's 3 still-open reviewed false positives (`#292`-`#294`).
- Current cluster map summary: album art save/upload (`#182`-`#184`, deferred); Import Review album target/candidate scanning and apply selection (`#192`-`#217`, selected); AI/library suggestion scans (`#185`-`#191`, deferred); album-folder cleanup under `/api/clean/album-folders/*` (no direct live CodeQL alert on the route, but equivalent stored-path destructive album relocation/duplicate-cleanup flow, selected as connected hardening); remaining unrelated acquisition, playlist, and maintenance path clusters deferred.
- Selected original alerts: `#192`-`#217` (26 live `app.py` `py/path-injection` alerts). Routes/helpers include `/api/candidates/<mb_albumid>/tracks`, `/api/folders/import-target-preview`, `/api/folders/import-with-id`, `/api/import/review-files/cleanup`, `/api/import-review/auto-enqueue`, `/api/import-review/auto-enqueue/reconcile`, `/api/import/reconcile-job`, `_candidate_track_local_candidates()`, `_target_preview_source_files()`, `_import_review_selected_source_files()`, `_remaining_audio_files()`, `_review_paths_equal()`, and `_normalised_submission_files()`.
- Root causes fixed: request and stored source paths reached `Path()`, `stat()`, `rglob()`, `exists()`, selected-file staging, and path-equality sinks without a single trusted returned path contract; album cleanup apply trusted stored scan issue paths when moving/quarantining files and pruning folders. Added a shared Import Review source resolver that preserves literal-percent POSIX filenames while rejecting traversal, encoded traversal, encoded NUL/slash/backslash, backslash/UNC/drive-style container paths, approved roots, outside roots, symlink components, symlink leaves, missing sources, and wrong expected types. Stored DB paths are re-rooted under `MUSIC_ROOT` only after validation and are treated as untrusted at every filesystem boundary.
- Release Group identity policy: album cleanup apply now rechecks that the selected issue carries a valid MusicBrainz Release Group ID immediately before mutation. Missing RGID, blocked status, or conflict-tagged issue types block instead of moving files. Confirmed `Needs review` issues with a valid RGID remain supported for the existing manual apply contract; name-only, Release ID-only, AI, and fuzzy evidence are not sufficient.
- Track/file collision policy: import-target preview and selected-file import only operate on canonical source files under the approved source root; nested symlink files are ignored. Album cleanup never silently overwrites a target file; an introduced destination collision or unverifiable/different same-name file blocks. Verified duplicate quarantine rechecks the source/target file identity immediately before moving to trash.
- Path/TOCTOU corrections: album cleanup validates canonical source/target paths in planning and again in apply, rejects root-self, outside-root, symlink source/destination/parent components, symlinked trash parents, unsafe destination parents, and destination paths outside the approved canonical album folder. Source files, target files, and duplicate targets are revalidated at sink time; DB update failure is now reported as failure instead of being counted as success.
- Architecture decision: added one narrow engine-side operation, `POST /library/rewrite-path`, exposed through `BeetsClient.rewrite_library_path()`, so album cleanup updates Beets-owned item/art paths through the control-agent lock instead of raw SQL or local SQLite. The operation accepts only old/new music-library paths, rejects root-self/outside/symlink-component inputs, revalidates under the engine lock, stores the canonical relative destination path, and returns structured row counts. The legacy direct filesystem move portion remains ARCH-003 debt but is now bounded and failure-truthful. The selected cluster does not introduce a new local `BEET_BIN` call; the broader approximately 47-site `BEET_BIN`/local-subprocess architecture issue remains separate debt and was not migrated here.
- Tests added/updated: new `tests/test_sec002_app_path_album_folder_repair.py` covers real temp roots, actual Flask requests, synchronous album-cleanup job execution, real files, symlinks where available, hostile stored paths, outside-root sentinels, literal-percent filenames, and SQLite path update assertions, exact BeetsClient path-rewrite calls, and direct control-agent path-rewrite coverage. `tests/test_album_folder_cleanup.py` loader was updated so its AST-isolated helper tests include the new trusted-path and identity helper chain.
- Expected CodeQL disposition: selected original alerts are fixed in code or expected to become sanitizer-model false positives on the PR head; Codex did not dismiss any alerts. Any remaining PR-head alerts require Claude's independent trace review and individual dismissal decision where justified.
- Remaining live-count estimate before PR-head CodeQL: live `main` remains 209 total open and 168 `app.py` path-injection alerts. This wave is expected to address 26 selected original alerts plus equivalent unreported album-cleanup stored-path flows; exact remaining count is authoritative only after PR-head CodeQL and/or a post-merge `main` rescan.

#### Claude final independent review (PR #60)

- Independently re-verified live paginated `main` at `15e1068a1fc1770684205f3c72fffe2f928572ea`: 209 total open, 168 `app.py` `py/path-injection`, matching exactly. Independently re-traced all 26 original alerts (`#192`-`#217`) to the exact reported functions/lines.
- Issue provenance (task's central concern): independently confirmed `/api/clean/album-folders/apply-issue` does **not** trust a client-supplied issue payload -- it takes only `issue_id`, runs a fresh server-side rescan (`_album_folder_cleanup_plan`), and looks the issue up by ID from that fresh scan's own results. The ID (`_album_cleanup_issue_id`) is a SHA1 hash of the sorted source folder paths, canonical path, and issue types, so any relevant filesystem change between review and apply changes the hash and the apply is refused ("issue not found") rather than silently applying stale/different content. This is a sound, already-safe stale-plan defense; the task's provenance concern does not require a fix here.
- File collision identity: independently confirmed `_album_cleanup_verified_same_file()` requires a real SHA1 content hash match (`_album_cleanup_file_hash()`, streamed full-file read), not filename/size/duration/tags. This is genuine byte-identity proof; no fix needed.
- **Genuine defect found and fixed -- fingerprint-tri-state-class bug did not recur here, but a real filesystem/database consistency bug did.** `_album_cleanup_apply_issue()`'s move-then-DB-rewrite sequence: when the file move succeeded but the subsequent `beets_client.rewrite_library_path()` call failed, the code raised a truthful error but left the file stranded at the new location while the Beets DB still pointed at the old path -- exactly the split-state the review brief warned a "truthful error message alone" does not repair. Fixed by moving the file back to its original location before re-raising when the DB rewrite fails.
- **Genuine defect found and fixed -- new `/library/rewrite-path` engine endpoint silently reported success on zero-row and swallowed a partially-committed change.** (a) The endpoint computed `items_changed`/`artpaths_changed` but never checked them -- an `old_path` that matched no stored row (e.g. stale/incorrect) still returned `ok: true` with an empty change count, giving the caller no signal that nothing actually happened. Fixed: zero total rows changed now rolls back and returns 404. (b) The two `albums.artpath` UPDATE statements (text and BLOB form) were wrapped in one shared try/except that reset `changed_artpaths` to 0 on *any* exception -- if the first statement succeeded and the second raised, that first statement's row change remained part of the transaction and was still committed by the unconditional `con.commit()` immediately after, while the reported count silently said zero. Fixed by isolating each statement in its own try/except (matching the original pre-PR app.py code's per-statement isolation) so a failure in one never discards or miscounts the other's real, committed effect. Also added explicit `try/except/finally` transaction handling with `con.rollback()` on failure and `con.close()` in `finally`, replacing reliance on GC-timing for cleanup.
- **Genuine defect found and fixed -- `_review_paths_equal()` authorization fallback failed open.** This function backs `_pending_review_matches()`, which authorizes destructive Pending Review `delete_rejected`/`delete_duplicate` cleanup actions. It attempted trusted resolution on both sides, but fell back to weak raw-string (backslash/trailing-slash normalized) equality whenever *either* side failed trusted resolution -- meaning a stored Pending Review entry that itself failed containment/symlink validation could still be accepted as "equal" to a request path if their raw text happened to normalize the same way. Fixed to fail closed: return `False` whenever either side cannot be trust-resolved, never falling back to string comparison for that case.
- **Regression found and fixed -- preview-cache freshness was weakened by the security fix.** The old cache-key implementation included `size`/`mtime_ns` from `Path(source_path).stat()`; the new containment-validated implementation replaced that entirely with just the canonical path string, losing invalidation when a reviewed folder's contents change (file added/replaced/removed) without the path itself changing. Restored the same stat-based freshness signal, now computed from the already-validated canonical path rather than raw request text, so the security fix and the freshness property both hold.
- **Investigated and reverted -- album-edition-safety classification.** Initially added a blocker demoting any same-RGID multi-folder merge that introduces new (non-duplicate) audio content to "Needs review" instead of auto-"Safe," reasoning that RGID proves album-family membership but not identical track layout/edition. This directly contradicted deliberate, extensively tested pre-existing product behavior in `tests/test_album_folder_cleanup.py` (e.g. `test_source_only_audio_under_same_rgid_is_safe_move`, `test_same_rgid_two_stale_sources_plus_canonical_all_safe`), which establishes that merging non-colliding same-RGID files *is* the intended, reviewed "Safe" outcome (the product's model is that a confirmed same-RGID match legitimately arises from fragmented/partial imports splitting one album across folders, and the team has accepted the tradeoff against the rarer distinct-edition-sharing-an-RGID case). Reverted the code change and the tests that asserted it, per CLAUDE.md's "do not rewrite broad areas or user-facing behavior unless the task explicitly calls for it." The theoretical risk (a genuinely different edition sharing an RGID getting auto-merged) is real but unresolved -- recorded here as a deferred, unresolved product-design question requiring a stakeholder decision (e.g. a track-count/duration-continuity heuristic), not a unilateral code change against tested intent.
- Architecture boundary: independently re-verified `POST /library/rewrite-path` is appropriately narrow (rejects outside-root, root-self, and symlink-component paths; requires the destination to already exist as a real file) but has no per-call authorization input beyond the two paths themselves (no album/item ID, no idempotency key) -- any caller that can reach `BeetsClient.rewrite_library_path()` can rewrite an arbitrary matched item's/album's stored path. Given the only caller is the already-path-and-identity-validated album-cleanup apply flow (not directly exposed to the web-manager's own HTTP API), and the control-agent endpoint itself is internal-only (port 8338, not published), this is accepted as sufficiently bounded for the current caller set; a stronger per-operation authorization contract (expected row count enforced server-side, not just reported) would be warranted before any second caller is added.
- Alert #475 (`py/clear-text-storage-sensitive-data`) independently traced via the full SARIF code-flow (18-node thread flow): the recognized "source" is `_album_cleanup_trusted_destination()`'s return value, reached through `action.get("target")` -- CodeQL's sensitive-data heuristic evidently pattern-matches on the generic key name `"target"`, not any actual credential. The traced sink writes a filesystem destination path (a duplicate-file quarantine target inside the user's own music library) into the operational cleanup report (`ok`, `dry_run`, `root`, `summary`, `operations`, `errors`, `rollback` -- all counts, paths, and status text). No `AUTH_TOKEN`, `BEETS_API_TOKEN`, provider API key, Plex token, request header, provider URL, raw exception text, or database credential value is anywhere in this flow; `log` entries in this exact code path were already edited to use `type(exc).__name__` instead of raw exception text. The report file (`METADATA_CACHE_ROOT/album-folder-cleanup-last.json`) is read back only through the app's own authenticated status endpoint, the same pattern as other operational caches. Dismissed as `false positive` with this exact trace as justification.
- Test quality: added/kept `ReviewPathsEqualFailClosedTests` (4 tests) and `AlbumCleanupMoveDbFailureRecoveryTests` (1 test) to the new wave-4 test file, plus one new `ControlAgentLibraryRewriteTests` case for the zero-row-rewrite rejection (skipped on Windows for the same pre-existing POSIX-only-path-validator reason as the existing success-path test; real verification via Docker). 3 of these new tests independently verified to fail against the pre-review head via `git stash`/`git stash pop`. No regressions: full `tests/test_album_folder_cleanup.py` (239 tests) and the rest of the full suite pass unchanged.
- Remaining live count: not claimed here -- see the final report for the exact live count on the final reviewed/merged head.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged).
- Status: Open (SEC-002 overall). See the final report for exact reviewed head, alert disposition, and merge decision.
### Path-Injection Wave 5: album artwork save/upload -- Codex implementation, pending Claude final review

- Starting main SHA: `a92b3d4d6594fa6dc80bf2cf8e26df8f074f0ea8` (PR #60 squash). Live paginated `main` inventory on this SHA: 199 total open alerts, 182 `app.py` alerts, 158 `app.py` `py/path-injection`, all high severity. Latest Python CodeQL analysis was on this exact SHA. Creation-date distribution: 185 alerts from 2026-07-20, 8 from 2026-07-30, and 6 from 2026-08-05.
- Fixed-but-open prior-wave accounting, not subtracted from the live totals: Wave 1's 32 (`#256`-`#272`, `#274`-`#277`, `#280`-`#285`, `#417`-`#421`); Wave 2's 17 (`#156`-`#163`, `#166`-`#170`, `#174`-`#176`, `#178`); Wave 3's 3 reviewed false positives (`#292`-`#294`); and Wave 4's 11 (`#194`, `#196`, `#197`, `#205`, `#207`-`#212`, `#216`). These remain live GitHub alerts until CodeQL or Claude disposes them individually.
- Selected Wave 5 alerts: `#182` (`app.py:12788`, request-controlled `aldir` to `dest.write_bytes(data)`), `#183` (`app.py:12790`, same tainted destination to `os.chmod(dest, 0o644)`), and `#184` (`app.py:12819`, request-controlled `aldir` to `(Path(aldir) / aname).exists()`). All three remain open, high-severity `py/path-injection` alerts in `app.py`, and the SARIF source-to-sink trace is the same album-art save/upload flow.
- Functions and routes: legacy `POST /api/save-album-art`, `POST /api/albums/<aid>/art/url`, `POST /api/albums/<aid>/art/upload`, `DELETE /api/albums/<aid>/art`, `_repair_album_art()` Discogs fallback, `_album_art_candidates()`, `_usable_album_art_file()`, and new control-agent `POST/DELETE /albums/<id>/art` operations.
- Root causes fixed: clients could choose or influence album-art destinations through `aldir`, upload filename/extension/MIME header, or stored `albums.artpath`/item paths before the filesystem sink. The app now binds artwork mutation to a Beets album ID and expected `mb_releasegroupid`; it no longer uses a client-selected album folder as the destination. Legacy `aldir` is accepted only as optional disambiguation when no album ID is provided.
- Album identity policy: artwork mutation is authorized by current Beets album ID, with the route passing the current MusicBrainz Release Group ID as an expected identity. The engine re-reads the album row under the mutation lock and rejects if the release-group identity changed before replacement. Missing RGID does not become an alternate path authority; the engine still resolves the current album folder from current item rows.
- Upload validation and destination policy: app and engine both reject empty/oversized/animated/unsupported or undecodable images using Pillow, with JPEG/PNG/WebP accepted at the API boundary and normalized to fixed `albumart.jpg` in the engine. The client filename, extension, and `Content-Type` never select the destination. URL flows keep using `validate_outbound_url()` before download; redirects remain subject to the installed secure urllib policy.
- Architecture decision: Beets/media album artwork and `albums.artpath` are Beets-owned state, so new authoritative replacement/deletion moved behind `backend.beets_control_agent` and `backend.beets_client`. No media mount, local Beets package, or broad `BEET_BIN` migration was added. Existing embed/fetch command-array follow-up remains part of the repository-wide `BEET_BIN` architecture debt; this wave only documents it and does not migrate unrelated call sites.
- Engine operations added: `BeetsClient.replace_album_art()`, `BeetsClient.delete_album_art()`, and control-agent `POST /albums/<id>/art` / `DELETE /albums/<id>/art`. The engine operation uses fixed roots, stored-path validation that preserves POSIX literal-percent names, NUL/backslash/Windows/UNC rejection, canonical album-folder resolution, root-self refusal, symlink-component and existing/dangling symlink-leaf rejection, mutation lock, identity recheck, fixed filename, atomic temp write + replace, rollback on post-replace failure, and quarantine-on-delete constrained under `BEETSDIR`, explicit cross-device copy/fsync/unlink fallback, and DB clear rollback.
- Equivalent unreported fixes: multipart upload no longer writes `dest.write_bytes()` in `app.py`; URL replacement and `_repair_album_art()` Discogs fallback no longer set `artpath` separately from the file operation; `DELETE /api/albums/<aid>/art` no longer unlinks app-side candidates and no longer returns raw `BeetsError` text to public responses (PR-head alert `#485`); artwork status/read helpers now reject symlink leaves/components; the engine stored-path helper no longer converts POSIX DB paths through host `Path` text before validation and now rejects stored NUL/backslash/Windows/UNC paths.
- Tests added/updated: new `tests/test_sec002_app_path_album_art.py` covers real temporary music roots, real SQLite albums/items, real image bytes, symlink album folders and symlink artwork leaves, stale RGID rejection, fixed destination filename, dangling destination-symlink rejection, pre-backup and post-replace rollback, quarantine-root containment, cross-device quarantine, Flask legacy-save/upload/delete route behavior, upload filename traversal, and safe public error text. Updated `tests/test_pr39_codeql_alerts.py` so the existing album-art delete exception-sanitization regression covers `delete_album_art()` instead of the removed direct `clear_album_artpath()` path.
- Remaining live-count estimate before PR-head CodeQL: live `main` remains 199 total open and 158 `app.py` `py/path-injection` alerts. This wave is expected to address the 3 selected original artwork alerts plus the equivalent upload/delete/fallback flows above; exact remaining count is authoritative only after PR-head CodeQL and/or a post-merge `main` rescan. No alert was dismissed by Codex.
- Recommended Wave 6 cluster: the deferred AI/library duplicate scanning region (`#185`-`#191`) if Claude wants to stay adjacent to this artwork area, or qBittorrent hardlink repair (`#253`-`#254`) if prioritizing a smaller request-controlled filesystem mutation cluster. The repository-wide `BEET_BIN` migration remains separate architecture debt and should not be mixed into a path-injection wave.
- Priority: P1 (authenticated path-injection into media-owned artwork write/delete and Beets DB `artpath` mutation).
- Status: Open (SEC-002 overall). Codex implementation is in progress; Claude has not performed final independent review, alert disposition, or merge decision.

#### Claude final independent review (PR #61)

- Reviewed exact PR base `8ad8a85968d40e3b7f5cca1c4749ea497b32d6ad` / head `91b3034d235fcdf3212511acc5c11b1c212895e6` (5 commits, 7 files). `origin/main` had independently advanced to `27b4632eac6a0f79edfeffa42e613dd55f023164` (two unrelated deployment/CI/Beets-engine-version commits, `4d465a7` and `27b4632`) during this review; confirmed via `git diff --stat` that neither touches `app.py`, `backend/beets_client.py`, `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, `requirements.txt`, or `frontend/package.json` -- zero overlap with this PR's files or dependencies.
- Original alerts `#182`-`#184` reconstructed against `origin/main`'s pre-wave-5 `app.py`: all three trace to the same legacy `save_album_art()`/download-and-cache helper, sink lines 12788 (`dest.write_bytes(data)`), 12790 (`os.chmod(dest, 0o644)`), 12819 (`(Path(aldir) / aname).exists()`), all fed by request-body `aldir` guarded only by a **lexical** `Path(aldir).relative_to(music_root)` check (no `realpath`/symlink resolution, so a `..`-containing `aldir` such as `/data/media/music/../../etc` passes the check while resolving outside the root). Confirmed this entire vulnerable function no longer exists anywhere in the PR-head `app.py` -- the class of defect is removed, not patched in place. Disposition: **fixed by code** for all three, pending merge + `main` rescan to auto-close.
- Genuine findings independently identified and fixed (none flagged by CodeQL; found through direct code reading and cross-file tracing, matching the task's "equivalent unreported artwork vulnerabilities" charge):
  1. **Unclamped environment-configurable image-safety limits.** `ALBUM_ART_MAX_BYTES`/`ALBUM_ART_MAX_PIXELS`/`ALBUM_ART_MAX_DIMENSION` read directly from `os.environ` with no minimum/maximum clamp -- a misconfigured or compromised `.env` could set `BEETS_ALBUM_ART_MAX_PIXELS` to an unbounded value and re-enable a decompression-bomb-class resource-exhaustion vector regardless of image byte size. Fixed with a new `_env_int_clamped()` helper (mirroring `app.py`'s pre-existing, already-proven `_env_int(minimum=, maximum=)` pattern) with hard ceilings (100MB / 200MP / 20000px) the environment cannot override.
  2. **Two remaining raw-`BeetsError`-text exposure sinks.** `album_upload_art()`/`album_replace_art_from_url()` both still had `except BeetsError as ex: raise RuntimeError(str(ex)) from ex`, propagated verbatim into `job_engine.py`'s `PythonJob._run()` (`self.log.append(f"ERROR: {exc}")`) and exposed through the job-status API's `log` field -- the same class alert `#485` fixed for the delete route but did not cover here. Traced `beets_client.py`'s `_request()`: a `BeetsUnavailableError` message always includes the raw `exc.reason` and internal `self.base_url`, so this was a genuine, not theoretical, leak surface. In the current call graph both branches are shadowed by `_replace_album_art_bytes()`'s own broader `except Exception` (which already converts to a safe message before the outer handler ever sees it), making them currently-unreachable dead code -- but CodeQL's taint analysis does not require reachability to flag `str(ex)` on an exception, and a future simplification of the inner helper could silently reactivate the path. Fixed both to match the delete route's already-correct pattern (fixed generic message + `app.logger.warning(type(ex).__name__)`), as defense-in-depth, not merely dead-code removal.
  3. **`_album_art_album_dir()` picked the first resolvable item directory without checking the rest agreed.** Iterated up to 25 item rows and `return`ed on the first one that safely resolved, never comparing it against the others. A stale/corrupted database row (items genuinely split across two unrelated directories, not a legitimate multidisc `Disc N` layout) would silently scope the artwork mutation to whichever item happened to be first, rather than refusing. Fixed: the function now requires every resolvable sampled item to agree on the same directory (multidisc unification via the existing `Disc N` climb-to-parent logic is unaffected and still passes) and raises 409 `"Album items are split across conflicting directories"` on disagreement.
  4. **`_fsync_file()`/`_fsync_directory()` silently swallowed all exceptions.** Both existed as bare `except Exception: pass`, so a real fsync failure after a successful rename was invisible -- the operation still reported unqualified success. The primary content write's own fsync (before the rename) was already unguarded/non-swallowed, so file *content* durability was never actually at risk; the swallowed calls only covered the rename/directory-entry durability step. Fixed: both now return `bool` and log a warning on failure; `_replace_album_art_locked()`'s result now includes a `"durable"` field reflecting whether both post-rename fsyncs actually succeeded, rather than presenting an unqualified durability guarantee the code could not back up. Deliberately did not add rollback-after-commit for a fsync-only failure -- by that point the DB commit and file rename have already succeeded correctly; rolling back a fundamentally correct write over a narrow, low-probability durability-metadata gap would be more disruptive than informative.
  5. **Quarantine target claim was check-then-act, not atomic.** `if target.exists() or target.is_symlink(): ... ; if target.exists() or target.is_symlink(): raise` left a real race window before the later `os.replace()`/`shutil.copy2()` in `_replace_or_copy_unlink()`, both of which silently overwrite an existing destination -- exactly what the review brief warned a random UUID alone does not prevent. Fixed with a new `_claim_trash_target_exclusive()` using `os.open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL)`, a real atomic create-if-absent syscall, closing the window entirely rather than narrowing it.
- Reviewed and accepted as already correct, no fix needed: `_resolve_album_art_request_album()`'s artist+title fallback requires exact case-folded equality and `len(matches) != 1` rejection (two same-artist/same-title albums correctly block rather than picking one); client-supplied `aldir` is used only as a filter predicate against the server-computed `_album_dir_for_art()` value, never as a destination itself; `validate_outbound_url()`/`OutboundPolicyError` (the centralized SSRF policy) is reused unmodified for the URL-replace route, no weaker artwork-specific reimplementation was introduced; EXIF-transpose happens after dimension/pixel-count validation but a transpose only swaps width/height, both of which were already checked against the same symmetric limit, so no post-transpose bypass exists.
- Documented, not fixed (pre-existing, out of narrow scope, or fails safe): `_album_dir_for_art()` (pre-existing, not added by this PR) depends on `MUSIC_ROOT` being mounted in the web-manager container for its `.exists()`-based checks; the canonical production `docker-compose.yml` does not mount it. This only affects the *optional* `aldir` disambiguation narrowing in the legacy save route, and fails **closed** (every candidate gets excluded, so the route correctly refuses rather than guessing) -- not a security defect, but the `aldir` disambiguation feature is non-functional in the canonical production deployment. `RGID`-empty albums get no identity re-verification at mutation time (`if expected and expected != actual` short-circuits on empty `expected`) -- the mutation is still bound to the numeric album ID re-read fresh under lock (not name/folder matching), so this is a narrower "can't detect a content swap under the same ID between resolution and lock" residual risk, not an "authorize an unrelated album" defect; would need a secondary heuristic (track count/duration) to close fully, out of narrow scope here. The local `beet embedart` follow-up call in `album_upload_art()`/`album_replace_art_from_url()` cannot succeed in the standard deployment (`BEET_BIN` resolves to a nonexistent path in the web-manager container, which intentionally has zero local Beets install) -- already treated as best-effort/non-blocking by the existing code, left in place with an explanatory comment; embedding into file tags (as opposed to the folder-level `albumart.jpg` the engine already writes) is tracked here as future engine-side work, not part of this route's success contract, and the repository-wide `BEET_BIN` migration remains explicitly out of scope per this task's authorization.
- API compatibility: no client-visible contract change from this review's fixes. The new `"durable"` result field is additive. Response shapes, status codes, and accepted image formats are unchanged from Codex's implementation.
- Tests added: `EnvClampedArtworkLimitTests` (7 tests, direct unit coverage of `_env_int_clamped` plus a module-constant sanity check), `test_replace_album_art_blocks_when_items_point_to_conflicting_directories` / `test_replace_album_art_unifies_legitimate_multidisc_item_directories` (positive and negative coverage for finding 3), `test_replace_album_art_reports_durability_true_on_clean_write` / `..._false_without_failing_when_fsync_fails` (finding 4; the "true" assertion is POSIX-only-gated since `_fsync_directory`'s `os.O_DIRECTORY` has no Windows equivalent and this agent only ever runs in Linux containers), `test_claim_trash_target_exclusive_rejects_pre_existing_file` / `..._creates_new_file` (direct coverage of finding 5's atomic primitive) plus an end-to-end quarantine-collision contract test, `test_upload_album_art_does_not_leak_beets_error_text` / `test_replace_art_from_url_does_not_leak_beets_error_text` (finding 2, mocking the inner helper directly so the outer handler's own sanitization is what's actually exercised, not the inner helper's). All new tests independently verified via `git stash` to fail against the pre-fix code for the intended reason (`AttributeError` for the three new-helper-based fixes, wrong status code / leaked literal text for the other two).
- Validation: full suite (`python -m unittest discover -s tests -p "test_*.py"`) run 3 consecutive times, 1691 tests, OK each run, no skips beyond the pre-existing 6; targeted artwork/security modules run twice more (255 tests each) for additional determinism confidence. `py_compile`, `scripts/security_secret_scan.py`, `scripts/validate_compose_security.py` all clean. `git diff --check` clean (no whitespace issues).
- PR-scoped CodeQL on the pushed fixes flagged 2 new alerts (`#486`, `#487`, both `py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data`, `backend/beets_control_agent.py` lines 837/850 -- the two new `print()` warnings added by finding 4's durability fix). Traced the full SARIF thread-flow for both: the reported taint source is the variable literally named `trusted` (lines 574/577, and the `resolve_safe_path()` return value at line 627 assigned to a variable of the same name) -- this codebase's established, load-bearing name for "a path-traversal-validated, safe filesystem `Path`" throughout the whole SEC-002 series, not a credential or secret of any kind. The sink logs only `path.name` -- a bare artwork filename such as `albumart.jpg`. No `BEETS_API_TOKEN`, password, session token, or other genuine credential is anywhere in this call chain. This is the identical naming-heuristic false-positive class already documented and dismissed for alert `#475` (there: the generic key name `"target"`). Both dismissed individually as `false positive` with this trace as justification, not renamed/refactored to dodge the heuristic (renaming `trusted` throughout the path-validation architecture would be a large, out-of-scope refactor of a name used consistently across every prior wave).
- Remaining live count and exact CI/CodeQL disposition: recorded in the PR's final report, not duplicated here.
- Priority: P1 (unchanged).
- Status: Open (SEC-002 overall). See the final report for exact reviewed head, alert disposition, and merge decision.

### SEC-002 Wave 6: qBittorrent Hardlink Repair Path Security -- Codex implementation, pending Claude final review

- Starting main SHA: `3994d6818531e6b4855bb3717a7ef2b94fd326a4` (PR #61 merged).
- Overlap verification: PR #65 (`fix/harden-truenas-rollout`) verified open; modifies auth token persistence and deployment scripts with ZERO overlap with qBittorrent hardlink repair helpers.
- Live inventory: 198 total open alerts, 180 `app.py` open alerts, 155 `py/path-injection` open alerts in `app.py`.
- Selected Wave 6 alerts: `#253` and `#254` (`py/path-injection` at `app.py:33176` for qBittorrent hardlink repair path injection).
- Architecture boundary & refactoring: Moved direct local filesystem hardlink creation (`os.link`) out of `app.py` and behind the Beets control agent boundary (`POST /files/hardlink` in `backend/beets_control_agent.py` and `BeetsClient.create_hardlink` in `backend/beets_client.py`).
- Path safety & containment: `_path_under` updated to use `os.path.realpath` with root-parent containment check instead of `resolve(strict=False).relative_to()`, eliminating CodeQL's untrusted `resolve` path-injection source. `_qbit_map_path` updated to enforce component-boundary matching.
- Security contract: Fixed root containment (`music`, `staging`, `tmp`), source file regular/non-symlink check, same-inode idempotency check (`already_present`), cross-device link rejection (`EXDEV`), pre- and post-link identity verification under lock, and error message sanitization.
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_app_path_qbittorrent_hardlink.py` covers path containment, alias prefix mapping, control agent endpoint validation, idempotency, size checks, and error message sanitization.
- Status: In Progress / Ready for Draft PR and Handoff to Claude.

#### Claude final independent review (PR #66, Wave 6)

- **SHA corrections**: the handoff report's pushed-commit SHA (`9d110b411d3bfcaae9b61d36d40081d59664db80`) and its `VCS_REF` Docker claim (`3994d6818531e6b4855bb3717a7ef2b94fd326a4`, the base commit) were both wrong. Live PR head verified via `gh pr view 66`: `9d110b43573cf9a399211a95cc122f6d1239d8af`. Reported Docker/OCI validation against the base SHA did not validate this branch's code; rebuilt and reverified against the actual final head below.
- **CI infra flake**: the initial `statusCheckRollup` showed 6 duplicate check failures (CodeQL x3, frontend-lint, frontend-typecheck x2, security, python-tests) alongside successful runs for the same head SHA. Root-caused via `gh run view --log-failed`: all failed for the identical reason, `Failed to resolve action download info. Error: Service Unavailable`, a transient GitHub Actions outage unrelated to code; the parallel `push`-triggered runs on the same commit succeeded. Re-ran the affected jobs; not a code defect.
- **Alert #253/#254 mischaracterization**: both alerts trace to `_path_under()` (`app.py:33176` pre-fix), a shared helper with 17 call sites spanning album-folder cleanup, rename dedup, artwork replacement, and template-token cleanup -- **none of which are qBittorrent-specific**. The GitHub alert API does not expose full codeFlows via REST, but no `_qbit_*` function calls `_path_under()` at all. Labeling this wave "qBittorrent hardlink repair" is an approximate wave assignment, not a precise trace; fixing `_path_under()` is still the correct and sufficient fix for these two alerts.
- **Real authorization gate was untouched**: `_qbit_allowed_repair_path()` -- the actual function gating qBittorrent-derived destination paths -- called `_path_is_under()`, a separate, older, ~60-call-site helper with the exact CodeQL-flagged pattern (`resolve(strict=False).relative_to()`), left unfixed by the original patch. Changed `_qbit_allowed_repair_path()` to call the hardened `_path_under()` instead, closing the gap for this feature without touching the other 60 unrelated call sites of `_path_is_under()`.
- **Endpoint authority narrowed**: `/files/hardlink` validated both `source_path` and `target_path` against the same `["music", "staging", "tmp"]` root set in either direction -- a generic cross-root hardlink primitive, not a scoped repair operation. The only real caller (`_qbit_hardlink_missing_impl`) always uses a database-verified library file as source and a torrent/staging-side path as destination. Endpoint now hardcodes `source_roots=["music"]`, `dest_roots=["staging", "tmp"]`; `music` is no longer a valid destination and `staging`/`tmp` are no longer valid sources.
- **Source identity gap**: `_library_file_candidates_for_qbit()`/`_metadata()` selected a hardlink source from the Beets `items.path` DB column without verifying it actually resolved under `MUSIC_ROOT`. Added an explicit `_path_under(path, MUSIC_ROOT)` check before a DB row becomes source-eligible.
- **Symlink checks were dead code -- found via real Linux execution, not static reading, then corrected twice**: `resolve_safe_path()` fully dereferences symlinks via `os.path.realpath()` before returning, so the existing `safe_src.is_symlink()` / `safe_dst.is_symlink()` checks operated on the already-resolved target and could never observe a symlink whose target existed. Running the new endpoint-level tests in a `python:3.12-slim-bookworm` container (Windows dev host cannot exercise `resolve_safe_path`'s POSIX-absolute-only inputs against real files) surfaced this directly: a symlink source with an in-root target was silently linked instead of rejected. First fix: checked `os.path.islink()` on the raw, pre-resolution `source_path`/`target_path` strings. That introduced 24 new PR-scoped `py/path-injection` alerts (a fresh filesystem call fed directly by unresolved request data, flagged even though `resolve_safe_path` had already validated the same value moments earlier -- CodeQL's dataflow does not credit that as a barrier for a second, independent call on the original variable). On reflection this check was also not actually load-bearing: containment is enforced on the fully resolved path either way, so a symlink source/destination grants no capability beyond naming its resolved target directly would, and the TOCTOU window is already closed by the lock + pre-link stat + post-link inode-identity verification regardless of symlink involvement. Removed the raw-string check rather than reimplementing it in a disguised form; a symlink source with an in-root target is now a legitimate, intentionally-accepted request (see `test_symlink_source_with_in_root_target_links_the_resolved_file`), while one escaping every allowed root is still rejected by the pre-existing containment check (`test_symlink_source_escaping_every_root_is_rejected`). A pre-existing symlink destination remains safe by construction: it either matches the source (`already_present`) or differs (`409`, no overwrite) via the existing dev/ino comparison, with `os.link()` never reached in either case.
- **`expected_size` validation**: previously silently skipped the size check for any non-`int` value (string, float) and accepted `bool` (a subclass of `int`) as if it were a byte count. Now rejects missing-but-wrong-type, `bool`, zero, and negative values with 400 before any path resolution.
- **Insecure `os.link` fallback removed**: the `TypeError`-triggered fallback to a symlink-following `os.link()` call has no reachable trigger on the Linux production target (CPython raises `NotImplementedError`, not `TypeError`, when `follow_symlinks=False` is unsupported) and was dead/misleading code; replaced with a `NotImplementedError` catch that fails closed (500) instead of silently downgrading the no-follow guarantee.
- **Destination-parent TOCTOU**: added a re-validation (symlink check + `resolve_safe_path` re-containment) of `safe_dst.parent` immediately after `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`, defending the window where a concurrent actor could swap the parent for a symlink between the locked validation and directory creation.
- **Idempotency reporting bug**: `summary["linked"]` was incremented for both newly-created and already-existing links. Fixed to increment `summary["already_present"]` (an existing, previously-unused-here counter) when `already_present` is true, so an idempotent no-op is never reported as a new link.
- **Response information trimming**: the engine's `/files/hardlink` responses included absolute container paths and raw `st_dev`/`st_ino` values that no caller consumes (`_qbit_hardlink_missing_impl` only reads `ok`/`already_present`/`error`, using its own locally-known paths for display). Trimmed to `{"ok": true, "linked"|"already_present": true}`.
- **Error-message allowlist**: `action["error"]` in the web-manager job log forwarded the control agent's `error` string verbatim. All current engine error strings are fixed, non-secret text, but the boundary should not assume every future one is; added `_qbit_hardlink_public_error()`, an explicit allowlist of the known-safe strings, with a generic fallback for anything else (including a malformed/non-dict response).
- **Tests**: added `ControlAgentHardlinkEndpointTests`, invoking `ControlAgentHandler.do_POST()` directly (the established real-endpoint pattern from `ControlAgentLibraryRewriteTests`) rather than mocking `BeetsClient._request()`/`create_hardlink()`. Covers: valid link creation, idempotency, different-file collision, outside-root source, wrong-role source/destination, invalid `expected_size` (string/float/bool/zero/negative), symlink source, symlink destination, symlinked destination parent, and no-overwrite-on-race. Success/collision/symlink tests that require real POSIX-absolute paths against real files skip on Windows (`resolve_safe_path` requires a literal leading `/`) and are exercised for real in Linux CI/Docker; the initial fixture design accidentally nested all three root categories under the OS temp directory (itself a valid `tmp` root), masking the role-separation and symlink-escape checks it was meant to prove -- corrected to place fixture roots outside the system temp directory.
- **Torrent/file identity** (deeper hardening -- filename+size+optional album/track metadata matching, no checksum/AcoustID cross-check) and **plan provenance/undo audit trail** (a bound, server-generated repair-plan record with rollback) are both genuine product/architecture gaps raised during this review but are out of scope for a narrow CodeQL-remediation wave; they require a product decision on acceptable evidence strength and a persistent audit ledger this codebase does not yet have. Recorded here as follow-up technical debt, not blocking this wave.
- **CI note**: this review's final validation pass on this PR ran during a documented GitHub-wide GitHub Actions major outage (per githubstatus.com, began 2026-08-06 15:22 UTC, resolved 2026-08-07). All failures observed were confirmed via job logs (`Failed to resolve action download info: Service Unavailable`) or GitHub-side queueing delays, not code defects; every check was independently re-verified either by CI reruns once dispatched, or directly via local/Docker-based execution (full test suite, focused suite, and real HTTP round-trips against the built engine image) documented below. The outage prevented the ordinary `lint`/`typecheck`/`security`/`unit-tests`/`node-build`/`docker-build` workflows from ever dispatching for the exact final head (`df86ca0fde490a19bedb88ce764e6e93a6cd56d6`) even after it resolved; this documentation correction commit is expected to retrigger them now that GitHub Actions reports "All Systems Operational."
- **Final independent re-verification on the exact final head** (`df86ca0fde490a19bedb88ce764e6e93a6cd56d6`): PR-scoped CodeQL alerts are **0 open** -- the 20 sanitizer-model false positives (`#493`, `#495`-`#516`, `#494` never existed as a distinct alert) were individually dismissed with the justification above (one, `#493`, dismissed directly; the remaining 19 dismissed manually by the repository owner after Claude's per-alert classification, per the auto-mode safety classifier blocking a rapid dismissal sequence as a bulk-action pattern -- each still carries its own individual, accurate justification, not a blanket one). ## SEC-002 Wave 8: AI Batch Import / Reimport / Unmatched-Draft Path Security -- Codex implementation, pending Claude final review

- Starting main SHA: `a7ec96c12ad6f68b9569c9c7af2f5f733429065d` (PR #67 merged).
- Live inventory at start: 188 total open alerts, 143 `py/path-injection` open alerts in `app.py`.
- Historical candidate cluster (`#219`-`#252`, `#414`-`#416`, per earlier SEC-002 analysis) reconstructed against the live tree: 31 of the historical numbers remain open, mapping to `reimport_disk`, `library_import_all`, `_mark_pending_review_status`, `_ai_batch_write_state`/`_ai_batch_load_state`, `start_ai_batch_import`, `create_unmatched_draft`, `start_import`/`import_preflight`, `_scan_scope_label`, `dedup_scan`/`dedup_cleanup`, `_resolved_path`/`_folder_clean_root`, `_mb_release_tracklist_read_disk`/`write_disk`. `#417`-`#421` (`_folder_cleanup_path`/`apply_folder_placeholder_action_api`) and `#480`-`#484` (`_target_preview_source_files`/`_remaining_audio_files`/`_remove_pending_review_for_path`) are adjacent but were **not** part of the original historical mapping and were not pulled into this wave's scope without independent justification -- left for a future wave's reconstruction.
- **Two genuine vulnerabilities found and fixed**, not CodeQL sanitizer-recognition false positives:
  1. **`start_ai_batch_import()` / `ai_batch_import_recover()` (`#226` area / `#229`/`#230`/`#414` area on the pre-fix tree)**: both routes read a client- or job-state-supplied `scan_path`/`source_path` with only an `os.path.exists()` check, then passed it directly into `_ai_batch_find_audio_dirs()` -- an **unbounded recursive `os.walk()`** with zero root-containment check anywhere in the call chain. Any authenticated caller (or corrupted/tampered job state on recovery) could point AI Batch Import at an arbitrary container path (e.g. `/`, `/web-manager-data`) and every discovered audio-bearing directory would be queued for AI-driven import review. Fixed by validating in the single shared entry point both routes call into (`_start_ai_batch_job`), via the already-hardened `_resolve_import_review_source_path()` (SEC-002 Wave 7), so stored job state is revalidated at execution time rather than trusted because it was "already validated when queued." Also validated at the `start_ai_batch_import()` route level as defense-in-depth.
  2. **`create_unmatched_draft()` (`POST /api/library/unmatched-draft`, `#229`/`#230`/`#414`)**: built `MUSIC_ROOT / artist / album_folder` from entirely unsanitized client-supplied `artist`/`album`/`year` text, then called `mkdir(parents=True)` + `write_text()` on the result -- an embedded `/` or `..` in any of those three fields escapes `MUSIC_ROOT` entirely (`Path.__truediv__` re-parses embedded separators as additional path components; concatenating first and sanitizing the combined string afterward would not have caught this). This was a genuine arbitrary-directory-creation-and-file-write primitive, more severe than the read-only AI-batch-scan issue, and had **zero prior test coverage**. Fixed by sanitizing each field through the existing `_safe_path_component()` helper (already used elsewhere in `app.py` for exactly this purpose -- reused, not reinvented) before folder-name construction, plus a final `_path_is_under()` containment re-verification immediately before the destructive `mkdir`/`write_text` calls as defense-in-depth.
- **Proven false positive (existing precedent, not re-derived)**: `_ai_batch_write_state`/`_ai_batch_load_state` (`#223`/`#226` on the current tree) are the renumbered continuation of `#327`/`#328`, already proven false positive during the main-branch CodeQL backlog triage above: `_ai_batch_state_file()` reduces `batch_job_id` to `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` before ever joining it onto `_AI_BATCH_STATE_DIR`, so no input can introduce a path separator; existing test `tests/test_sec002_codeql_backlog.py::AiBatchStateFileContainmentTests` already covers this. Recommend Claude re-confirm and dismiss under the current alert numbers.
- **Defense-in-depth, not independently exploitable**: `library_import_all()`'s alert (`#220`, a `Path(...).resolve()` used only for a deduplication key) routes every album's `aldir` through `_ai_suggest_folder_internal()` for the actual discovery/import action, which is already hardened (SEC-002 Wave 7). The flagged line itself performs no filesystem mutation and no unvalidated read beyond the dedup key computation.
- **Not yet independently traced, deferred**: `_mark_pending_review_status`, `start_import`/`import_preflight`, `_scan_scope_label`, `dedup_scan`/`dedup_cleanup`/`_resolved_path`/`_folder_clean_root`, `_mb_release_tracklist_read_disk`/`write_disk` (roughly 20 of the 31 live historical alerts). A first read of `dedup_cleanup`'s `_clean_empty_parents` helper (bounded-depth, empty-directories-only `rmdir` walk toward a computed boundary) did not surface an obvious issue, but was not traced with the same rigor as the two fixed vulnerabilities above and is not being classified as either genuine or false-positive without that proof. Recommended as the starting scope for SEC-002 Wave 9, alongside the `#417`-`#421` and `#480`-`#484` adjacent clusters noted above.
- **AI authority**: confirmed unchanged by this wave. `_ai_suggest_folder_internal()` (already advisory-only per Wave 7) is the only AI-facing function touched, and only for path validation. The pre-existing, unrelated high-confidence batch-auto-apply pathway inside `library_import_all()`'s `_do()` closure (AI suggestion accepted directly into `mb_albumid` when confidence is `high`/`medium` and `mb_valid` is true, without a discrete human-reviewed step for that specific match) predates this wave, is unmodified by this diff, and remains a genuine, separate architecture question for Claude/product to decide -- not silently folded into this narrow path-injection remediation.
- **Root/staging ownership**: `reimport_disk()`'s ad-hoc `_allowed_roots = ["/data/media/music", "/data/torrents/music"]` check (which additionally ran *after* an existence probe, functioning as a path-existence oracle for arbitrary container paths) was replaced with `_resolve_import_review_source_path(aldir, allow_music=True, expected_type="dir")`, the same shared, already-audited resolver used throughout the import-review/AI-suggest flow. This is a deliberate widening from the original 2-item hardcoded list to the full `_import_review_cleanup_roots()` set (`DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT`, `_DOWNLOADS_ROOTS`, `TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS`, plus `MUSIC_ROOT`) -- judged architecturally correct, not an accidental cross-feature leak: all of those roots represent the same "server-controlled acquisition/staging area" trust boundary already established for every sibling import-review function, and none are attacker-controlled or independently-configurable-by-an-unrelated-feature in the way `QBIT_REPAIR_ALLOWED_ROOTS` was found to be in Wave 7. Existence-oracle behavior closed as a side effect (containment is now checked before any filesystem probe).
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_app_path_ai_batch_replacement_staging.py` (17 tests) covers outside-root/traversal/symlink/NUL rejection for both fixed vulnerabilities, a stale-job-state-revalidation regression test for the recover-route bypass, and Unicode/literal-name/embedded-separator correctness for `create_unmatched_draft`. `tests/test_ai_batch_retry_race.py`'s shared `BehavioralTestCase` fixture updated to patch `_DOWNLOADS_ROOTS` with its own scratch directory (needed once scan-path validation became real; matches the established pattern from SEC-002 Waves 6/7 of patching trusted roots in test setup rather than relying on production accommodating a test's arbitrary path). Both new fixtures were placed outside the OS temp directory after the identical Wave 6/7 "the OS temp dir is itself already a trusted root, silently masking the containment check" bug reproduced during Linux verification.
- Unrelated frontend finding fixed as part of mandatory frontend validation (not part of the CodeQL cluster, but blocking the required `npm audit --audit-level=high` zero-vulnerabilities gate): `nanoid@3.3.16` (transitive via `postcss`) had a known high-severity advisory (GHSA-2v37-7h3g-55p8, indefinite loop on a zero `size` argument). Pinned via a new `nanoid` entry in `frontend/package.json`'s existing `overrides` block and regenerated `frontend/package-lock.json`. The lockfile was regenerated inside a Linux container (`node:22-bookworm-slim`), not on the Windows host -- a first Windows-side regeneration silently dropped Linux-only optional dependency entries (surfaced as `npm ci` failing inside the Docker build stage with "Missing: yaml@2.9.0 from lock file"); the corrected lockfile installs cleanly via `npm ci` on both platforms.
- Disposable runtime validation (real HTTP server, throwaway bind mounts, no production paths): built `beets-web-manager-codeql-wave8:latest` and `beets-engine-codeql-wave8:latest` on the exact final head, ran the web-manager image standalone with disposable temp directories bind-mounted at the container's `/data/media/music` and `/data/torrents/music` (these are not mounted into `beets-web-manager` in either shipped compose topology -- confirmed pre-existing architecture debt, not something this wave changes or relies on for production). Exercised over real HTTP: `POST /api/library/unmatched-draft` normal creation (succeeds, correct folder under music root), traversal artist (`../../../../etc`) neutralized to a literal folder name and contained under music root; `POST /api/ai-batch-import` outside-root scan_path (`/etc`) rejected pre-walk, symlink scan_path rejected, valid trusted-subdirectory scan_path passes the validation gate (fails further downstream only on an unrelated missing `/config` mount in this minimal harness); `POST /api/albums/reimport-disk` traversal `aldir` rejected, existing outside-root `aldir` (`/etc`) rejected with the same generic containment error as the non-existent case (existence-oracle regression check), valid in-root `aldir` passes the validation gate (same unrelated downstream `/config` limitation). All containers, images, and temp directories torn down after.
- Status: In Progress / Ready for Draft PR and Handoff to Claude.

### Claude independent final review of Wave 8 (PR #70) -- BLOCKED, not merged

Reviewed exact head `71abd47df10321ac4091b862805da831d6b2e88a` on base `main@18bf329ec9cdb728ea07e2f744e1eff41dfd27d3`, independently of Codex's own classifications.

- **PR-scoped CodeQL alerts, independently traced via the raw SARIF codeFlows (`gh api -H "Accept: application/sarif+json" .../code-scanning/analyses/{id}`), not just alert-summary text**:
  - `#529` (`py/path-injection`, `app.py:26564`, `draft_path.resolve()`): all 4 codeFlow paths trace `request` -> `payload["artist"/"album"]` -> (for two of the four) directly through `_safe_path_component()` (lines 34087-34093) -> `safe_artist`/`safe_album` -> `draft_path`. Confirmed by full trace, not precedent alone: every component reaching `draft_path` passes through the sanitizer, the flagged `.resolve()` call is read-only (no mutation), and the actual destructive `mkdir`/`write_text` calls are gated by a `_path_is_under()` re-check immediately after. **Disposition: false positive (sanitizer-recognition gap). Dismissed.**
  - `#530` (`backend/transaction_engine.py:200`) and `#531` (`app.py:24388`): both `py/clear-text-storage-sensitive-data`. The SARIF `relatedLocations` for every one of the 8 combined codeFlow paths (across both alerts) point to the exact same origin: `self.trusted` in `tests/test_sec002_app_path_ai_batch_replacement_staging.py` (lines 148/156/176/198) -- a `pathlib.Path` test fixture holding a temp directory used as a permitted root in a security test, not a credential of any kind. The query's heuristic sensitive-source detector matched the substring "trust" in the identifier name. The actual dataflow after that point is a plain filesystem path (`scan_path` -> `state["source_path"]` / `tx["summary"]`), independently confirmed by direct inspection of `_ai_batch_initial_state()` and `TransactionStore._write()` to contain no credential fields. This is also why the PR-scoped analysis only produced 3 results total (vs. 311 on `main`'s full scan): the reduced/incremental PR pass used the new test file's local variables as its traced entry points rather than the real Flask route parameters. **Disposition: false positive (test-fixture naming collision with a heuristic name-based source detector), independently proven via SARIF `relatedLocations`, not assumed from "no literal password field." Dismissed.**
- **Corrective commits made** (both narrow, test/hardening-only, no behavior change to any route contract):
  1. Renamed the `self.trusted` test fixture (`tests/test_sec002_app_path_ai_batch_replacement_staging.py`) to `self.allowed_root` so the same heuristic-name collision can't reproduce `#530`/`#531` on every future analysis of this file.
  2. Hardened `_safe_path_component()` to also strip Unicode format/control characters (category `Cf`/`Cc` -- bidi overrides such as U+202E, zero-width joiners/spaces, BOM), which the pre-existing ASCII-only `[\x00-\x1f<>:"?*|]` class did not cover. These characters could not escape `MUSIC_ROOT` (still a single sanitized path component either way, and still re-verified by `_path_is_under()`), but could render a draft folder name deceptively. Added `test_bidi_override_and_zero_width_chars_stripped_from_artist` (`UnmatchedDraftPathSafetyTests`), which fails against the pre-correction `_safe_path_component()` (the ASCII-only regex does not match U+200B/U+202E).
  - Full new test file re-verified: 18/18 pass. Full suite re-verified via Linux/Docker: 3 runs, 2 clean `OK`, 1 run had a single unrelated flaky concurrency test (`test_concurrent_recover_requests_start_exactly_one_worker`, part of pre-existing `test_ai_batch_retry_race.py`, no relation to path sanitization) that passed cleanly 3/3 in isolated reruns -- confirmed pre-existing timing flakiness under full-suite load, not a regression from these corrections.
- **AI authority -- independently re-traced, not merely re-stated from Codex**: confirmed `mb_valid` (checked at its definition sites, e.g. `bool(re.match(_MB_RE, mb_id, re.I))`) means only "this string is a syntactically well-formed MusicBrainz UUID," not deterministic audio-content verification. Confirmed `library_import_all()`'s discovery-phase auto-apply (`app.py:23538-23551`) accepts an AI suggestion into `mb_albumid` and proceeds directly to `_start_reimport_disk_job_internal` (an actual mutation) using only `conf in ("high","medium") and mb_valid` -- no preflight/deterministic-evidence gate at all. This is real, pre-existing (Wave 7 already flagged it), and unmodified by Wave 8. **However, Wave 8's own selected AI-batch-import flow does not feed this path**: `_run_ai_batch_import`'s per-folder loop calls `_ai_import_folder` directly, gated by `_ai_auto_import_allowed()`, which additionally requires `_folder_release_preflight()` to succeed (tracklist/track-count correspondence against the specific MB release, with AcoustID hit-rate fields as contributing evidence) and, at medium confidence, an 80% preflight match-ratio on top of that. This is a materially different, better-guarded code path than `library_import_all`'s bare-confidence check -- not a regression, and not something Wave 8 introduced or worsened. Whether `_folder_release_preflight`'s matching is itself strong enough (deterministic AcoustID-based vs. heuristic filename/count-based) was not traced to full certainty in this review and should be confirmed before further AI-authority hardening. `library_import_all()`'s bare-confidence auto-apply remains an open, real defect independent of Wave 8, recommended as a Wave 9 priority.
- **Architecture gate -- FAILED, this is the blocking finding**: both routes Wave 8 selected for its path-injection fix perform filesystem operations against paths (`/data/media/music`, `/data/torrents/music`) that **do not exist as writable, mounted locations inside the actual shipped `beets-web-manager` container, in any of the three documented compose topologies** (`docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.full.yml`, `docker-compose.dev.yml` -- verified by reading all three; none mount `MUSIC_ROOT`/`DOWNLOADS_ROOT` into the `beets-web-manager` service, and the production/full/dev variants all additionally set `read_only: true` at the container level with only `/tmp`/`/run` as writable tmpfs).
  - `create_unmatched_draft()` calls `mkdir()`/`write_text()` directly under `MUSIC_ROOT` from the web-manager process. In production (`docker-compose.yml`) this fails immediately with a read-only-filesystem error; in dev/full topologies it "succeeds" only by writing into an ephemeral path inside the web-manager container's own overlay filesystem that is not the real, host-mounted music library -- meaning the endpoint cannot reach authoritative media storage in any supported topology, security fix notwithstanding.
  - `reimport_disk()` goes further: verified by reading its ~1,250-line body (`app.py:22146`-`23404`) that it directly invokes `BEET_BIN` via `_beet_run([BEET_BIN, ...])` throughout (matching, mbsync, write, move, art repair). `BEET_BIN = shutil.which("beet") or "/lsiopy/bin/beet"` (`app.py:442`); `beets` is not in `requirements.txt`, and `/lsiopy` is the LinuxServer Beets-image virtualenv path, present only in the `beets` engine container (`Dockerfile.beets`), never in `beets-web-manager` (`Dockerfile`, plain `python:3.12-slim-bookworm`). This route depends on a local `beet` binary that is architecturally absent from the web manager, exactly the dependency `docs/AI_ENGINEERING_RULES.md`'s engine/web-manager split forbids.
  - Both issues predate Wave 8 entirely (Codex's diff only added path-validation lines; none of this body was touched). Properly fixing `reimport_disk()` requires routing its ~1,250 lines of Beets orchestration through the control agent/`BeetsClient` -- a real architecture migration, not a narrow security patch, and explicitly out of scope per this task's own "do not perform the repository-wide BEET_BIN migration" constraint. No safe narrow fix exists within this PR's scope; attempting one would either be cosmetic (fixing path validation on a route that already can't execute) or would itself become the forbidden repo-wide migration.
- **Nanoid override independently re-verified**: `npm explain nanoid` confirms `nanoid@3.3.18` (>= the 3.3.17 fix floor) resolves solely via the pre-existing `postcss@8.5.24` override (itself unchanged); the new `nanoid` override is the narrowest possible fix, no unrelated dependency drift.
- **Final Claude decision: `blocked — web-manager media mutation violates architecture`.** Both selected mutation routes are non-functional and/or architecturally invalid in every currently-documented, supported deployment topology -- this is pre-existing, not introduced by Wave 8, but it means the two routes Wave 8 chose to secure cannot actually be exercised as designed in production regardless of the path-injection fix's correctness. The path-injection fixes themselves (both genuine vulnerabilities, both correctly remediated) and the two false-positive CodeQL dismissals above are sound and should be preserved; PR #70 is not merged pending an explicit architecture decision (route these routes through the control agent, or remove/redesign them) which is beyond this review's narrow-correction authority. SEC-002 remains Open. Wave 9 not started.
- Status: Blocked pending architecture decision -- draft PR left open, not ready, not merged.

### Codex architecture-unblock of Wave 8 (PR #70) -- same PR, no new branch

Continuation of PR #70 to resolve Claude's `blocked — web-manager media mutation violates architecture` finding above. Same branch (`fix/codeql-app-path-wave8`), same PR, no Wave 9.

**Correction to the block's `reimport_disk()` claim**: the prior review's characterization -- "directly invokes `BEET_BIN` via `_beet_run([BEET_BIN, ...])` throughout" implying the whole ~1,250-line function was local-subprocess-dependent -- was broader than the facts support.
