Tracing `_beet_run()` itself (`job_engine.py:118`) shows it does **not** run a local subprocess at all: it calls `_parse_remote_beet_command()` to strip the leading `beet`/`BEET_BIN` executable token and any `-c <path>`/`--config <path>` pair, then sends the remaining subcommand+args to `beets_client.run_command()` -- an authenticated HTTP call to the real Beets engine's control agent (`/commands/execute`, which already allowlists `import` and every other command this function uses). Every `_beet_run(base_std + [...])` call in `reimport_disk()` (repair-in-place mbsync/write/move, post-import mbsync/write/rename, modify/mbsync/write/rename-by-item, fetchart/embedart) was **already architecture-compliant**. DB access in the same function (`with _db() as con: ...`) is also already compliant -- `_db()` yields a `RemoteSQLiteConnection`, never a local `sqlite3.connect`. The one genuine violation was a single, isolated call: the core "tag & import in-place" step at the old `app.py:22907` used `subprocess.run(base_tmp + ["import", ...])` directly, bypassing `_beet_run()` entirely -- likely a leftover from before the control-agent split (`import` needed real config content, and `_beet_run()`'s `-c` stripping silently discards local config-file content with no way to forward it, which this call site never had a substitute for until now).

- **`reimport_disk()` fix**: replaced the one direct `subprocess.run(base_tmp + ["import", ...])` call with `_beet_run(base_tmp + ["import", ...], log, timeout=import_timeout, cancel=cancel_event, config_override=temp_cfg_content)`. Added a `config_override: str = ""` parameter to `_beet_run()` (`job_engine.py`) -- forwarded to `beets_client.run_command()`'s existing (previously always-empty) `config_override` argument, which the control agent already writes to its own temp YAML and passes as `-c` to its *local* `beet` invocation. This is the mechanism that lets `import`'s `copy: no / move: no / duplicate_action: ...` policy (essential -- without it Beets could copy/move files, causing data loss/duplication) and the shared `lyrics: auto: no / replaygain: auto: no` guards (preventing unwanted network calls during write/mbsync) actually reach the engine, instead of being silently dropped the way every pre-existing `-c <path>` token in this function already was. Applied `config_override=temp_cfg_content` (and `repair_cfg_content` for the separate `_repair_existing_album_in_place` closure's own temp config) to all `_beet_run(base_std/base_tmp + ...)` calls in the function for consistency, not just the one that was blocking. Timeout-preservation: the control agent maps a subprocess timeout to HTTP 408 with `"...timed out after Xs"`; the client wraps it as a plain `BeetsError`, which `_beet_run` returns as `_R(1, "", str(exc))` -- `reimport_disk()` now detects `"timed out" in r.stderr.lower()` to preserve its original "treat timeout as inconclusive, verify via the DB afterward" behavior (`import_timed_out`) instead of a hard failure.
  - `import`'s server-side root policy (`allowed_roots = ["staging"] if command == "import" else ["music", "staging"]` in `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, applied only to the dedicated `source_path` request field) turned out not to need any change: `_beet_run()` never populates `source_path` -- the folder argument travels inside `args` like every other command's arguments already did, validated instead by `_sanitize_command_path_args(args, ["music", "staging", "config", "tmp"])`, which already permits `"music"`. This covers both of `reimport_disk()`'s scenarios (already-in-library untracked audio, and staged/downloaded audio) without loosening the `import`-specific `source_path` policy that guards other callers (e.g. AI batch import, which should stay staging-only).
  - No repository-wide `BEET_BIN` migration performed. `BEET_BIN`/`_beet_run` call sites outside `reimport_disk()` are unchanged and out of scope; a full inventory is a Wave 9 candidate (see below).
- **`create_unmatched_draft()` fix**: this route only ever wrote review/submission metadata (`draft.json`, `musicbrainz_submission.txt` -- artist/album/year/source_url/tracks/status, no audio file placed or referenced) -- confirmed by reading the full route body and confirming nothing else in the backend or frontend reads these files back to drive any import (`grep` across `app.py`, `routes_*.py`, `backend/`, `tests/`, and `frontend/src` found zero other references; the frontend does not currently call this endpoint at all). Per the "prefer web-manager state unless MUSIC_ROOT placement is actually necessary" guidance, moved the write target from `MUSIC_ROOT` to a new `UNMATCHED_DRAFT_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("UNMATCHED_DRAFT_DIR", "/web-manager-data/unmatched_drafts"))` -- `/web-manager-data` is the one path every shipped Compose file actually mounts into `beets-web-manager` (used already for `.auth_token`, `last_scan.txt`, `.plex_client_identifier`). No engine-side capability needed; this is not authoritative media, so it was not moved behind the control agent. If a draft is later actually imported, that goes through `reimport_disk()`, which owns `MUSIC_ROOT` and validates its own identity evidence at that time -- this route only ever prepares review metadata.
  - Added an explicit collision policy: re-submitting the same `(artist, album)` is idempotent; a different pair that sanitizes to the same folder name (e.g. `"A/B"` vs. `"A\B"`, both becoming `A_B`) now returns `409` instead of silently overwriting the earlier draft.
  - Added recursive secret redaction (`_redact_unmatched_draft_tracks()`, reusing the existing `_redact_security_text()` helper already used for confirmation-reason sanitization elsewhere) over `tracks` and `source_url` before they can reach persisted JSON/text -- `tracks` is client-controlled free-form JSON and was previously written through unfiltered field-for-field; it is now projected down to a fixed `{title, duration}` shape with `_redact_security_text()` applied to each, and any extra field (e.g. a stray `api_key`/`authorization` value) is dropped outright rather than merely redacted in place.
  - API response shape preserved (`ok`, `local_id`, `path`, `album_folder`, `submission_file`) since nothing consumes it today; `path`/`submission_file` now honestly point at `UNMATCHED_DRAFT_ROOT`, not a fabricated `MUSIC_ROOT` location.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_sec002_wave8_engine_ownership.py` (new, 7 tests) -- source-level guards that `reimport_disk()` never calls `subprocess.run`/`Popen`/`call` directly and that its import step routes through `_beet_run(..., config_override=...)`; that `create_unmatched_draft()`'s code (excluding its explanatory docstring) never references `MUSIC_ROOT`; behavioral tests that `_beet_run` forwards `config_override` and strips the local `-c` token/executable correctly; that a remote 408 timeout is surfaced via `"timed out"` in `stderr` rather than silently swallowed; and -- the direct regression proof for the architecture block -- that the import step succeeds via the mocked remote client even when `BEET_BIN` points at a path that does not exist anywhere on the machine (`subprocess.run` mocked to raise `AssertionError` if ever called), reproducing the real web-manager container. `tests/test_sec002_app_path_ai_batch_replacement_staging.py`'s `UnmatchedDraftPathSafetyTests` updated: patches `UNMATCHED_DRAFT_ROOT` instead of `MUSIC_ROOT`, adds `test_music_root_is_never_touched` (asserts a separately-mocked `MUSIC_ROOT` stays completely empty across both a normal and a traversal-attempt submission -- the architecture assertion, not just containment), plus new collision (`test_different_draft_mapping_to_same_folder_is_a_collision`, `test_same_draft_resubmitted_is_idempotent`) and secret-redaction (`test_secret_like_track_fields_are_not_persisted`) tests. All prior path-injection/traversal/symlink/Unicode assertions preserved, not weakened.
- **Verification**: focused suite (new files + `test_ai_batch_retry_race`, `test_ai_batch_import_reliability`, `test_import_auto_enqueue`, `test_music_format_replacement_retry_pipeline`, `test_external_beets_architecture`, `test_transaction_engine`, `test_no_owner_specific_deployment_details`): 375 tests, 5/5 consecutive `OK`. Full suite via Linux/Docker: 3 runs, 2 clean `OK` (1865 tests), 1 run with the same single pre-existing flaky concurrency test noted in the prior review section (passed 3/3 in isolated reruns). Frontend: `npm ci`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`test` (15/15)/`build` clean, `npm audit --audit-level=high` 0 vulnerabilities (also fixed a duplicate `nanoid` key in `overrides{}` left by the earlier `git merge --no-ff origin/main` of PR #71's independent identical fix, and regenerated the lockfile on Linux). Static/security: `py_compile`, secret scan, `validate_compose_security.py`, `verify_security_config.py` (synthetic values) all pass; `git diff --check` clean. Privacy/compose: `test_no_owner_specific_deployment_details` 12/12 pass; zero diff to any of the three Compose files (confirms no media mount was added to unblock this); all three parse cleanly.
- **Mandatory disposable two-service runtime test -- surfaced a second, deeper, pre-existing architecture gap `reimport_disk()` still cannot cross.** Ran a real two-container stack: `beets-web-manager` with no media/staging mount and no local `beet` binary at all (confirmed: `which beet` and `ls /lsiopy` both fail; `ls /data` fails -- reproducing every shipped Compose topology exactly), talking over the network to a real `beets` engine container with genuine `MUSIC_ROOT`/staging mounts, a real `beet` binary, and the control agent. `create_unmatched_draft()` worked correctly end-to-end (files land under `/web-manager-data/unmatched_drafts`, the mocked `MUSIC_ROOT`-equivalent stayed empty). `reimport_disk()` did **not**: calling it with a real staged folder that only the engine can see returned `{"error": "Source path does not exist.", "ok": false}` -- the engine's own logs confirm the request never even reached `/commands/execute`; it failed inside `reimport_disk()`'s own pre-existing (not modified by this fix) web-manager-side call to `_resolve_import_review_source_path()`, which does `candidate.exists()`/`is_symlink()` checks against the *web manager's own filesystem* -- and the web manager has no local view of `/data/torrents` or `/data/media/music` in any shipped topology, so that check can never succeed for a real path. Reading further into `reimport_disk()` shows this is not an isolated line: `_validate_import_source_audio()` (called immediately after) calls `_validate_audio_tree_preferences()`, which inspects each audio file's actual format/bitrate -- also requiring local file reads the web manager cannot perform. So the BEET_BIN fix above is necessary but not sufficient: it addresses execution (once the engine receives an import command, it now runs there, not locally), but `reimport_disk()` still cannot be reached with a real staged path today, because its own pre-invocation validation and audio inspection assume local filesystem access this container has never had in the shipped topology.
  - This is exactly the debt already named `ARCH-003` in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Residual direct filesystem moves, deletes, copies, and directory removals in app.py. Under the supported Compose files the web-manager image is not the media owner, so these call sites need ARCH-003 controlled-boundary cleanup") -- confirmed here to be broader and more load-bearing than a documentation note: `_resolve_import_review_source_path()` and `_validate_audio_tree_preferences()` are shared by the entire Wave 6/7/8 import-review/AI-suggest/reimport surface, not just these two routes. A prior disposable-runtime pass in this same PR's history validated `reimport_disk()`/`start_ai_batch_import()` against a single container with media bind-mounted directly into the web-manager image for convenience -- which inadvertently tested a topology that does not match any shipped Compose file and masked this exact gap. This pass deliberately did not repeat that mistake.
  - **Not fixed here.** A safe fix requires an actual architecture decision, not a narrow patch: either (a) move existence/symlink/audio-format validation to the engine (a new engine capability, since `resolve_safe_path()`/`_sanitize_command_path_args()` in `backend/beets_control_agent.py` already do real, authoritative containment+symlink checking server-side for the args that reach `/commands/execute` -- meaning the web-manager's client-side pre-check may be functionally redundant defense-in-depth for THIS route rather than a distinct security boundary, but that needs to be confirmed deliberately, not assumed under time pressure), or (b) have the web manager query the engine for path/audio metadata before proceeding, or (c) something else product/architecture wants. Attempting a narrow patch (e.g. `require_exists=False`) without also addressing `_validate_import_source_audio()`'s local audio-format reads would still leave the route non-functional, and loosening a check shared by other callers (e.g. AI-batch scan, which genuinely does need real local access for its own `os.walk()`) risks weakening it elsewhere for the wrong reason.
- **Remaining debt, explicitly not resolved by this unblock** (kept for Wave 9, `SEC-002 Status: Open`):
  - The `reimport_disk()` local-filesystem-access gap above -- recommended as the next priority, likely larger than a normal wave slice.
  - `library_import_all()`'s bare-confidence (`conf in ("high","medium") and mb_valid`, no preflight/deterministic-evidence gate) auto-apply pathway -- real, pre-existing, Wave 7 and the Claude review both flagged it, still unmodified.
  - Whether `_folder_release_preflight()`'s track-matching (used by Wave 8's own, better-guarded AI-batch auto-import gate) is itself deterministic (AcoustID-based) or heuristic (filename/count-based) enough was not traced to full certainty.
  - Repository-wide `BEET_BIN`/local-subprocess inventory outside `reimport_disk()` -- not touched by this fix, still open across the rest of `app.py`.
  - AI-batch resource limits (unbounded folder count from `_ai_batch_find_audio_dirs`'s `os.walk()`, no cross-batch concurrency cap, no cancellation checkpoint during the initial walk) -- flagged by the Claude review, not addressed here (out of scope for the architecture unblock specifically).
  - Historical Wave 8 cluster members deferred since the original Wave 8 entry above (`_mark_pending_review_status`, `start_import`/`import_preflight`, `dedup_scan`/`dedup_cleanup`, `_mb_release_tracklist_*`, plus the adjacent `#417`-`#421`/`#480`-`#484` clusters).
- **New PR-scoped CodeQL alerts from this commit, not dismissed (Codex may not dismiss)**: `#532`/`#533` (`py/path-injection`, `app.py:26653`/`26655`, both in the new collision-check code -- `existing_draft_json.exists()`/`.read_text()`). Full SARIF codeFlow traced: both originate from the same `artist`/`album` -> `_safe_path_component()` -> `draft_path` chain as the already-dismissed `#529`, reaching `existing_draft_json = draft_path / "draft.json"` (`draft_path` at that point is already `draft_path_resolved`, re-verified by `_path_is_under()` a few lines earlier). Same established sanitizer-recognition gap, same root cause, no new risk -- recommended for Claude to independently confirm and dismiss using the same reasoning as `#529`.
- Status: `create_unmatched_draft()`'s architecture violation is fully resolved and verified end-to-end. `reimport_disk()`'s specific BEET_BIN/local-subprocess violation is fixed and verified, but a second, deeper, pre-existing gap in the same route (local path/audio validation assuming filesystem access the web manager does not have) means it still cannot be exercised end-to-end against a real staged folder in the shipped topology, and resolving that requires an architecture decision beyond this task's narrow-correction scope. Draft PR left open, not ready, not merged, pending Claude's second independent review.

### Codex ARCH-003 fix for reimport_disk() -- same PR, no new branch

Continuation of PR #70 to resolve the deeper gap the previous entry found. Same branch (`fix/codeql-app-path-wave8`), same PR, no Wave 9.

- **Root cause confirmed**: `reimport_disk()`'s own `_resolve_import_review_source_path()` call validated existence/containment/symlink-safety against the *web manager's own filesystem* -- which has no view of `MUSIC_LIBRARY_PATH`/`DOWNLOAD_PATH` in any shipped Compose topology. That responsibility now belongs to the Beets engine, the only place those roots are actually mounted.
- **Fix**: new engine-side `POST /imports/source/inspect` (`backend/beets_control_agent.py`, `inspect_import_source()`) -- reuses the engine's own `resolve_safe_path()` (full `realpath()` resolution then containment, which closes symlink escapes without a separate explicit check) against a fixed, operation-specific root policy (`IMPORT_SOURCE_OPERATIONS`: `"reimport"`/`"ai_batch_discovery"` -> `["music", "staging"]`; the caller names an operation, never its own trusted root), rejects root-self, then does a bounded (`IMPORT_INSPECT_MAX_ENTRIES_SCANNED`=20000, `IMPORT_INSPECT_MAX_AUDIO_FILES`=500, both env-overridable and clamped) walk that never follows symlinked subdirectories out of the trusted root, calling `audio_preferences.inspect_audio_file()` (real ffprobe-based inspection, `/usr/bin/ffprobe` confirmed present in the engine image) per audio file. Returns `canonical_path`, bounded `audio_files` evidence, and a `source_signature` (staleness fingerprint, explicitly documented as not recording identity) -- never raw file contents or host-path internals beyond the one validated canonical path.
  - `audio_preferences.py` is a portable, stdlib+ffprobe-only module with no Flask/app.py coupling, so it's copied into the engine image alongside the control agent (`Dockerfile.beets`) rather than reimplemented; `beets_control_agent.py` imports it with a try/except fallback (`import audio_preferences` when run standalone in the deployed image; `from backend import audio_preferences` when imported as `backend.beets_control_agent` for testing) so the file keeps working both ways.
  - New `BeetsClient.inspect_import_source()`. `reimport_disk()` now calls it instead of the old local validator, and only ever reuses the engine's own `canonical_path` afterward -- the raw client-supplied `aldir` is never reused past that point.
  - New `_validate_import_source_evidence()` (web-manager side): the same accept/reject policy as `_validate_import_source_audio()`, reusing the same underlying pure `validate_audio_properties()` decision function, just fed the engine's evidence instead of a local `ffprobe`/`rglob` pass. Used by `reimport_disk()` only -- `_validate_import_source_audio()`'s other 6 call sites (unrelated routes) are untouched. `_beet_import_timeout()` similarly gained a `_beet_import_timeout_for_count()` variant fed by the engine's `audio_count` instead of a local file count.
- **Verified via the mandatory disposable two-service runtime test** (real engine with real `MUSIC_LIBRARY_PATH`/`DOWNLOAD_PATH` mounts and a real `beet`; web manager with no media mount, no `beet`, no `/lsiopy` -- confirmed live): the "already in library, needs cataloging" reimport scenario (the primary documented use case; `aldir` under the engine's music root) now runs end-to-end past validation -- the job correctly receives the engine-validated canonical path, proceeds through real MusicBrainz release-group lookup and text-search fallback (both legitimate, pre-existing, unrelated logic), and safely queues for manual review when no candidate's tracklist matches (exactly the correct deterministic-identity-required behavior; it does not import against a wrong release). This is the concrete failure originally reproduced ("Source path does not exist") and it is fixed.
- **Two additional gaps found by the same test, not fixed in this pass**:
  1. **Torrent/download-staged (not-yet-in-library) reimport scenario**: when `aldir` is under a staging root rather than the music library and `TORRENT_SOURCE_MOVE_ALLOWED` is false (the default), `_preserve_torrent_source_path()` routes through `_stage_preserved_torrent_source()` -> `_audio_files_in_dir()`, which still does a local directory listing to copy files into a protected staging location before import -- so it currently fails with "No audio files found in torrent source folder" for a source the web manager cannot see. This is a genuine, separate local-filesystem mutation dependency (copies real file bytes, not just a read), not merely a validation check, and migrating it needs a new engine-side staging/copy capability comparable in size to the AI-batch discovery work below -- deferred, not silently accepted as fixed. The "already in library" scenario above is unaffected (it never reaches this code path).
  2. **`TransactionStore` default storage location**: unrelated to media/staging paths, but also surfaced by this test -- `backend/transaction_engine.py`'s `TransactionStore` defaults to `/config/transactions`, and `/config` is not mounted into `beets-web-manager` in any shipped Compose file either (only `/web-manager-data` is). Every job that goes through `start_python_with_transaction` (not just `reimport_disk()`) would hit this in real production. This is a distinct pre-existing bug from ARCH-003 (general web-manager local state, not engine-owned media), well outside this task's scope; flagged here because the runtime test could not otherwise proceed without a workaround (a temporary writable `/config` mount used only for this disposable test, never for production).
- **AI-batch discovery migration: not attempted in this pass.** `_ai_batch_find_audio_dirs()` still uses a local `os.walk()` and remains architecturally broken in the shipped topology for the same underlying ARCH-003 reason. `IMPORT_SOURCE_OPERATIONS` already includes an `"ai_batch_discovery"` root policy in anticipation of this, and `inspect_import_source()`'s bounded-walk logic is close to what a recursive-candidate-discovery endpoint would need, but AI-batch discovery needs a genuinely different operation (enumerate multiple candidate album directories across a whole tree, not inspect one already-known directory) with its own pagination/resource-limit contract (task explicitly calls for `max_directories`/`max_files`/`max_candidates`/cursor-based continuation) -- a comparably-sized separate piece of engineering that was not started, given the scope already delivered in this pass. `start_ai_batch_import()`/`ai_batch_import_recover()`'s own path *validation* (via `_resolve_import_review_source_path()`, SEC-002 Wave 8's original fix) is unaffected by this and still correctly rejects traversal/outside-root/symlink scan_path values -- what's missing is that the *walk itself*, once past validation, still cannot see engine-owned content from the web manager.
- Tests: `tests/test_sec002_wave8_engine_ownership.py` gained `ImportSourceInspectionTests` (11 tests, real temp directories and real symlinks, `@unittest.skipUnless(os.name == "posix", ...)` since `resolve_safe_path()` requires POSIX-absolute paths and the control agent only ever runs inside the Linux engine container) covering unknown-operation/outside-root/nonexistent/traversal/root-self/NUL rejection, valid staging and music directories, symlink-directory-escape non-traversal, source-signature change detection, and the `ai_batch_discovery` operation's music-root permission. `tests/test_sec002_app_path_ai_batch_replacement_staging.py`'s `ReimportDiskPathSafetyTests` rewritten to verify `reimport_disk()`'s handling of engine rejection/unavailability/success responses (mocked `beets_client.inspect_import_source`) instead of asserting on the now-removed local validator. `tests/test_music_format_preferences_integration.py`'s `test_import_paths_call_central_audio_validation` updated to expect `reimport_disk()`'s deliberate exception (`_validate_import_source_evidence`) rather than the shared local-scan validator, without weakening the assertion for the other 6 unaffected call sites.
- Verification: focused suite (198 tests: Wave 8, engine-ownership, music-format-preferences-integration, import-auto-enqueue, replacement-retry-pipeline, control-agent, AI-batch-retry-race, transaction-engine, privacy) 5/5 consecutive `OK`. Full suite via Linux/Docker: 3/3 consecutive clean runs, 1878 tests, `OK (skipped=69)`. Both images rebuilt `--no-cache` on the exact final head; OCI revision labels exact on both; `verify_beets_engine_image.py --check-s6-supervision` passed; confirmed `audio_preferences.py` and `/usr/bin/ffprobe` present in the engine image.
- Status: `reimport_disk()`'s originally-reported failure mode ("Source path does not exist" for any real path) is fixed and verified for its primary documented scenario. Two further, smaller gaps (torrent-staged local copy, unrelated `TransactionStore` default) and the full AI-batch discovery migration remain open, explicitly not claimed as resolved. SEC-002 remains Open. Draft PR left open, not ready, not merged.
- **New PR-scoped CodeQL alerts from this commit**:
  - `#532`/`#533` (`py/path-injection`, `app.py:26750`/`26752`, `draft_path.resolve(strict=False)` inside `create_unmatched_draft()`'s containment check): same established sanitizer-recognition gap as the already-dismissed `#529` (line numbers shifted from earlier edits to this same function) -- every component reaching `draft_path` still passes through `_safe_path_component()`, and this `.resolve()` is still read-only, still re-verified by `_path_is_under()` immediately after. Recommended for Claude to dismiss using the same reasoning as `#529`; Codex does not dismiss.
  - `#534` (`py/stack-trace-exposure`, `app.py:22293`, now fixed rather than dismissed): the new `except BeetsError as exc: ... f"Import source rejected: {exc}"` line interpolated the exception text directly into the HTTP response. `BeetsClient._request()`'s engine-error path normally wraps one of `inspect_import_source()`'s four fixed `error_code` strings, but falls back to embedding up to 200 raw response-body characters for any error response it doesn't recognize as JSON -- a real, if narrow, path for stack-trace-shaped text to reach the client. Fixed by mapping only the known error codes to a fixed safe message, collapsing anything else to one generic string, never interpolating `exc` itself. New regression test (`test_unrecognized_engine_error_never_leaks_raw_response_text`) asserts a synthetic embedded traceback never appears in the response, and fails against the pre-fix code.

### Claude independent review of a user-authored ARCH-003 completion commit -- same PR, no new branch

A subsequent commit (authored directly, not through the Codex/Claude review loop) added engine-side AI-batch discovery (`discover_import_sources`), torrent-source preservation (`preserve_import_source`), and an atomic signature/identity-bound reimport operation (`reimport_source_atomic`), plus wired `_ai_batch_find_audio_dirs()` and `_stage_preserved_torrent_source()` to use the first two. Independent review (not accepting the commit message's claims at face value, per instruction) found several genuine defects, all corrected in place rather than reverted:

1. **Pagination cursor was unsound, not merely inelegant.** The original cursor was a lexicographic "resume after this relative path" string marker. `/` (0x2F) sorts *above* space, `-`, and `.` (0x20/0x2D/0x2E) -- extremely common in real album/artist folder names ("Artist - Album") -- so a sibling directory named e.g. `"Parent-suffix"` can sort *before* `"Parent/child"` as a string while still being visited *after* it in the stack-based DFS traversal order. Resuming by skipping every `rel_dir <= marker` then silently dropped that sibling's entire subtree: real, silent candidate loss for common real-world names, proven with a constructed adversarial tree (`A/Z`, `A-suffix`, `AA`, `B/A`, `Z/A`), not a theoretical concern. **Fixed** by encoding the actual remaining DFS stack in the cursor (root/operation bound in for correctness, not as a security boundary -- `source_path` is independently re-validated via `resolve_safe_path()` on every call regardless of cursor content, so a tampered cursor can never grant access beyond what the caller was already authorized to discover). The file-count-limit break was also fixed to defer a directory *whole* rather than partially, for the same reason (partial-directory pushes plus a stack-encoded cursor could otherwise duplicate or drop entries at the boundary).
2. **`discover_import_sources()` rejected its own primary use case.** It copied the root-self rejection from `inspect_import_source()`/`preserve_import_source()`/`reimport_source_atomic()` (correct there -- those operate on one candidate album, where "the whole trusted root" is nonsensical), but discovery's entire purpose is scanning a whole root for multiple candidates, and `start_ai_batch_import()`'s default `scan_path` *is* the trusted staging root itself. Confirmed by writing the pagination test above and watching every call fail with `root_self_rejected`. **Fixed** by removing the check from `discover_import_sources()` only.
3. **`verify_deterministic_identity()` failed open on absent evidence**, not just on a missing DB row: no `expected_identity` argument, an empty `expected_identity` dict, and -- most importantly -- a `target_mb_albumid`/`target_mb_rgid` expectation where the source's audio simply had *no* embedded MusicBrainz tags to check, all previously returned `{"ok": True}`. A DB lookup exception for `existing_album_id` was also swallowed (`except Exception: pass`) and fell through to success. **Fixed**: every one of these now returns `review_required` (missing evidence) or `identity_verification_failed` (DB error) instead of `ok: True`; a real mismatch still returns `identity_mismatch`. Both are distinct error codes so callers/UI can present them differently ("we could not confirm" vs. "we confirmed it does not match").
4. **`reimport_source_atomic()` identified the imported album by "most recently inserted row"** when a lookup by `mb_albumid` failed, which can attribute the wrong album under concurrency or an unexpected Beets resolution. **Fixed**: falls back only to a lookup by the caller's own already-identity-checked `existing_album_id`, never a bare `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1`; if neither resolves, the result reports `"album_id": null, "album_id_verified": false` rather than a guess.
5. **`reimport_source_atomic()` used `--move` for the non-preserved (already-in-library) scenario**, forcing Beets to relocate/rename already-correctly-placed files according to its path template -- a real behavioral regression from `reimport_disk()`'s own established `copy: no / move: no` in-place semantics for that exact scenario. **Fixed**: `--copy` is used only for the preserved-staging branch (a disposable protected copy that specifically exists to be organized into the library); the non-preserved branch now defaults to an explicit `copy: no / move: no` config override when the caller didn't supply their own.
6. **Unvalidated `beets_options`**: `config_override` (an arbitrary caller-supplied YAML blob written directly to a config file Beets loads) had no length bound, `duplicate_action` accepted any string forwarded straight to `beet import -D`, and `mb_albumid` wasn't checked as a real UUID before being used in a search. **Fixed**: `mb_albumid` validated against the existing MB-UUID pattern, `duplicate_action` restricted to Beets' actual `{skip, keep, remove, merge}` values, `config_override` capped at 8192 characters.
7. **Raw exception/subprocess output reached HTTP responses.** `preserve_import_source()`'s exception handler embedded `str(ex)` (potentially host paths/OS error text) in its `message`; `reimport_source_atomic()` returned raw Beets `stdout`/`stderr` on both its failure and success paths. **Fixed**: every `message` in both functions is now either a fixed string or interpolates only caller-supplied identifiers (MBIDs, integers) that were already present in the request; `stdout`/`stderr` are no longer returned at all.
8. **`preserve_import_source()` allowed `"music"` as a source root**, despite existing specifically to protect torrent/download sources from Beets mutating them during import -- library content never needs this operation. **Fixed**: staging-only (least privilege).
9. **Critical, unresolved by this review: `reimport_source_atomic()`/`verify_deterministic_identity()` are not called by production.** `grep -n "reimport_source\b" app.py` returns zero matches outside `backend/beets_client.py`'s own method definition -- `reimport_disk()`'s actual mutation still runs through the pre-existing `_beet_run()` -> `/commands/execute` path (fixed for local-BEET_BIN dependency in an earlier commit, but with no signature re-check or deterministic-identity verification immediately before the mutation itself). The source-signature check `_stage_preserved_torrent_source()` forwards *does* protect the preservation-copy step for torrent-staged sources, and `inspect_import_source()`'s validation *does* run before any mutation -- but the atomic, identity-verified, signature-bound mutation path this commit built exists only as unreachable infrastructure today. Claude did not wire it into `reimport_disk()` in this review: doing so safely requires reconciling it with `reimport_disk()`'s existing branching (the separate `_repair_existing_album_in_place()` mbsync/write/move path for existing albums vs. a fresh `beet import`), its established timeout/`import_timed_out` semantics, and its multi-step "find album in DB, match tracks, write tags, rename" logic that runs *after* import today -- enough independent risk to a real, in-production mutation path that rushing it under review time pressure would be irresponsible. This is recorded as the reason PR #70 is not approved in this pass.
- New tests: `DiscoverImportSourcesPaginationTests` (6, real adversarial-tree/symlink/cursor-tamper coverage), `VerifyDeterministicIdentityFailClosedTests` (9, no filesystem needed), `PreserveImportSourceRootPolicyTests` (5, real copy/collision/idempotency coverage). All added to `tests/test_sec002_wave8_engine_ownership.py`.
- Status: the fixes above are real, verified, and safe to ship as improvements to the engine-side discovery/preservation infrastructure and to close the AI-batch `os.walk()` gap (which *is* wired into production via `_ai_batch_find_audio_dirs()`). The atomic reimport/identity-verification path remains unused by production and is not claimed otherwise. SEC-002 remains Open. Wave 9 not started.

### Codex: wire production `reimport_disk()` to the reviewed atomic engine mutation -- same PR, no new branch

Resolves Claude's remaining blocker (`reimport_source_atomic()`/`verify_deterministic_identity()` built but unreachable from production). `reimport_disk()`'s actual Beets mutation now runs through `beets_client.reimport_source()` -> `POST /imports/reimport` -> `reimport_source_atomic()`, not a bare `_beet_run(... "import" ...)`.

1. **Evidence selection is branch-dependent, by design, not by omission.** `expected_deterministic_identity` is built from the strongest evidence `reimport_disk()` already has at the mutation point:
   - `existing_album_id` present (repairing/re-tagging an already-known album): passes `{"existing_album_id": existing_album_id}` only -- deliberately *not* also `mb_albumid`. `verify_deterministic_identity()`'s `existing_album_id` check only objects if the source's *embedded* tags actively conflict with the DB row; absence of embedded tags (the common case for a folder being repaired) is not a conflict, so this branch succeeds for its ordinary case without weakening the check.
   - No `existing_album_id` (brand-new folder): passes `{"mb_albumid": mb_albumid}`, the only concrete deterministic claim available. If the source has no embedded MusicBrainz tags -- the common case for freshly downloaded, not-yet-tagged audio -- the engine correctly returns `review_required` rather than importing on trust. **This is a deliberate, real behavior change**: a from-scratch import of untagged audio that previously proceeded automatically (trusting Beets' own `asis --search-id` matching alone) now requires embedded source tags or an existing DB row to import without manual review. AcoustID evidence *is* already gathered by `_folder_release_preflight()` for this same branch, but the engine has no AcoustID/network capability to independently re-verify it at the mutation boundary, so it is intentionally not passed as identity evidence -- using it would mean trusting the caller's own claim exactly the way the original fail-open bug did. Not resolved in this pass; recorded as remaining debt below.
2. **Torrent-staged sources are preserved exactly once, not twice.** `_stage_preserved_torrent_source()` (the up-front preservation call `reimport_disk()` used to make before its own mutation) is removed entirely -- `reimport_source_atomic()` already detects a torrent-staged source and preserves it internally, immediately before the same import it protects. Calling both would have silently double-copied the source into `.preserved_staging`. Confirmed via `grep`: `reimport_disk()` no longer references `beets_client.preserve_import_source` or `_preserve_torrent_source_path(aldir)` at all.
3. **Album lookup after import no longer guesses.** The old three-strategy heuristic (path-prefix match, "recently added" timestamp heuristic, album-name search) is replaced by the atomic endpoint's own `album_id`/`album_id_verified`/`album_lookup_failed` fields (looked up server-side by `mb_albumid`, the same key used for `--search-id`). If the result isn't verified, `reimport_disk()` now raises rather than falling back to a heuristic guess.
4. **Timeout regression fixed engine-side.** `reimport_source_atomic()`'s Beets subprocess previously had a flat 180s timeout regardless of album size -- a real regression against `reimport_disk()`'s own `_beet_import_timeout_for_count()` (300s-1200s, scaled by track count). Added `_reimport_timeout_for_count()` to `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, computed from the engine's own freshly re-inspected `audio_count` (never trusted from the caller), mirroring the web-manager formula exactly. `reimport_disk()`'s HTTP client timeout is set to the same scaled value plus a margin so the client doesn't give up before the engine's own bound could elapse.
5. **Cancellation semantics unchanged, not regressed.** Traced `_beet_run()`'s `cancel=cancel_event` parameter: it is accepted but never referenced in `_beet_run()`'s body -- the pre-existing architecture already has no way to interrupt an in-flight remote Beets subprocess mid-execution; cancellation is cooperative, checked *between* steps only. `reimport_disk()` now checks `cancel_event.is_set()` immediately before calling `beets_client.reimport_source()`, matching the same cooperative-only guarantee the old `_beet_run(import...)` call actually had (not the stronger guarantee its `cancel=` parameter's name implied).
6. **Soft-timeout recovery preserved.** The old code treated a CLI-reported import timeout as inconclusive rather than a hard failure, since the mutation might have completed despite the process exceeding its wall-clock budget, and checked the DB directly afterward. `reimport_source_atomic()`'s own timeout returns a hard `import_failed`; `reimport_disk()` now detects the "timed out" message and performs an equivalent direct `mb_albumid`-keyed DB lookup (the web manager has its own authoritative read access to the same Beets library DB) before treating it as a real failure.
7. **Review-routing, not raw failure, for identity/staleness rejections.** `stale_source`, `identity_mismatch`, `review_required`, and `identity_verification_failed` responses are routed through the existing `_maybe_queue_review()` mechanism with a fixed, honest per-code message, instead of surfacing the engine's raw error code as an unhandled failure.
8. **`_delete_if_already_in_library()`'s phrase-detection input is now always empty.** The atomic endpoint deliberately never returns raw Beets stdout/stderr (an intentional SEC-002 sanitization, not an oversight), so the old "beet reported this as a duplicate" text-matching signal no longer exists. Traced `_delete_if_already_in_library()` itself first: it already resolves paths against the web manager's own local filesystem view, which has no view of engine-owned download paths in the shipped topology -- it was already an inert no-op in production before this change, so passing it an empty string changes nothing observable.
9. **Tests**: `ReimportSourceAtomicProductionPathTests` (real, non-mocked-filesystem coverage of the already-in-library `copy:no/move:no` path, the torrent-staged single-preservation path, and engine-side timeout scaling, against a real temporary sqlite library DB), `ReimportTimeoutScalingTests` (pure bounds), `ReimportDiskSinglePreservationTests` (source-level: the removed helper stays removed, no direct preserve call), two new `VerifyDeterministicIdentityFailClosedTests` cases proving `existing_album_id`-alone evidence succeeds without embedded tags and correctly conflicts when the DB's own current `mb_albumid` disagrees with embedded source tags, and an updated `ReimportDiskNoLocalSubprocessTests` case asserting the old `_beet_run(... "import" ...)` pattern is gone and `beets_client.reimport_source(` is present with signature/identity/config_override forwarding. Full suite: 1920 tests, 3/3 consecutive clean runs on Linux, `OK (skipped=69)`.
- **Remaining debt, explicitly not resolved by this pass**:
  - AcoustID evidence gathered during preflight cannot be independently re-verified by the engine (no network/API-key access there) and is therefore not usable as engine-side deterministic identity evidence. A from-scratch import of untagged staged audio with no existing DB row will correctly, and more conservatively than before, require manual review.
  - The post-import pipeline (`_match_tracks_from_mb`, AcoustID validation for `wanted_tracks`, merge-into-existing-album logic, mbsync/write/move/art) was deliberately left structurally unchanged, fed by a single-element `album_ids = [aid]` list from the atomic endpoint instead of the old heuristic's (occasionally multi-element) result -- not independently re-audited in this pass beyond confirming it still receives the shape it expects.
  - `_delete_if_already_in_library()` remains dead/inert code in the current shipped topology (pre-existing, not introduced here); not removed, since it is out of scope for this specific unblock.
- Status: production `reimport_disk()` no longer bypasses source-signature and (where real evidence exists) deterministic-identity verification at its actual mutation point. SEC-002 remains Open pending Claude's independent final review of this wiring. Wave 9 not started. PR #70 left draft/unmerged -- Claude retains final merge authority.

### Claude final merge review of the production reimport wiring -- two critical findings, both fixed -- same PR, no new branch

Independent review did not accept green tests/CI as proof this wiring was safe or functional. Two genuine, serious defects were found via live, non-mocked adversarial testing against the actual engine code and a real `beet` binary -- neither was reachable by the mocked-subprocess unit tests added in the prior round, which is exactly why this round tested outside the mocks.

1. **CRITICAL, exploitable: `existing_album_id` alone authorized importing ANY trusted source into ANY existing album, with zero content verification.** `verify_deterministic_identity()`'s `existing_album_id` branch only queried `SELECT id FROM albums WHERE id = ?` -- proving the DB row *exists*, never that the supplied `source_path` actually belongs to it. When the source's audio had no embedded MusicBrainz tags (the common case for untagged/fresh content), the tag-conflict check silently no-opped (`source_album_mbids` empty short-circuits the comparison), and the function returned `{"ok": True}`. **Proven live**: constructed a real trusted-but-unrelated staging folder with untagged fake audio, called `reimport_source_atomic()` with an arbitrary real `existing_album_id`, and confirmed a real `beet import` subprocess was actually launched against it (`beet import ... --search-id <caller-supplied-mbid> <unrelated-folder>`) -- a caller with nothing but ordinary authenticated API access could have overwritten/re-tagged any existing album with any unrelated content. **Fixed**: when there is no embedded-tag evidence to cross-check, `verify_deterministic_identity()` now additionally requires that the existing album's own current library item paths (queried from the same DB) are actually located under the source's canonical path -- real, deterministic, content-independent proof that the source folder *is* that album's own library location, not merely that the album ID is real. An unrelated source now correctly returns `review_required`, and no Beets import is launched. The legitimate library-repair-in-place case (source path already under the album's own folder) is unaffected and still requires no embedded tags. A staged-missing-track replacement into an existing album (source is a staging folder, not the album's own library path) now also correctly requires review unless the source carries matching embedded tags -- a real, deliberate tightening of a narrower legitimate use case, in favor of closing the exploit; not silently worked around.
2. **CRITICAL, functional: the atomic endpoint's `beet import` command was syntactically invalid and could never succeed against a real Beets binary.** Two independent bugs in the same command: (a) `--quiet-fallback` (which pairs with a following `asis` VALUE) was omitted -- the bare trailing `"asis"` token was parsed by Beets as an extra positional `PATH` argument, which does not exist, aborting the entire import before it reached the real target (`error: no such file or directory: asis`, confirmed against a real installed `beet` binary); (b) `-D <duplicate_action>` is not a real Beets CLI flag at all -- Beets only supports `import.duplicate_action` via config file (confirmed against real `beet import --help`, which lists no `-D`/`--duplicate-action` option) -- its presence caused `beet import: error: no such option: -D` regardless of the `--quiet-fallback` fix. **In every real invocation** (`duplicate_action` always defaults to `"remove"`, so `-D` was always present), the command could never run to completion; the exit code was 1 or 2 depending on which parse failure was hit first, both silently absorbed or misread as ordinary "no MusicBrainz match" failures by anyone testing only against mocked subprocess calls or without a real MB release to match against (exactly what happened in the prior round's own "live" runtime test, which got a real `import_failed` result but for entirely the wrong diagnosed reason). **Fixed**: `--quiet-fallback` now directly precedes its `asis` value; `-D`/`duplicate_action` removed from the CLI args entirely, with `duplicate_action` applied only via `config_override`'s `import.duplicate_action` YAML key (the function's own synthesized default config now includes it too, so the parameter still has effect when a caller relies on that default). Re-verified against a real installed `beet` binary: the corrected command now parses and runs to completion (exit 0).
- Tests: `test_unrelated_source_with_existing_album_id_is_never_authorized` (real filesystem + real sqlite DB, proves the exploit is closed and no `beet import` subprocess call is ever made for it), `test_existing_album_id_alone_with_no_corroborating_evidence_requires_review` / `test_existing_album_id_with_source_matching_its_own_library_path_succeeds` (unit-level positive/negative coverage of the new path-binding check), structural CLI-shape assertions added to the existing copy/move test (`--quiet-fallback` immediately precedes `asis`, no `-D` present, `duplicate_action:` present in the default config), and `test_beet_import_command_is_syntactically_valid_against_real_beets` (skipped when no real `beet` binary is on `PATH`, e.g. the web manager's own CI job; exercised whenever one is available, which is how bug 2 was actually found and how its fix was verified).
- Verification: full suite 1922-1923 tests, 3/3 consecutive clean runs on Linux; focused suite 5/5 consecutive clean runs on Windows including the PR #72 first-run auth subset (unaffected, confirming no regression from main's unrelated advance).
- Status: both findings fixed and independently re-verified against real (non-mocked) code paths, including a real `beet` binary. This is what final review is for: neither defect was visible from green CI or from tests that never left the mock boundary.

## ARCH-013 Endpoint Security Inventory Judgment Fields Incomplete For Newer Route Modules

- Affected area: `security/endpoint_inventory.json`, `scripts/generate_endpoint_inventory.py`, `tests/test_security_hardening.py`.
- Evidence: The remaining 36 `NEEDS_REVIEW` routes reported by the v0.1.10 Tier 1 setup/auth review were regenerated from source, individually inspected, and annotated. The inventory now covers all 240 current Flask routes and records an explicit `security_classification` for each route (`PUBLIC`, `SETUP_ONLY`, `AUTHENTICATED`, `ADMIN_ONLY`, or `INTERNAL`). Current counts are: 15 `PUBLIC`, 6 `SETUP_ONLY`, 219 `ADMIN_ONLY`, 0 `AUTHENTICATED`, 0 `INTERNAL`, and 0 `NEEDS_REVIEW`. In this app's current single-administrator model, protected operator routes are intentionally classified `ADMIN_ONLY` rather than inventing a new RBAC layer.
- Enforcement: `scripts/generate_endpoint_inventory.py` now carries `security_classification` forward as a human-reviewed field, so brand-new routes are generated with `NEEDS_REVIEW` until explicitly classified. `.github/workflows/security.yml` already runs `python3 scripts/generate_endpoint_inventory.py --check`, and `tests/test_security_hardening.py::SecurityHardeningTests::test_endpoint_inventory_has_no_unreviewed_routes` asserts the committed inventory has no `NEEDS_REVIEW` fields and every route has one of the allowed classifications.
- Additional fix found during route review: `backend.transaction_engine.TransactionStore._path()` now accepts only the generated transaction ID format (`txn_<epoch>_<12 hex chars>`), not merely any `txn_`-prefixed string without slashes/NULs. This preserves path containment and prevents attacker-shaped IDs from reaching transaction export headers if a malformed transaction file ever appears.
- Status: Closed.
## ARCH-014 Custom-Order Test Subset Can Still Contaminate `test_auth_and_setup.py`

- Affected area: `tests/test_routes_setup.py`, `tests/test_auth_and_setup.py`.
- Reproducer before fix: `python -m unittest tests.test_routes_setup tests.test_auth_and_setup` failed deterministically with 10 `test_auth_and_setup.py` failures; Claude's five-module non-alphabetical sequence (`tests.test_routes_setup tests.test_external_beets_architecture tests.test_auth_and_setup tests.test_v0110_upgrade_and_matched_stack tests.test_beets_fresh_install_plugins`) reproduced the same contamination.
- Root cause: `tests/test_routes_setup.py` imported `routes_setup` against a stub `sys.modules["app"]`, then relied on module teardown to pop `app`/route modules. `unittest` imports later test modules before running earlier suites, so `test_auth_and_setup.py` could already hold references to the real `app` and `routes_setup` modules when the stub suite ran. After `test_routes_setup` popped `sys.modules["app"]`, route handlers that perform runtime `from app import ...` loaded a second, unpatched app module during `test_auth_and_setup.py`, bypassing that test's temporary credential/setup paths and causing order-dependent 401/409/rate-limit/cookie failures.
- Fix: `tests/test_routes_setup.py` now snapshots the relevant module entries before each stub import and restores the exact previous modules with `TestCase.addCleanup()` after each test. `tearDownModule()` is now only a best-effort interrupted-test guard for a still-installed stub, not the normal isolation mechanism.
- Verification after fix: `python -m unittest tests.test_routes_setup tests.test_auth_and_setup` passed (`Ran 59 tests ... OK`), and the prior five-module reproducer passed (`Ran 262 tests ... OK (skipped=1)`).
- Status: Closed.

## ARCH-015 `/api/config` Read Beets config.yaml From The Wrong Container (BUG-1, v0.1.12)

- Affected area: `app.py` (`get_config`/`save_config`/`revert_config`), `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, `backend/beets_client.py`.
- Discovered: production `/api/config` (Settings page config editor) verification during the v0.1.11 TrueNAS rollout -- `GET /api/config` returned 500 unconditionally.
- Root cause: `_CONFIG_PATH = Path("/config/config.yaml")`/`_CONFIG_BAK` read/wrote a path local to the web-manager container. Since the two-service architecture cutover (2026-07-28), `config.yaml` lives only on the Beets *engine* container's own `/config` mount; the web manager's own `/config` volume is a separate, unrelated directory for its own app state. The route had never been updated for that split and always 500'd in the real deployed topology, undetected because unit tests mocked the local filesystem directly rather than exercising the real two-container boundary.
- Fix: added `GET /config`, `POST /config`, `POST /config/revert` to the control agent (BEETSDIR-relative, never a client-supplied path -- no traversal surface). `app.py`'s three routes now proxy through `beets_client.get_config()`/`save_config()`/`revert_config()` instead of touching the local filesystem, with a stable `error_code`-based mapping (`config_not_found`/`config_permission_denied`/`config_backup_not_found`/`beets_unavailable`/`beets_auth_failed`) instead of a generic 500. Secret-line redaction stays in `app.py`, at the boundary before content reaches the browser -- not duplicated onto the agent.
- Regression tests: `tests/test_v0112_config_engine_access.py` (21 tests: readable/unavailable/permission-denied/malformed-response/save-validation/revert, plus real `ControlAgentHandler` round-trip tests against a temp BEETSDIR), `tests/test_sec002_app_stacktrace.py::ConfigYamlExceptionSanitizationTests` (updated to mock `beets_client` instead of `Path`), `scripts/verify_first_run_docker_acceptance.py` scenario 2 (real two-container check: `GET /api/config` returned 502, not 500, confirming the request now reaches the engine boundary and gets a structured response instead of crashing on a never-mounted local path).
- Status: Closed.

## ARCH-016 Legacy-Established Installs Never Got `.setup_complete` (BUG-2, v0.1.12)

- Affected area: `routes_setup.py` (`_migrate_legacy_setup_complete_if_established`, new).
- Discovered: live on the v0.1.11 TrueNAS production rollout -- `/api/setup/status` reported `first_run_required=false` but `setup_complete=false`. The install had valid legacy credentials (`.initial_admin_password`, `.browser_setup_state` = `legacy_established`) predating the two-step `setup_first_run` -> `setup_complete` flow, so nothing in its history had ever called `POST /api/setup/complete`. `_first_run_setup_required()` correctly never reopened first-run for it, but `frontend/src/App.tsx`'s route guard additionally gates on `setup_complete` specifically -- a real logged-in administrator was shown the First-Run Setup wizard instead of their dashboard on every visit. Corrected on that production instance by hand at the time; this closes it with a real, automatic migration.
- Migration condition (fail-closed): runs once at `routes_setup.py` import time (a no-op under the `test_routes_setup.py` stub-app harness). Only creates the marker when `.setup_complete` is missing, `_first_run_setup_required()` is already `False`, AND `_browser_password_is_usable(_security_auth_password())` independently re-verifies a real, currently-usable credential exists right now -- the same full env-var/file/persisted-hash/legacy-initial-password resolution chain `_migrate_or_initialize_setup_state()` itself used to originally classify the install as `claimed`/`legacy_established`, not a cached state-file string alone. (`_has_explicit_browser_password()` was deliberately not used -- it omits the legacy `.initial_admin_password` fallback and would wrongly fail-closed on exactly the installs this migration exists for.) A fresh install, or one whose credential has since been deleted/corrupted, is correctly left alone.
- Atomicity: reuses `_claim_setup_completion_marker()` (the same `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` cross-process-safe claim `POST /api/setup/complete` itself uses) -- safe under concurrent worker/startup races, idempotent on every later restart.
- Never touches username/password/token/Flask-secret/browser-setup-state files -- only ever creates the completion marker.
- Regression tests: `tests/test_v0112_legacy_setup_migration.py` (8 tests: legacy-established/claimed installs get migrated, already-migrated installs untouched, no-credential and corrupt-state installs fail closed, fresh installs stay fresh, concurrent calls don't corrupt state, stub-app harness no-ops). `scripts/verify_first_run_docker_acceptance.py` scenario 2 confirmed `setup_complete=true` on a real v0.1.8-style upgrade container. Frontend routing coverage (`authenticated + setupComplete=true -> dashboard, not /setup`) was already added in the v0.1.11 pass (`frontend/tests/router.test.tsx`); unchanged since no frontend code needed to change.
- Status: Closed.

## ARCH-017 Control-Agent Diagnostics Produced False Timeout/Unhealthy Results Under Load (BUG-3, v0.1.12)

- Affected area: `backend/beets_control_agent.py` (`run_agent`, `_beet_version_snapshot`, `_cached_beet_version_snapshot` (new), `get_loaded_beet_plugins`, `_agent_status_payload`), `routes_setup.py` (`_beets_plugin_diagnostics`).
- Discovered: live on the v0.1.11 TrueNAS production rollout under real host memory pressure -- `beet version` measured ~0.5s warm vs. up to ~29s under load; the control agent's `/status` (and every capability-gated `/commands/execute`/`/jobs/create` call, via `get_loaded_beet_plugins()`) launched a fresh, uncached `beet version` subprocess on every call with a 5s timeout, so `plugin_loader_ok=false`/`0 plugins loaded` was reported despite ~20/20 plugins genuinely loading against a warm probe moments later.
- Root cause (two compounding issues, both fixed):
  1. `run_agent()` used a plain single-threaded `http.server.HTTPServer` (`serve_forever()` handles exactly one request at a time). A slow in-flight `/status` probe blocked *every other request behind it*, including Docker's own `/health` liveness check -- which is otherwise a hardcoded, dependency-free response with no subprocess cost at all. Production logs showed `BrokenPipeError` on both `/health` and `/status` responses: the handler finished computing a (correct) response only after the client (Docker healthcheck, or the web manager) had already given up and closed the connection.
  2. No caching: `_beet_version_snapshot()` ran a fresh subprocess launch on every single call site, so the expensive cost was paid far more often than necessary, worsening the effect of (1) and directly causing the diagnostic timeout under load.
- Fix: (1) `run_agent()` now constructs `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` (stdlib, Python 3.7+) instead of the plain `HTTPServer`, with `daemon_threads=True` -- a slow `/status` call can no longer block `/health` or any other concurrent request. Verified safe under concurrency: `JOBS`/`JOBS_LOCK` were already lock-protected, SQLite connections are opened fresh per request, and `acquire_os_lock()` already opens an independent file object per call (POSIX `flock()` semantics correctly serialize across both threads and processes on the same open file description) -- no new shared-state hazard introduced. (2) Added `_cached_beet_version_snapshot()`: single-flight cached (mirrors `routes_setup.py`'s own `/api/setup/status` cache design -- the whole check-build-store sequence runs under one lock so N concurrent callers past the TTL trigger exactly one subprocess launch), default TTL 30s (`BEETS_VERSION_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`), per-probe timeout widened from a hardcoded 5s to a configurable 12s default (`BEETS_VERSION_PROBE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) since caching -- not a larger timeout alone -- is what actually bounds how often the cost is paid. A genuine probe failure (timeout, or `beet` not launchable at all) now falls back to the last known-good cached snapshot (bounded by `BEETS_VERSION_CACHE_MAX_STALE_SECONDS`, default 300s) instead of replacing known-good plugin data with an empty/failed result -- exactly BUG-3's observed symptom. A completed run reporting real `plugin_failures` is still current ground truth and is always cached/returned directly, never treated as a reason to fall back to older data. `GET /status?refresh=1` (matching the web manager's own `/api/setup/status?refresh=1` convention) forces a fresh probe. New `diagnostics_fresh`/`diagnostics_cache_age_seconds`/`diagnostics_refresh_error` fields flow from the agent's `/status` response through `_beets_plugin_diagnostics()` into the browser-facing setup-status payload.
- Subprocess safety unchanged and reconfirmed: `subprocess.run([BEET_BIN, "version"], timeout=...)` (argument list, never `shell=True`) already kills and reaps the child on `TimeoutExpired` -- no new zombie-process risk from the timeout change, verified by a dedicated test.
- Docker healthcheck: `/health` was already a hardcoded, dependency-free response (not itself the bottleneck) -- the fix is that it can now actually be *served* promptly even while `/status` is mid-probe.
- Regression tests: `tests/test_v0112_diagnostics_timeout.py` (15 tests: fast/slow/timeout probes, subprocess reaping, cold start, cache hit/miss, forced refresh, transient-timeout-preserves-known-good-plugins, cold-timeout-reports-real-failure, max-stale-window expiry, completed-run-with-failures still cached directly, single-flight under 10 concurrent callers, `/status?refresh=1` wiring, server-class assertion). Existing `tests/test_external_beets_architecture.py` tests (`TestAcoustIDChromaCapability`, `TestControlAgentCapabilityEnforcement`) needed a per-test cache reset added to `setUp()` -- the new module-level cache otherwise leaked a previous test's mocked `subprocess.run()` result into a later test expecting its own fresh mock; a real order-dependency bug in the new code, found and fixed via the full-suite run, not assumed away. `scripts/verify_first_run_docker_acceptance.py` scenario 1 confirmed the new `diagnostics_fresh` field is present end to end (real container, first call 0.61s) and that a second `/api/setup/status` call is fast (0.00s -- cache hit, not a repeat multi-second subprocess launch).
- Status: Closed.

### Wave 9: Playlist, Plex Sync & Staging-Manifest Path Security (22 alerts)

- Starting main SHA: `98d490ec3c40f331dd23061d522e2ac79b015576` (PR #81 / v0.1.13 release commit).
- Starting open-alert count (live, paginated, independently re-verified via GitHub CodeQL API): 188 total open alerts, 147 `py/path-injection` alerts across the repository, 143 `py/app.py` `py/path-injection` alerts.
- Alert cluster selected: **Playlist, Plex Sync & Staging-Manifest Path Security**, alert numbers `#297, #298, #299, #300, #301, #302, #303, #304, #305, #306, #307, #308, #309, #310, #313, #314, #315, #316, #317, #318, #319, #320` (22 open alerts total).
- Scope & Remediations:
  1. **Playlist Name Sanitization & Staging Directory Containment** (`_clean_playlist_name`, `get_playlist_staging_root`, `_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs`, `#297`): Hardened `_clean_playlist_name` to strip control characters, null bytes, unicode bidi/zero-width chars, path separators (`/`, `\`), path traversal segments (`..`), and leading/trailing dots and underscores. Enforced `_path_is_under` containment under `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` for all staging directory creation and permission setting (`os.chmod`).
  2. **Atomic JSON Replace Security** (`_playlist_atomic_json_replace`, `#302-#308`): Validated that both target `path` and temporary `tmp` files are strictly contained within allowed data/staging roots (`PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT`, `PLAYLIST_DIR`, `MUSIC_ROOT`) before any `mkdir()`, `open()`, `replace()`, or `unlink()` call.
  3. **Staged Track Deletion Safety** (`_playlist_delete_staged_track_file`, `#317-#320`): Enforced root-self rejection, strict staging containment (`PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` or per-playlist staging root), Beets music library path rejection (`MUSIC_ROOT`), audio extension verification (`AUDIO_EXT`), and TOCTOU symlink re-validation immediately before calling `unlink()`.
  4. **Plex Path Translation & Library Path Resolution** (`_plex_translate_beets_path`, `_plex_is_final_library_path`, `_plex_beets_path_candidates`, `_playlist_resolve_item_path`, `#298, #299, #300, #301`): Hardened `_playlist_resolve_item_path` so absolute paths are strictly validated against allowed music and staging roots before resolution. Enforced component-aware path containment to prevent prefix-collision traversal (e.g. `/data/media/music2` matching `/data/media/music`).
  5. **M3U Generation & Local M3U Parsing** (`_create_playlist_outputs`, `playlist_parse`, `_playlist_m3u_track_rows`, `#310, #313, #314, #315, #316`): Enforced `PLAYLIST_DIR` path containment and sanitized embedded newlines (`\r`, `\n`) in artist/title metadata to prevent M3U directive/line injection.
- Tests added: `tests/test_sec002_wave9_playlist_plex_staging_paths.py` (11 tests covering traversal sanitization, staging containment, atomic JSON replacement, staged file deletion safety, non-audio/outside file rejection, M3U newline sanitization, and component-aware Plex path containment).
- Verification: Passed 11/11 wave 9 tests, 75 Wave 8/PR#72 regression tests, 5/5 focused test suite runs, 3/3 full Linux test runs (2047 tests, 0 failures), frontend typecheck/lint/test/build/audit (0 vulnerabilities), static security compilation, secret scanning, compose validation, and exact-head Docker verification.
- Status: Code remediated & verified locally. Draft PR open for Claude review.

### Claude independent final review of Wave 9 (PR #82) -- BLOCKED, not merged

Reviewed PR #82 (`fix/codeql-app-path-wave9`, head `f53a3e687eafc87d9681a180c1a8f06e01ff85c2`, then corrected at `<final head recorded in the PR>`) against all 11 live CodeQL alerts individually (`#556, #559, #561, #562, #565, #566, #567, #569, #571, #572, #573` -- the alert numbers moved between the original 22-alert cluster estimate above and the live PR-scoped set, but all trace to the same five functions).

- **Alert disposition** (traced individually, no bulk dismissal): all 11 sinks are `path.resolve()` / `mkdir()` / `write_text()` / `.exists()` / `.is_file()` calls in `_playlist_resolve_item_path`, `_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs`, `_playlist_atomic_json_replace`, `_create_playlist_outputs`, and `_playlist_delete_staged_track_file`, each already followed (in AGY's original diff) by a `relative_to()`-based containment check. CodeQL does not recognize this codebase's resolve-then-`relative_to()` pattern as a sanitizer (the same non-recognition already documented in the Wave 2/Wave 3 entries above), so all 11 are **false positives for the specific injection CodeQL flags** -- but tracing them independently surfaced four *different*, genuine issues CodeQL's `py/path-injection` rule was not designed to catch, listed below. None of the 11 are dismissed as "not applicable" without this trace; all are expected to auto-close once GitHub re-scans the corrected head (containment logic unchanged in shape, only the allowed-root lists and one fallback behavior changed).
- **Architecture gate -- FAILED, the primary blocking finding, same class as Wave 8's original finding above**: verified live, not just by reading the compose files, that `MUSIC_ROOT` (`/data/media/music`), `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`/`PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` (under `/data/torrents/music`), `PLAYLIST_DIR` (`/data/media/music/playlists`, nested under `MUSIC_ROOT` by default), and `PLAYLIST_JOB_STATE_DIR` (`/config/playlist_jobs`) do not exist as writable, mounted locations inside the `beets-web-manager` container in any of the three documented Compose topologies (`docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.full.yml`, `docker-compose.dev.yml` -- all three mount only `/web-manager-data` into `beets-web-manager` and set `read_only: true` at the container level). Built the exact-head web-manager image, ran it with the exact production constraints (`--read-only`, `/tmp` and `/run` as size-capped tmpfs, no `/data` or `/config` mount), and called the real production functions directly inside that container: `_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs()` and `_playlist_atomic_json_replace()` both raised `OSError: [Errno 30] Read-only file system: '/data'` immediately. This is the same pre-existing, already-documented architecture gap as Wave 8's original finding (not introduced by Wave 9, and not fixed by it either -- unlike Wave 8's later architecture-unblock, this diff does not route any playlist staging/manifest/M3U/job-state operation through `backend/beets_control_agent.py`/`backend/beets_client.py`; it only hardens path validation on operations that cannot reach the filesystem they validate against in the shipped topology).
- **Genuine finding #1 -- cross-playlist staged-track deletion (fixed)**: `POST /api/playlists/<name>/tracks/action` passes `payload.get("path")` directly as `requested_path` into `_playlist_delete_staged_track_file()`, which used it in preference to the server-owned manifest's own recorded path, and validated containment against *either* the playlist-specific staging root *or* the broad, shared `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` that every playlist's staging directory sits under. A `delete_staged` action against playlist A, given a `path` pointing into playlist B's staging subdirectory, passed containment and deleted playlist B's staged file. Fixed by scoping containment authorization to the calling playlist's own `get_playlist_staging_root()` subtree only, in both the pre-delete and the immediately-pre-`unlink()` re-validation. `requested_path` itself was left in place (removing it would be a larger API contract change than this review's narrow-correction scope, and the existing test suite and apparent frontend design already treat it as a legitimate parameter, not a legacy compatibility shim) -- the fix is the authorization boundary, not the parameter's existence. Regression test: `test_cross_playlist_staged_deletion_is_refused`.
- **Genuine finding #2 -- unauthorized absolute path gained basename authority (fixed)**: `_playlist_resolve_item_path()`'s fallback for an absolute path outside every allowed root was `MUSIC_ROOT / path.name` -- silently reinterpreting e.g. `/etc/passwd` or `/data/media/music2/song.mp3` as a new, different, plausible-looking in-library path instead of rejecting it. Empty input resolved to `MUSIC_ROOT` itself (the library root), which trivially satisfies `_path_is_under(root, root)` and existence checks, creating root-self operation risk in at least one caller (`_playlist_repair_track`'s file-move flow). A third, related gap found during this trace (not one of the 11 CodeQL sinks, but the same function, same class of bug): the *relative*-path fallback (`MUSIC_ROOT / raw`) was returned without verifying the resolved result actually stayed under `MUSIC_ROOT`, so a relative traversal string such as `../../../etc/shadow` would resolve outside the library the moment any caller called `.resolve()`/`.exists()` on it. All three fixed by introducing `_PLAYLIST_UNRESOLVED_PATH`, a fixed, guaranteed-nonexistent, guaranteed-outside-every-allowed-root sentinel `Path`, returned instead of fabricating a new path in all three cases. Traced all 11 call sites of `_playlist_resolve_item_path()`: every one already either checks `.exists()` or re-validates containment against `MUSIC_ROOT`/an allowed root before treating the result as authoritative, so every caller fails closed on the sentinel without needing any call-site changes or a return-type change (`Path`, not `Optional[Path]`, preserved). Regression tests: `test_unauthorized_absolute_path_does_not_gain_basename_authority`, `test_empty_path_does_not_resolve_to_library_root`, `test_relative_traversal_path_does_not_escape_music_root`, `test_relative_path_within_music_root_still_resolves_normally` (non-regression proof for the legitimate case).
- **Genuine finding #3 -- `_playlist_atomic_json_replace()`'s allowed-root list was both too broad and missing a real root (fixed)**: enumerated all 4 production call sites (staging manifest, desired-track manifest, playlist manifest replace, job-state checkpoint). None ever targets a path under `MUSIC_ROOT` or the system temp directory (`tempfile.gettempdir()`) -- both were accepted allowed roots with zero justifying caller; `MUSIC_ROOT` is Beets-engine-owned media, not web-manager state, and a process-wide `/tmp` is not an application-owned root. Both removed. Separately, `PLAYLIST_JOB_STATE_DIR` (the actual root `_playlist_save_job_state()` writes checkpoints under) was **absent** from the allowed-root list entirely -- meaning every real job-state checkpoint save raised `ValueError` in every environment, mounted or not; this is a genuine, independent functional bug (not a security widening) fixed by adding it. `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR` (a test-only injection point; not a real module-level name in `app.py`, confirmed by grep) was left in place since it is inert in production and used by the existing test-isolation pattern. Regression tests: `test_atomic_json_replace_no_longer_trusts_music_root_or_system_temp`, `test_atomic_json_replace_accepts_playlist_job_state_dir`.
- **Genuine finding #4 -- playlist-name sanitization collisions (confirmed, NOT fixed, documented instead)**: `_clean_playlist_name()` is the *only* identity used to derive the staging root, manifest path, and M3U path for a playlist -- and it collapses distinct raw names to the same sanitized string (e.g. `"A/B"`, `"A\B"`, `"A:B"`, and `"A?B"` all sanitize to `"A_B"`; two names differing only after character 120 collide after truncation). Two genuinely different playlists with colliding raw names would silently share the same staging directory, manifest file, and M3U output. The correct fix is a stable playlist-ID identity (or explicit collision refusal) independent of the sanitized display name -- a real design change to how playlists are keyed throughout this ~50k-line file, not a narrow patch, and explicitly out of this review's "narrow corrective commits" authority per this task's own scope boundary. Locked in as a known, demonstrated, unresolved defect via `test_playlist_name_sanitization_collision_is_a_known_unresolved_defect` rather than silently left untested.
- **Not independently re-traced to full rigor in this pass, no defect found but not claimed clean either**: `_plex_translate_beets_path`/`_plex_is_final_library_path`/`_plex_beets_path_candidates`'s full call graph (traced enough to confirm `_playlist_resolve_item_path`'s fix propagates correctly, not exhaustively beyond that); M3U entry path provenance beyond the primary beets-library-match path (`_create_playlist_outputs`'s `items` argument); the residual TOCTOU window in `_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs()`/`_playlist_atomic_json_replace()`/the staged-delete pre-`unlink()` re-validation between each function's final `resolve()`/`relative_to()` check and its actual `mkdir()`/`open()`/`unlink()` call -- narrower than before this review (an immediate re-`resolve(strict=True)` now directly precedes `unlink()`) but not eliminated, and not closed with `O_NOFOLLOW`/dir-fd-relative operations given the scope of this pass. None of these block this review's verdict, since the architecture gate above is already sufficient to block merge on its own, but they should be carried into whatever review picks this PR back up.
- Tests added this pass: 8, in `tests/test_sec002_wave9_playlist_plex_staging_paths.py`'s new `Wave9FinalReviewCorrectionTests` class (19 tests total in the file, up from 11). Two existing tests' fixtures (`tests/test_playlist_pipeline.py::test_staged_delete_refuses_library_copy`, `tests/test_sec002_wave9_playlist_plex_staging_paths.py::test_delete_staged_track_refuses_non_audio_file`) were corrected to place their staged-file fixtures under the calling playlist's own staging subdirectory (matching real production layout) rather than directly under the shared staging root, since the cross-playlist authorization narrowing above correctly refuses the old fixture placement.
- Verification: 19/19 Wave 9 tests (was 11), 44/44 combined with `test_playlist_pipeline`/`test_playlist_match_quality`, 3/3 full Linux suite runs (2066 tests, 0 failures, 93 skipped), 5/5 focused runs (253 tests: Wave 9, playlist pipeline, playlist match quality, `test_external_beets_architecture`, `test_sec002_app_stacktrace`), frontend typecheck/lint/test(23/23)/build/audit (0 vulnerabilities), static compilation, secret scan, compose security validation, all three Compose files parse with synthetic required values, endpoint inventory regenerated (240 routes, 0 `NEEDS_REVIEW`, pure line-number drift), exact-head web-manager Docker image built and used as the live architecture-gate proof above.
- **Final Claude decision: `blocked — Wave 9 violates engine ownership`.** The architecture gate is the primary, independently sufficient blocker (empirically proven, not asserted) and is pre-existing, not introduced by Wave 9 -- properly fixing it requires routing playlist staging/manifest/M3U/job-state operations through the control agent/`BeetsClient` boundary, a real architecture migration comparable in size to `reimport_disk()`'s Wave 8 unblock, and explicitly out of scope per this task's own "do not add the mount" / "do not rewrite Wave 8 architecture" / "do not perform repository-wide refactors" constraints. The playlist-name collision gap (finding #4) is a second, independent blocking-grade issue in its own right, also left unresolved for the same out-of-narrow-scope reason. The path-injection fixes, the cross-playlist deletion fix, and the allowed-root corrections (findings #1-#3) are real, verified, safe, and preserved regardless of the architecture verdict -- consistent with the Wave 8 precedent above. SEC-002 remains Open. Wave 10 not started.
- **Recommended Wave 10 cluster, in priority order**: (1) route playlist staging/manifest/M3U/job-state writes through `backend/beets_control_agent.py`/`backend/beets_client.py` the same way `_resolve_import_review_source_path()` and the config.yaml (BUG-1, v0.1.12) work already did -- this is the actual unblock for Wave 9's entire feature surface, not a narrow patch; (2) design and migrate a stable playlist-ID identity so `_clean_playlist_name()`'s sanitized display name stops being the sole on-disk identity anchor (finding #4); (3) the remaining, not-yet-independently-traced call graph noted above (`_plex_translate_beets_path` family beyond this pass's scope, M3U entry provenance, residual TOCTOU windows) once the architecture migration gives these functions a real filesystem to operate against, so any test written against them is testing production reality rather than a mock.
