- Remaining SEC-002 backlog: unchanged from before this wave at the repository level (188 total open `py/path-injection`-family alerts recorded at wave start; this wave's 11 PR-scoped alerts are expected to auto-close as false-positive-for-the-flagged-issue on re-scan of the corrected head, but the underlying architecture and identity-collision defects they surfaced remain open work, not closed by an alert auto-closing).

### Claude second final review of Wave 9 (PR #82) -- architecture continuation, still not merged

AGY/Codex added engine IPC scaffolding after the blocked verdict above (`BeetsClient.ensure_playlist_staging()`/`delete_playlist_staged_track()`/`export_playlist_m3u()`, corresponding `backend/beets_control_agent.py` endpoints). Independently re-traced the actual production call graph rather than trusting the new tests' own framing (the new tests exercise the `BeetsClient` methods directly against a mocked `_request`, not any real app.py caller). Findings and corrections:

- **`_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs()` still had a local fallback, and it was worse than "falls back"**: on any exception from `beets_client.ensure_playlist_staging()` it silently fell through to local `Path.mkdir()`/`os.chmod()` -- and that local fallback itself caught and swallowed `OSError`, so in the real "no `/data` mount, read-only root" topology it could return successfully having created nothing at all. **Fixed**: removed the fallback entirely; a failed or not-`ok` engine call now raises `PlaylistStagingUnavailableError`, and the one route caller (`POST /api/playlist/download`) returns a truthful `{"ok": false, "error": "staging_unavailable"}, 503` instead of either a fake success or an opaque 500. Regression tests: `test_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs_fails_truthfully_when_engine_unavailable`, `test_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs_fails_truthfully_when_engine_says_not_ok`.
- **`delete_playlist_staged_track()` and `export_playlist_m3u()` were never actually called from production code** -- grepped the whole repository; the only callers were the new test file's direct client-level tests. `_playlist_delete_staged_track_file()` still did `path.unlink()` locally; `_create_playlist_outputs()` still did `m3u.write_text()` locally. The scaffolding existed but nothing in `app.py` used it for either operation.
  - **Delete: fixed.** `_playlist_delete_staged_track_file()` now delegates the actual mutation to `beets_client.delete_playlist_staged_track()`; local containment/library/audio-extension checks remain as fast-fail, defense-in-depth pre-validation, but the unlink itself is engine-owned. Also fixed a second, independent gap while wiring this: `requested_path` (attacker-controlled, from `POST /api/playlists/<name>/tracks/action`'s raw JSON body) previously took precedence over the server-owned manifest's own recorded `staged_path`. It is now the reverse -- the manifest's recorded path is authoritative, and a supplied `requested_path` is accepted only if it canonically matches that recorded path, otherwise the call is rejected outright. Regression tests: `test_delete_staged_track_refuses_requested_path_mismatch`, `test_delete_staged_track_deletes_valid_staged_mp3`, `test_delete_staged_track_fails_truthfully_when_engine_unavailable`, plus the existing containment tests updated to establish manifest state first (they previously called the function with no manifest state at all, which the new state-authority precedence would otherwise reject before ever reaching the containment logic under test).
  - **M3U export: deliberately NOT wired this pass.** Traced the actual consumer: Plex playlist creation does not read the M3U file at all (it uses the Plex API directly via `_plex_find_music_section`/`_plex_create_audio_playlist`, matching by path). The M3U *is* still engine-owned in the sense that matters -- `PLAYLIST_DIR` is used pervasively (~12 call sites) as the authoritative on-disk playlist representation: listing (`PLAYLIST_DIR.glob("*.m3u")`), existence checks, deletion, and elsewhere -- not merely a UI download artifact. But `backend/beets_control_agent.py`'s `/playlists/export_m3u` names the file `{playlist_key}.m3u`, while every one of those other ~12 read/list/delete call sites in `app.py` expects `{name}.m3u`. Wiring only the write path through the engine would silently break every other consumer the moment a playlist is created via the engine path instead of locally. This is the same class of gap as finding #4 below (name-based on-disk identity) and requires the same larger identity migration -- not fixed here, left as local (broken-in-the-true-topology, but not newly broken by this pass) M3U write, and documented rather than half-migrated.
- **Engine-side cross-playlist delete containment: fixed.** `POST /playlists/staging/delete-track` accepted a target under `playlist_staging OR staging_root` -- since every playlist's directory is also under the shared `staging_root`, the `OR` made per-playlist containment meaningless, reintroducing the exact class of cross-playlist deletion bug the first final review fixed on the web-manager side only. Narrowed to `playlist_staging` containment alone, in both the pre-lock and post-lock (immediately pre-`unlink()`) re-validation. `track_id` remains unused for authorization at the engine layer by design -- the engine has no manifest/track-state access to check it against; the actual fix for "requested_path as sole authority" lives at the web-manager layer (above), which now resolves the server-owned path before ever calling the engine. Regression tests: `ControlAgentDeleteTrackEndpointTests` (real `do_POST` invocation, not mocked) in `tests/test_sec002_wave9_playlist_plex_staging_paths.py`.
- **Stable playlist identity: reviewed, not further changed this pass.** The legacy fallback (`pl-legacy-{sha256(name.casefold())[:12]}`) is deterministic per input and already fixes the sanitization-/truncation-collision defects (finding #4's `_clean_playlist_name()` collisions do not propagate into this hash, since it hashes the raw name before cleaning). It does not, however, survive a rename, because identity is still derived from name rather than persisted independently. Did not build a persisted-UUID store to fix this: `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR` is not a real path anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by grep, same finding as the first review), so persisting identity durably would mean designing new state-storage infrastructure -- a real architecture addition, not a narrow fix. Also confirmed there is currently no playlist-rename endpoint anywhere in `app.py`, so this is forward-looking hardening for a feature that does not exist yet, not an active production bug. **Required before any rename feature ships**: a real persisted playlist-ID store independent of `_clean_playlist_name()`'s output, written once and read back on every subsequent lookup.
- Tests: 2 new staging-fallback tests, 3 new delete-flow tests (mismatch rejection, engine-wired success, engine-unavailable), 2 new real end-to-end `ControlAgentHandler.do_POST` tests for the engine delete endpoint, plus 5 existing tests updated to establish manifest state before calling the now-state-authoritative delete function. `tests/test_playlist_pipeline.py::test_staged_delete_refuses_library_copy` updated similarly (added a fake `beets_client` and split the library-file scenario onto its own manifest-state row, since `requested_path` alone no longer determines the delete target).
- Not re-opened, not claimed newly fixed: the M3U engine-ownership migration and the stable-ID persistence store are both still open, same as the first review's verdict -- this pass closed the *silent-fallback* and *authorization* gaps in staging-ensure and staged-delete specifically, not the remaining feature-surface architecture debt.
- SEC-002 remains Open. Wave 10 not started.

### Wave 10: Persistent Playlist Identity + Complete M3U Lifecycle (PR #83) -- Claude second final review

AGY implemented exactly the two items this doc's Wave 9 entry above flagged as required: a real persisted `pl_<uuid4 hex>` identity (`index.json` under `PLAYLIST_STATE_ROOT`, fail-closed on corruption/duplicate-provider/mismatched-id), and a full engine-owned M3U lifecycle (`/playlists/m3u/{read,delete,list}` alongside the existing `export_m3u`), replacing every local `PLAYLIST_DIR` read/write/glob in `app.py` with `beets_client` IPC calls. Independently re-verified the architecture (read paths use `_playlist_existing_key()`/`allocate=False` throughout -- confirmed no read/list/detail route allocates an ID) and found several genuine gaps beyond AGY's own 16 flagged CodeQL alerts:

- **Plex same-name deletion was unsafe (the review's hard gate)**: Wave 10 explicitly allows same-name app playlists (each `playlist_create()` call mints a fresh `pl_<uuid>` regardless of name collisions), but both `playlist_delete()` and the replace-before-sync path in `_create_playlist_outputs()` still called `_plex_delete_playlist_by_title()`, which deletes *every* Plex playlist matching that title -- guaranteed collateral damage the moment two same-named app playlists both sync to Plex. `_plex_create_audio_playlist()` already computed a stable Plex `ratingKey` at creation time but discarded it instead of returning it, which is what forced every deletion path back onto title matching. Fixed: the ratingKey is now returned and persisted into `manifest.last_plex.rating_key`; `_plex_delete_playlist_by_rating_key()` deletes precisely one playlist by it. When no stored ratingKey exists (legacy manifest) and another *live* app playlist shares the display name, Plex delete/replace now fails closed (skips, reports `ambiguous Plex playlist title`) instead of guessing; falls back to legacy title-based deletion only when genuinely unambiguous.
- **Internal path disclosure**: `PlaylistStateError`'s message could embed a concrete container path (`f"Playlist state directory is unavailable: {d}"`), and three routes (`playlist_create`, `playlist_delete` x2) returned `str(exc)` directly in the JSON error body -- this is exactly what CodeQL alerts #608-#610 (`py/stack-trace-exposure`) flagged, and unlike most alerts on this PR it was a genuine finding, not a false positive. Fixed: the raise site no longer embeds the path (logged server-side via `app.logger.warning` instead); all three client-facing sites now go through `_playlist_state_error_payload()`/`_playlist_state_error_status()`, a fixed `code -> safe message` mapping. All three alerts auto-closed (`state: "fixed"`) on CodeQL's rescan of the corrected head, confirming this wasn't just a line-number shift.
- **Manifest corruption silently treated as "doesn't exist"**: `_playlist_read_manifest()`'s `except Exception: return {}` meant a manifest that exists but fails to parse was indistinguishable from one never created -- `_playlist_write_manifest()`'s `previous = _playlist_read_manifest(...)` would then silently synthesize a brand-new manifest over the corrupt one, discarding whatever track/import/Plex-sync state it still held. Fixed narrowly: added `raise_on_corrupt=True` (used only by the write path), which raises `PlaylistStateError("manifest_corrupt", ...)` instead of swallowing the parse failure; every other (read-only/display) caller keeps the lenient default so one corrupt playlist doesn't 500 an entire list/detail page.
- **Orphaned index entries on delete**: `playlist_delete()` never removed the playlist's identity record from `index.json`, so delete-then-recreate of a same-named playlist could leave a dead orphan entry sharing the display name with the new live one -- risking a false `ambiguous_playlist` error or misattributed state on a later lookup. Fixed: the index entry is retired once the route's one hard-required step (engine M3U delete) has succeeded.
- **M3U write tempfile safety**: `tmp_m3u.write_text(content, ...)` used a plain `'w'`-mode open, which follows a pre-existing symlink at the predicted (8-hex-char) tempfile name instead of refusing it. Fixed: `_create_exclusive_temp_file()` uses `os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY` (plus `O_NOFOLLOW` where available), with a small retry loop on random-suffix collision.
- **M3U read TOCTOU / growth-race**: the symlink check and size bound were both validated *before* the lock was acquired (a filesystem object validated pre-lock can still be swapped before the lock is actually held), and the size bound was a `stat()` taken before an unbounded `read_text()` -- a file can grow between the two. Fixed: path re-resolution, the symlink check, and the read itself all now happen under the lock; the byte bound is enforced by `_bounded_read_text()` (reads at most `max_bytes + 1` and rejects if exceeded), not a stale stat. Delete's already-lock-protected path was also re-resolved under the lock for consistency with the other two operations.
- **M3U export cross-playlist staging entry**: an item `path` under a *different* playlist's own `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` subtree was silently authorized into this playlist's M3U merely because both satisfied the broad `"staging"` role check in `resolve_safe_path()` -- the same class of gap Wave 9 already closed for staged-track *deletion*, just not yet for M3U *export*. Fixed: entries resolving under `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` are now additionally required to resolve under this specific `playlist_key`'s own subtree.
- **Verified, not changed**: legacy M3U entry authorization (a malicious/arbitrary line in an old M3U cannot become an authorized media item -- every consumer in `app.py` either matches it against the trusted Beets library index (`_playlist_item_from_path`/`_playlist_item_from_text`, returns `None`/falls through to text-matching on no match, never trusts the raw path) or re-validates it through the already-hardened `_playlist_resolve_item_path()`/`_plex_is_final_library_path()`).
- CodeQL: AGY's 16 flagged alerts plus 2 introduced by this pass's own new code (18 total `py/path-injection` on the corrected head) individually traced and dismissed as false positives -- all downstream of `_playlist_m3u_path_for_key()`/`_playlist_m3u_target()`'s resolve()-then-containment(-then-symlink) validation, `_playlist_manifest_path()`/`_playlist_key()`'s ID validation, or values that never reach a filesystem call at all (pure `Path` containment comparisons). The 3 `py/stack-trace-exposure` alerts (#608-#610) were genuine and fixed, not dismissed -- see above.
- Tests: 14 new (Plex same-name safety x5, path-disclosure x2, manifest-corruption x2, index-cleanup x1, M3U tempfile/TOCTOU/bounds/cross-playlist-entry x4), 2 pre-existing source-inspection tests updated for the `_plex_replace_playlist_safely` call-site change. Full suite: 2106 tests, 0 failures, 98 skipped (Linux CI covers the POSIX-only symlink/TOCTOU tests that skip on non-POSIX).
- SEC-002 remains Open. Wave 11 not started. Recommended next: (1) route `PLAYLIST_JOB_STATE_DIR` job-state checkpoints through engine ownership the same way M3U now is (still local `app.py` filesystem writes); (2) revisit whether `_plex_playlist_rating_keys_by_title()` (used only for post-sync verification counts, not mutation) should also move to ratingKey-based lookup once one is reliably stored, since a same-name collision could currently misreport a verification count without misdirecting any actual mutation.
- **Merge result**: PR #83 approved and squash-merged. Reviewed head `4d56b5e8a17c3cb2d92b83db309a776b7bfed83d` on branch `fix/codeql-app-path-wave10` (preserved, not deleted). Squash SHA `50a28a3a4d958cd4e1b836d55d57ac8ffe021316`, now `main`. Post-merge CI green (lint, typecheck, node-build, unit-tests, security, docker-build, CodeQL). Post-merge default-branch CodeQL inventory: 179 total open alerts repo-wide, 128 `py/path-injection` in `app.py` -- both pre-existing backlog carried forward from before this wave, 0 unresolved alerts scoped to this PR. No production deployment, no release published, `v0.1.13` unmoved, Wave 11 not started.

### Wave 11: Playlist Job-State Authority + Plex Stable Identity (PR #84) -- Claude second final review, BLOCKED, not merged

AGY implemented persistent-identity-keyed checkpoints (`playlist_id`/`playlist_key`/`pl-<20 hex>` job IDs, corruption/symlink-hardened save/delete, secret redaction) and moved staged-file *inspection* to a new engine endpoint (`POST /playlists/staging/inspect-track` via `BeetsClient.inspect_playlist_staged_track()`), plus fixed the Wave 10-era same-name Plex delete gap more thoroughly (`_plex_delete_playlist_by_title_unambiguous()` now fails closed on >1 Plex-side match, not just >1 app-side match). Independently re-traced the full resume->import call graph rather than trusting AGY's own tests or the reported empty CodeQL inventory, and found:

- **Fixed -- Plex stale ratingKey could silently rebind to a different playlist's identity**: `_plex_playlist_rating_keys_by_key_or_title()` took a supplied `rating_key`, and if it no longer resolved in Plex (deleted out-of-band), fell through to a same-title lookup -- if exactly one *other*, unrelated Plex playlist happened to share the title, its ratingKey was returned and persisted into `manifest.last_plex.rating_key` as if it were this playlist's own. This is the exact scenario the review brief predicted as the primary hard gate. Fixed: the function now distinguishes "stale" (supplied key didn't resolve) from "no key supplied" -- a stale key never falls through to title matching; both `_create_playlist_outputs()` call sites now report `stale_plex_identity`/`review_required` instead of silently adopting the wrong key. Regression tests: `test_stale_rating_key_does_not_silently_rebind_by_title`, `test_stale_rating_key_verification_does_not_persist_wrong_key`.
- **Fixed -- cross-playlist checkpoint-corruption denial of service**: `_playlist_saved_job_states_for_name(..., strict=True)` scanned every `pl-*.json` file and raised on the *first* corrupt/malformed one encountered, regardless of which playlist it belonged to -- a corrupt checkpoint for playlist B could fail a strict-mode resume of an unrelated, perfectly valid playlist A. Fixed: ownership matching against the caller's target now happens before strict format validation; a checkpoint that doesn't belong to the target is skipped regardless of what's wrong with it. An unparseable file with genuinely unknowable ownership no longer hard-fails a target that has its own valid checkpoint elsewhere, but strict mode still raises if the target has *no* discoverable checkpoint and an unattributed corrupt file exists (it might have been the one being looked for) -- nothing is silently deleted or guessed at either way. Regression tests: `test_unrelated_corrupt_checkpoint_does_not_block_strict_resume_of_another_playlist` (new), `test_corrupt_checkpoint_json_fails_safely_for_strict_load` (AGY's existing test, still passes under the new logic).
- **Fixed -- checkpoint reads didn't check for symlinks**: `_playlist_job_state_path_for_delete()`/the delete path were already hardened against a symlinked checkpoint leaf, but `_playlist_load_job_state()` and the glob-based lister still did a plain `Path.read_text()`, which follows a symlink. Added `_playlist_job_state_safe_read_text()` (refuses a symlink leaf) and used it in both read paths. Regression tests: `test_job_state_load_refuses_symlinked_checkpoint`, `test_job_states_for_name_skips_symlinked_checkpoint`.
- **Verified, no fix needed**: staging inspect endpoint authentication (shared `_authenticate()` gate at the top of `do_POST()`, same as every other endpoint), cross-playlist staging inspection (existing `test_inspect_authorizes_only_calling_playlist_subtree` confirms `outside_playlist_staging` refusal), `track_id` is informational-only at the engine layer by design (same split-authority pattern already accepted for Wave 9's delete endpoint -- the engine has no manifest access to verify it against).

#### BLOCKING FINDING -- complete resume-to-import pipeline still violates engine ownership (not fixed this pass, PR held)

Wave 11 correctly moved staged-file *inspection* to the engine (`/playlists/staging/inspect-track`), but the actual import mutation immediately downstream, `_playlist_run_import_downloaded()`, was traced in full and still does, directly in the web-manager process:

- `batch_dir = _playlist_imports_dir(...)` then `batch_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` -- local mkdir under `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` (engine-owned, `/data/torrents/music/...`, not mounted into web-manager).
- `shutil.move(str(source_path), str(destination))` -- the actual staged audio file move, requiring local read/write access to engine-owned paths.
- `_enrich_playlist_file_tags()` -> `_read_file_media_tags()` has a **local fallback** to `beets.mediafile.MediaFile(p_str)` if the engine's `beets_client.read_tags()` IPC call returns empty/fails -- reachable in production (e.g. right after a move, before the engine has indexed the file), and requires both a local Beets media-tag library and local file read access, both explicitly disallowed by the architecture.

This is **pre-existing**, not introduced or worsened by Wave 11 (the diff only threaded `playlist_id` through `_playlist_imports_dir()`'s existing signature; the `mkdir`/`shutil.move`/tag-read-fallback lines themselves are unchanged). In the real, supported two-service topology (web-manager: read-only root, `/web-manager-data` only, no `/data` mount), every one of these calls would raise `OSError`/`FileNotFoundError` immediately. The `POST /playlists/staging/inspect-track` endpoint that Wave 11 *does* correctly engine-own only gates which staged entries are considered candidates for import (`_playlist_staged_entries()`) -- it never becomes the actual mutation authority, and nothing revalidates engine-side immediately before the local move happens.

**Required fix (not attempted this pass -- Wave 12-scale, not a narrow correction)**: a new engine-side import-handoff endpoint (e.g. `POST /playlists/import-staged`, or extending an existing Wave 8 import/reimport endpoint if its semantics fit) that receives logical identity (`playlist_key`/track IDs) and owns source resolution, staging validation, the batch/import directory, the move, tag enrichment, and the `beet import` invocation entirely engine-side. The web manager must stop calling `Path.mkdir()`/`shutil.move()`/a local `MediaFile` fallback against `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` paths.

**Final Claude decision: `blocked -- complete playlist import still violates engine ownership`.** The three fixes above (stale Plex ratingKey, checkpoint corruption isolation, checkpoint read symlink safety) are real, verified, safe, and preserved in the branch regardless of the architecture verdict. PR #84 held in draft, not marked ready, not merged. SEC-002 remains Open. Do not start a broader Wave 12 rewrite while resolving this -- the required fix above is narrowly scoped to the actual import mutation path.
- Tests this pass: 6 new (2 stale-ratingKey, 1 cross-playlist checkpoint corruption, 2 checkpoint-read symlink safety, plus AGY's existing suite unmodified except the one test whose expected corruption-fail-closed condition was re-verified under the corrected logic). Full suite: 2131 tests, 0 failures, 100 skipped.
- **Recommended Wave 12** (blocked on nothing else, ready to start once resourced): the engine import-handoff endpoint above is the single remaining piece for genuine end-to-end playlist-acquisition engine ownership; everything else in the playlist subsystem (identity, M3U lifecycle, staging ensure/delete/inspect, checkpoint state, Plex identity) is now engine-owned or web-manager-state-owned as appropriate.

### Wave 12: Engine Import-Handoff (PR #84 continuation) -- Claude second final review

AGY implemented the Wave 11 recommendation: a new engine endpoint (`POST /playlists/import-staged` via `BeetsClient.import_playlist_staged()`) that owns source resolution, staging containment, an idempotent per-operation staging directory (`.playlist-import-operations/index.json` under `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT`, symlink-checked, atomically written with fsync), tag enrichment, and the actual `beet import` invocation entirely engine-side -- replacing `_playlist_run_import_downloaded()`'s old local `mkdir`/`shutil.move`. This closes the Wave 11 blocking finding for the import step itself. Independently re-traced the new endpoint's full logic (not just AGY's own tests) and found:

- **CRITICAL, FIXED -- audio identity verification was circular and bypassable.** `_playlist_import_identity_ok()` only checked for *conflicting* tags between the staged file and the expected track. Completely untagged audio (or audio with tags forged to match) had nothing to conflict with, so it fell through to `return True, "verified" if tags else "metadata_unavailable", {...}` -- accepted with **zero** deterministic verification. Worse, the accepted-but-unverified file then had the *expected* `mb_trackid` written onto it by the engine itself, was imported, and "post-import verification" (`_verify_imported_track_in_library`) queried the Beets DB for that exact same self-written MBID -- proving only that the tag write succeeded, nothing about actual audio content. This is a genuine data-integrity vulnerability: arbitrary audio could be imported into the user's library mislabeled as a specific, different, expected track, with the system reporting it as "verified."

  **Fixed**: ported real AcoustID/`fpcalc` fingerprint verification into the engine (`_engine_acoustid_lookup()`, mirroring `helpers_mb.py`'s existing `_acoustid_lookup()` -- same rate limiting, same API shape -- since this module is a standalone engine script with no app.py/Flask coupling and can't import app.py's version directly). `_playlist_import_identity_ok()` now: (1) still fast-fails on an explicit tag *conflict* (cheap, catches obvious tampering before spending an AcoustID call), (2) requires `fpcalc` to be available -- fails closed (`identity_verification_unavailable`) if not, (3) requires a successful AcoustID lookup on the *original, untouched* staged source -- fails closed if the lookup itself errors, and fails closed (`review_required`... surfaced as `identity_verification_unavailable`/`review_required` status codes, never silently accepted) if it returns no candidates, (4) requires the returned candidates to actually confirm the expected track (by MBID, or by title+artist text similarity against the *fingerprint-returned* candidate metadata, never against attacker-controlled tags) before authorizing. Because this all runs before any tag is written or the file is moved, the evidence is never self-fulfilling -- the engine cannot verify a value it wrote itself.

  Regression tests (in `tests/test_sec002_wave12_engine_import_handoff.py`): `test_untagged_wrong_audio_is_not_authorized_without_fingerprint_match`, `test_forged_matching_tags_do_not_bypass_fingerprint_verification` (wrong audio deliberately tagged with the exact expected artist/title/mb_trackid, rejected once fingerprint evidence disagrees), `test_acoustid_unavailable_requires_review_not_silent_accept`, `test_acoustid_lookup_error_requires_review_not_silent_accept`, `test_correct_audio_with_genuine_fingerprint_match_is_authorized` (positive case), `test_post_import_verification_is_not_circular` (proves the fingerprint lookup runs exactly once, on the original file, before any mutation).

- **Confirmed, not fixed -- "complete playlist import is engine-owned" would still be overclaiming.** Traced the full pipeline (discover -> download/acquire -> stage -> resume -> inspect -> identity-verify -> import -> placement -> cleanup -> Plex sync), not just the new endpoint:
  - **Post-import placement**: `_playlist_place_recent_imports_for_tracks()` (called after every successful playlist import) delegates to `_playlist_repair_quality_candidate()`, which does a **local** `old_path.exists()` / `_path_is_under(..., MUSIC_ROOT)` check directly in `app.py` before ever reaching the engine-IPC `_beet_run(["write"/"move"...])` calls that do the actual move. In the real no-`/data`-mount topology this local check can never succeed, so the placement/reconciliation step is silently non-functional end-to-end for real playlist imports -- every candidate is reported "file missing" even though the corresponding Beets item is real. This is pre-existing, shared "quality cleanup" infrastructure (not modified by this PR, used by multiple non-playlist callers too), not something safe to narrowly patch in this pass without risking those other callers.
  - **Download/acquisition**: `_playlist_download_missing_tracks()` still does local `dl_dir.mkdir()` and `_playlist_copy_source_files()`/`_playlist_slskd_download_track()` write directly into `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` (engine-owned) from the web-manager process. Pre-existing, not touched by Wave 11 or 12 -- the same debt class as the old local-import path Wave 12 just fixed, just one pipeline stage earlier.
  - Both are confirmed via direct code reads, not assumed. Neither is a security vulnerability the way the circular identity check was (no wrong-content risk, no injection) -- they are architecture-completeness gaps: two of the ten pipeline stages still can't function in the supported topology.
- **Minor, not fixed -- crash-after-Beet-success-before-state-save.** If the engine process crashes after `beet import --move` has already moved a file into the library but before the following `_save_operation_state()` call persists `"imported"`, a retry finds the source gone (Beets already moved it) and falls through to a missing/failed status rather than first reconciling against `_verify_imported_track_in_library()`. Does **not** cause a duplicate import (the core idempotency safety property holds -- there's nothing left at the old path to re-import), but a track that actually succeeded is truthfully reported as failed, which could prompt an unnecessary re-download. Narrow, timing-dependent, UX-accuracy issue rather than a security or data-integrity one; not fixed this pass.
- **Verified, no fix needed**: operation-index symlink/corruption/storage-key handling (root and leaf symlink checks, atomic write with `fsync` + directory `fsync`, cross-operation storage-key collision refused, playlist_key/playlist_id binding mismatch refused), auth on both new endpoints (shared `_authenticate()` gate), request bounds (byte/track-count limits), command safety (list-form subprocess args, only a server-generated operation directory as source), error/log sanitization (stable `error_code`s, no raw stdout/stderr/tracebacks in responses).
- CodeQL: independently re-verified against the corrected head, not just trusted AGY's reported empty list (see below).
- Tests: 6 new (identity-verification adversarial suite above), plus 4 existing Wave 12 tests updated to mock `_playlist_import_identity_ok` directly (they test idempotency/retry/operation-scoping, not identity verification, and previously passed only because no `fpcalc` conflict existed in the un-mocked path -- now that a real fingerprint lookup is required, they need to bypass that check explicitly to stay focused on what they actually test). Full suite: 2152 tests, 0 failures, 121 skipped.
- SEC-002 remains Open. **Final Claude decision for this pass: the circular identity-verification vulnerability (the acute, severe finding) is fixed and verified. The broader "complete playlist import is engine-owned" claim remains not fully true** (post-import placement and download/acquisition still touch local engine paths, both pre-existing and out of this narrow pass's scope) -- see PR #84 body for the current merge decision.

### Wave 13: Complete Playlist Pipeline Engine Ownership & Related Debt Sweep (PR #86) -- combined fix + final review, no separate Codex Tier 2 pass

Task scope explicitly merged the Tier-2 corrective-implementation role into this final review: fix every remaining defect directly, not just document it. AGY's Wave 13 diff closed the two Wave 12 completeness gaps in principle -- new engine endpoints `POST /playlists/staging/list-files`, `/playlists/staging/validate-track`, `/playlists/quality-candidates`, `/playlists/place-imported` (via new `BeetsClient` methods) so post-import placement and download/acquisition staging queries no longer *need* local `/data` access -- but independent re-tracing of the full diff (not just AGY's own tests, not trusting the reported CodeQL count) found the implementation itself carried the corrected shape without the substance: several of the "engine-owned" call sites still fell back to local filesystem/fingerprint access on IPC failure, two severed identity keys were silently interchanged, one endpoint had no authorization relationship to the playlist it claimed to operate on at all, and two crash-level regressions (undefined name, deleted-but-still-called function) meant the placement/manual-repair code paths could not execute at all as committed. Fixed all of the following directly in this pass:

- **CRITICAL, FIXED -- `/playlists/place-imported` had no authorization binding between `item_id` and `playlist_key`.** The endpoint accepted both in the request body but only validated `item_id > 0` before running `SELECT * FROM items WHERE id = ?` and, for the non-`move_singleton` action, an unconditional `UPDATE items SET artist=...,mb_releasegroupid=...,... WHERE id=?` followed by `beet write`/`beet move` -- any caller able to reach the engine's authenticated HTTP API with an arbitrary `item_id` could rewrite metadata and relocate the file for *any* item in the library, not just one that actually belonged to the named playlist's import. Fixed by reusing the existing Wave 12 operation-state infrastructure rather than inventing a new tracking mechanism: `_verify_imported_track_in_library()` (called during `/playlists/import-staged`) now resolves and persists the real `item_id` onto each track's saved operation-state entry, and a new `_playlist_item_belongs_to_playlist(playlist_key, item_id)` scans that playlist's own recorded operations for a track entry with matching `item_id` and `status in {"imported","already_imported"}` before permitting any mutation; `playlist_key` format is also now validated (`_valid_playlist_key()`) at entry, matching the other three endpoints, which it previously skipped entirely. Regression tests: `test_item_belongs_to_playlist_true_when_recorded`, `_false_for_unrelated_item`, `_false_for_different_playlist`, `_false_when_no_state_exists`, `_rejects_invalid_key_format`, `_ignores_non_imported_status` (POSIX-gated, `resolve_safe_path` requirement).
- **FIXED -- placement mutation had no backup and could report success on a failed tag write.** The old web-manager-side `_playlist_apply_album_placement()` used to `shutil.copy2(LIB_PATH, backup)` before mutating; that guarantee was dropped when the mutation moved into the engine and was never rebuilt there. Separately, `repaired = proc_m.returncode < 2 and bool(new_path_text)` ignored the preceding `beet write` step's return code entirely -- a failed tag write with a successful move still reported `repaired: true`, leaving stale/incorrect tags on a relocated file with no indication anything was wrong. Fixed: the engine now backs up `LIB_PATH` immediately before the mutation and refuses (500) if the backup itself fails; `repaired`/`moved` now require both the write and the move to have succeeded (`proc_w.returncode < 2 and proc_m.returncode < 2 and bool(new_path_text)`), with a `reason` field naming which step failed.
- **FIXED -- `playlist_id`/`playlist_key` confusion and a shared `"pl_default"` fallback, present in six call sites.** Every acquisition/placement helper that needed a playlist's filesystem key used the same defective pattern -- treat a raw `playlist_id` as if it were already a derived `playlist_key` when present, else look the key up by *name alone* (ignoring `playlist_id`), else fall back to a literal `"pl_default"` shared across every playlist that hit either failure mode. Two distinct playlists with a matching cleaned display name (or any playlist whose id/key simply couldn't be resolved) could silently collide on staged-file deletion, staged-file reuse, or reused-download validation. Fixed with one shared strict resolver, `_playlist_resolve_operation_key(playlist_key, playlist_id, clean_name, log=...)`: a supplied `playlist_key` must already be validly formatted to be trusted as-is; a supplied `playlist_id` must match the real internal-id format (`pl_[0-9a-f]{32}`) and is *derived* into a key via `_playlist_existing_key(name, playlist_id=...)`, never used directly; anything else returns `""` and every caller now refuses the operation on empty string rather than guessing. Applied in `_playlist_validate_downloaded_files`, `_playlist_reusable_download_files`, `_playlist_validate_staged_download`, `_playlist_apply_album_placement`, `_playlist_move_singleton_candidate`. `app.py` also newly defines `_valid_playlist_key()` (mirroring the engine's own format check) since three call sites referenced it without it ever being defined in this module -- see crash-level regressions below.
- **FIXED -- three local IPC-failure fallbacks that violated engine ownership.** `_playlist_reusable_download_files()` fell back to `_audio_files_in_dir(str(job_dir))` (direct local directory listing of engine-owned staging) whenever the engine's `list_playlist_staged_files` call raised or returned non-ok; `_get_staged_paths()` inside `_playlist_download_missing_tracks()` did the same against `dl_dir`; `_playlist_validate_staged_download()` fell back to local `_playlist_download_text_candidates()` + `_audio_identity_decision()` (local AcoustID fingerprinting) + `_playlist_download_audio_allowed()` (local format inspection) on the same failure condition -- reintroducing exactly the "engine unreachable -> silently do it locally instead" pattern Wave 12 eliminated from the import path, on three call sites Wave 13 itself added. All three now fail closed: an IPC failure logs a warning and returns an empty/`ok: False` result (review-required semantics for the validation path) instead of touching the filesystem or running AcoustID locally. `_playlist_ensure_staging_dirs()` failure inside `_playlist_download_missing_tracks()` was also silently caught and logged as "delegated to engine" before proceeding anyway; the call is no longer wrapped, so an unconfirmed staging directory now fails the whole download round instead of proceeding into an unverified location.
- **FIXED -- `_playlist_copy_source_files()` reported a failed copy as a successful one.** On `shutil.copy2` raising, the function appended the original (uncopied) *source* path to its `copied` result list instead of omitting the entry -- callers treat every returned path as already staged for this playlist, so a copy failure could hand a downloader's raw source path (outside playlist staging) into validation/import as if it were a legitimate staged file. Fixed: a failed copy is now simply omitted from the result, with a log warning naming the failure; no fabricated path is returned. (The engine-side `/playlists/staging/validate-track` containment check would have refused such a path anyway -- `relative_parts[0] != playlist_key` -- but the web-manager-side function should not manufacture a false-success result regardless of that downstream defense.)
- **FIXED -- fabricated quality-candidacy in the manual-placement job.** `_playlist_place_quality_candidate_job()`'s inner job function re-fetches quality candidates (defense-in-depth against a race with the API route's own candidate check) and, when the target `item_id` was no longer found among them, changed from the original fail-closed `raise RuntimeError("... is not a playlist review/repair candidate")` to fabricating `candidate = {"id": item_id, "recommended_action": "repair"}` and proceeding anyway -- letting an arbitrary `item_id` reach placement the moment the race condition it was meant to guard against actually occurred. Restored the fail-closed raise.
- **FIXED -- `_playlist_quality_cleanup_candidates()` silently reported "0 candidates" on engine failure.** `except Exception: return []` is indistinguishable from "the engine was queried and the library is clean" to every caller, including the `/api/playlists/quality-cleanup` scan route and the `/api/playlists/quality-place` manual-placement route -- an operator could conclude a library-wide quality scan came back clean when it never actually ran. Fixed: the function now raises a new `PlaylistQualityCandidatesUnavailableError` on any IPC failure or non-ok engine response; both HTTP routes catch it and return `502` with the real error instead of a fabricated empty/clean result; the one internal caller for which failing the whole calling job would be disproportionate (`_playlist_place_recent_imports_for_tracks()`, called after an import that has *already* succeeded, purely for best-effort follow-up album placement) catches it locally, logs, and returns its normal empty-summary shape.
- **FIXED -- two crash-level regressions that made the entire placement/manual-repair path non-functional as committed.** (1) `app.py` called `_valid_playlist_key()` at three sites but never defined or imported it anywhere in the module -- every call to `_playlist_apply_album_placement()` or `_playlist_move_singleton_candidate()` would raise `NameError` at runtime. (2) `/api/playlists/quality-place` calls `_playlist_manual_placement_from_payload()`, which the diff deleted from `app.py` entirely without removing or updating its one caller -- every manual-placement request would raise `NameError`. Fixed: added `_valid_playlist_key()` to `app.py` (mirrors the engine's format check); restored `_playlist_manual_placement_from_payload()` (required-field + MusicBrainz-UUID validation, unchanged from its prior form) immediately before `_playlist_place_quality_candidate_job()`.
- **FIXED -- a duplicated `_playlist_apply_album_placement()` definition, plus a duplicated four-function helper block, silently shadowed dead code.** The diff introduced a new, engine-delegating `_playlist_apply_album_placement()` earlier in the file without removing the original (whose body it also edited in place further down) -- Python resolves a re-defined module-level name to whichever `def` executes last, so the second copy was live and the first (and every fix applied only to it, before this was caught) was dead. The same pattern also duplicated `_playlist_resolve_albumartist_info_for_release_group()`, `_playlist_resolve_albumartist_for_release_group()`, `_playlist_expected_album_path_hint()`, and `_playlist_validate_final_album_path()` verbatim (byte-identical, confirmed programmatically before removal). All duplicates removed, one canonical copy of each kept. An AST-based scan of `app.py` for duplicate top-level function names confirms none of these four remain duplicated; found two unrelated, pre-existing duplicates (`_s()`, `_album_cleanup_valid_rgid()`) that predate this PR and are outside its playlist-pipeline scope -- `_s()`'s later definition is a strict behavioral superset (adds bytes-decoding) so this is dead code, not a live bug, and `_album_cleanup_valid_rgid()`'s two copies are identical; left both for a future, unrelated cleanup pass rather than expanding this PR's scope.
- **FIXED -- engine `/playlists/staging/list-files` had no lock, an overbroad/redundant search scope, and an unbounded result list.** Unlike the other three new endpoints, it never called `acquire_os_lock()`; it walked both `safe_playlist_staging / "downloads"` *and* `safe_playlist_staging` itself (the latter redundantly re-walks the former and additionally surfaces files under `imports/` and the operation-state index, outside this endpoint's actual "pre-import staged downloads" purpose); and `files` had no size bound, so a staging directory with a very large number of entries could produce an unbounded response. Fixed: takes a read-only OS lock for the duration of the walk; scope narrowed to `downloads/` only; capped at `PLAYLIST_STAGING_LIST_FILES_MAX` (env-configurable, default 500, clamped [1, 5000]) with a `truncated` flag in the response when the cap is hit.
- **FIXED -- hardcoded, inconsistent "too-short/preview" quality threshold.** `_engine_playlist_quality_cleanup_candidates()` hardcoded `threshold = 180` (seconds) for flagging suspiciously-short "preview clip" items, independent of and inconsistent with the web-manager's own download-acceptance gate (`PLAYLIST_MIN_DOWNLOAD_SECONDS`, default 45) -- a track between 46-179s could be accepted on download and then immediately flagged as a preview-quality problem by the very next quality scan. Fixed: now reads the same `PLAYLIST_MIN_DOWNLOAD_SECONDS` env var (default 45, clamped [0, 3600]) as `PLAYLIST_QUALITY_PREVIEW_THRESHOLD_SECONDS`.
- **FIXED -- raw exception text disclosed to API callers.** `/playlists/place-imported` and `/playlists/quality-candidates` both returned `f"...: {exc}"` in their 500 error bodies. Replaced with the same static-message convention used by every other 500 response in this file.
- **Verified, no fix needed**: `/playlists/staging/validate-track` (the fourth new endpoint) was already well-contained on first read and remains so -- `resolve_safe_path(..., require_exists=True, expected_type="file")`, explicit music-root exclusion, `relative_parts[0] != playlist_key` containment, symlink check, and delegation to the existing, Wave-12-hardened `_playlist_import_identity_ok()` for fingerprint verification; no local fallback exists here to remove. `BeetsClient` wrapper methods (`list_playlist_staged_files`, `validate_playlist_staged_track`, `get_playlist_quality_candidates`, `place_playlist_imported_item`) are thin, correct `_request()` passthroughs with no independent issues.
- Three pre-existing tests (`test_playlist_pipeline.py::test_release_group_drives_album_reuse_and_beets_path`, `::test_playlist_import_album_artist_resolution_and_review_failures`, `::test_playlist_import_placement_uses_short_retryable_db_writes`; `test_playlist_backend_job.py::test_playlist_download_job_can_parse_and_match_inside_backend`) asserted source-text markers (`_sqlite_write_retry`, a specific local log string, a function-ordering assumption, a local subprocess-config filename) that were tied to the pre-Wave-13 local-mutation implementation and were only ever passing by accident against the dead/shadowed duplicate `_playlist_apply_album_placement()` copy described above. Updated to assert the actual current (and correct) engine-delegation shape instead of resurrecting the old markers.
- Tests: 21 new across `tests/test_sec002_wave13_playlist_pipeline_engine_ownership.py` (crash-regression guards, `_playlist_resolve_operation_key` strictness, placement/singleton-move refusal on unresolvable key, fabricated-candidacy fail-closed, quality-candidates fail-closed, copy-failure honesty, validate-staged-download fail-closed, engine-side `_playlist_item_belongs_to_playlist` authorization x6 POSIX-gated) plus updates to 6 pre-existing tests whose fixtures/assertions depended on the now-fixed defects. Full suite: 2178 tests, 0 failures, 128 skipped (POSIX-only).
- SEC-002 remains Open pending main-branch backlog (unrelated to this wave). No new architecture-completeness gap identified this pass beyond the pre-existing, out-of-scope `_s()`/`_album_cleanup_valid_rgid()` duplication noted above.
- **CI fallout, fixed in a follow-up commit before merge**: Linux CI (the only place `resolve_safe_path()`'s POSIX-only tests actually run) caught two issues the Windows-only local run couldn't: the two new `PlaylistQualityCandidatesUnavailableError` HTTP error responses returned `str(ex)` directly, which CodeQL correctly flagged as genuine `py/stack-trace-exposure` (not a false positive, unlike the six `py/path-injection` alerts below) -- fixed with static messages, real exception logged server-side only. Separately, the new engine-side authorization test class's `setUp()` patched `bca.PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` but not `bca.DOWNLOAD_PATH` -- `resolve_safe_path()`'s `"staging"` role actually validates against the latter, a distinct module global, so every `_playlist_import_operations_root()` call in the test raised `UnsafePathError`; the class is POSIX-gated so this was never exercised locally. Fixed by patching both globals.
- CodeQL, final state: 6 new `py/path-injection` alerts opened against the corrected head (all in the rewritten `/playlists/staging/list-files` and the unchanged `/playlists/staging/validate-track` handlers) -- each individually traced to a `stat()`/`os.walk()`/`is_symlink()` call downstream of a `resolve_safe_path()` + `.relative_to()` + `_path_has_symlink_component()` containment chain a few lines above; CodeQL does not model that chain as a sanitizer. Individually dismissed via the API with per-line justification (never bulk-dismissed). 0 open alerts on the final head.

All 19 CI checks green (lint, typecheck, node-build x2, python-tests x2, security x2, docker-build, GHCR publish, compose-verification, beets-engine-verification x2, beets-engine-latest-compat, CodeQL gate, per-language `Analyze` x3). 0 open CodeQL alerts. ### Wave 14: MusicBrainz Identity & Matching Consolidation (PR #87) -- Claude final independent review, second/final AGY attempt

AGY's second and final attempt introduced a shared, explicit matching-decision contract (`backend/matching_contract.py`'s `MatchingDecision`/`build_recording_matching_decision`/`build_album_matching_decision`) meant to make deterministic identity proof (Release Group ID equality, exact Recording ID matches) the single authority for whether a candidate can auto-import/auto-repair versus merely stay visible for manual review, replacing scattered independent heuristics across Import Review, Folder Preflight, Release Resolution, Reimport, AI Batch, Repair, Replacement, Duplicate Cleanup, Playlist Placement, MusicBrainz Submit, and Frontend. The PR's own claim table asserted all 11 workflows were "MIGRATED"/"VERIFIED EXISTING" with the new contract authoritative everywhere. Per this pass's explicit mandate ("do not send this back to AGY -- you are the final fix+review+merge authority"), independently traced every one of those 11 claims against the actual production call graph (not the PR's own test citations, which turned out to exercise `matching_contract.py`'s builders in isolation only -- the whole test file had zero `import app`/production references) and found:

- **CRITICAL, FIXED -- the real Import Review auto-import gate was completely disconnected from the new contract.** `_folder_release_preflight()` correctly computed `matching_decision` (including `action_allowed`/`review_required`), but two compaction functions downstream of it on the actual Import Review call path -- `_compact_preflight()` and `_import_review_revalidation_preflight()` -- each used a fixed key allowlist that silently dropped `matching_decision` before it ever reached `_import_review_build_revalidated_match()`, the function that actually computes `is_importable`/`auto_fix_eligible` for the real auto-import trigger (`ai_suggest_review_batch` -> `evaluate_import_eligibility` -> `_import_review_start_auto_import`). That function's own, entirely independent heuristic (`identity_validated` + `is_release_group_usable` + `has_representative` + `importable_count` + `preflight_status`) could therefore authorize automatic import even when the shared decision explicitly said `action_allowed=False` (e.g. an unresolved Release Group ID, or a track-identity conflict) -- the exact "review candidate silently becomes an automatic mutation" gap this whole wave was supposed to close. Fixed by threading `matching_decision` through both compaction layers and adding an explicit `action_allowed`-required check to `is_importable`'s computation (absent `matching_decision` -- e.g. no album preflight ran at all -- does not itself block, only an explicit `action_allowed=False` does). Because `_folder_release_preflight()` is also the function `_resolve_album_release_for_import()` (Release Resolution, used by both manual resolution and `reimport_disk`/Reimport) calls to accept or reject a release candidate via its `pre.get("ok")` field, this single fix transitively closes the same gap for those two workflows as well.
- **FIXED -- a second, independent bypass of the same kind**: `_preflight_oversized_subset_complete()` (a legacy tracklist-count-ratio heuristic used by `_resolve_album_release_for_import`'s "does a small local folder match part of a larger MB release" path) accepted a release candidate purely on track-count ratio, without ever consulting `matching_decision.action_allowed` -- so an oversized-subset match could bypass the shared authority entirely even after the first fix. Now requires `action_allowed` when a `matching_decision` is present.
- **FIXED -- `_folder_release_preflight()`'s own `gate_ok` only checked `conflicts`, not `action_allowed`.** `if decision_dict.get("conflicts"): gate_ok = False` meant a review-required candidate with an empty `conflicts` list (the normal case for "insufficient evidence" rather than "contradictory evidence") left `gate_ok` exactly as the old independent tracklist-match-ratio heuristic computed it -- i.e. still `True` if the tracklist matched well, regardless of what the shared decision said. `gate_ok` now also requires `action_allowed` explicitly (differential test: `test_folder_release_preflight_gate_ok_requires_action_allowed`, constructed so the legacy heuristic alone would pass while the shared decision withholds authority).
- **FIXED -- generic `details` mapping could be promoted to a fake release object (`build_recording_matching_decision`).** `raw_selected_release` fell back to the *entire* `details` mapping (not just `details["selected_release"]`) whenever neither `candidate["selected_release"]` nor `details["selected_release"]` was present -- any caller's broader match-context dict (recording ID, AI contribution, source metadata, ...) could have its unrelated top-level fields misread as release identity/title fields. Reverted to the safe `{}` fallback that was in place before this wave's diff.
- **FIXED -- Release-ID-only evidence was classified as a hard conflict, not insufficient evidence (`build_album_matching_decision`).** A missing/unresolved Release Group ID was added to `conflicts` (`conflicts.append("release_group_missing")`), which made the dedicated `elif not candidate_rg_id: safety_key = "review"` branch beneath it permanently unreachable (the `if conflicts:` branch above it always fired first) -- every Release-ID-only candidate was misclassified as `safety_key="conflict"`/`recommended_action="Reject candidate"` instead of `safety_key="review"`, exactly conflating "we don't have enough proof yet" with "we have proof this is wrong". `conflicts` is now reserved for genuine contradictions (RGID mismatch, Artist ID mismatch, fingerprint mismatch); missing evidence is warning-only and routes to review as originally intended. The existing test for this (`test_release_id_only_resolves_rgid_or_reviews`) had itself been asserting the buggy behavior (`assertIn("release_group_missing", d["conflicts"])`) as if it were correct -- fixed to assert the corrected classification instead.
- **FIXED, narrow scope -- local media-tag-read fallback in the new preflight evidence-gathering code.** `_folder_release_preflight()`'s new local-identity-evidence block called `_read_file_media_tags(str(fpath))` on up to 3 files from a locally-`rglob()`'d folder listing when neither an existing album row nor engine `inspect_import_source()` evidence supplied a Release Group ID; that helper's own local `MediaFile`/`mutagen` fallback (used whenever the primary `beets_client.read_tags()` IPC call fails/returns empty) is a real local-media-inspection risk in a container with no `/data` mount, and reachable if the container topology were ever misconfigured to have local access. Removed that fallback path entirely (no replacement local read); instead extended the engine's existing `inspect_import_source()` (used for exactly this "web manager has no local media access" case) to also return tag evidence (`title`/`album`/`mb_trackid`/`mb_releasegroupid`/...) per audio file, reusing the exact `MediaFile`/`mutagen` logic `/tags/read` already used (factored out into a shared `_read_media_tags_best_effort()` helper in `beets_control_agent.py`), so the same bounded, already-authorized directory walk that produces ffprobe format evidence now also produces tag evidence, with zero new engine attack surface.
- **Corrected a claim in the review task document itself, not just the PR.** The task's own framing characterized `with _db(...) as con: ... SELECT album, albumartist, mb_releasegroupid FROM albums ...` in the new preflight code as "local Beets SQLite access", listing it as a hard architecture blocker. Independently verified via `get_db_connection()`'s own docstring and `test_get_db_connection_never_uses_local_sqlite`/`test_external_beets_architecture.py` that `_db()` always returns a `RemoteSQLiteConnection` proxied through `BeetsClient` to the engine and has never opened a local SQLite file since PR #64 -- this was not a violation at all. Switched the call site to the more specific, purpose-built `beets_client.get_album()` anyway (equally correct, clearer intent), but did not characterize this as a security fix in the PR body, since it wasn't one. This is a reminder that even a detailed, mostly-accurate review brief can be independently wrong about a specific technical claim, and "don't trust the report" has to apply to the review instructions too, not only to the PR being reviewed.
- **Corrected two more false "MIGRATED" claims in the PR's own caller table** (in addition to fixing the real Import Review gap above): (1) **Repair Workflow** (`repair_album_mb_tracks`/`_album_mb_completeness`) never called `build_recording_matching_decision`/`build_album_matching_decision` at all -- confirmed via direct grep, zero hits. Rather than a full retrofit of its separate, independent title/track-alignment logic (`summarize_mb_track_alignment`, a pre-existing and differently-designed mechanism, out of narrow-correction scope to redesign), added a targeted guard: a caller-supplied `mb_albumid` override is now rejected outright if its release group disagrees with the album's already-established `mb_releasegroupid`, closing the most severe part of the gap (attaching an unrelated release's track IDs) without touching the rest of the function. Relabeled "NOT MIGRATED" (with the fix noted) rather than left as the false "MIGRATED" claim. (2) **Frontend Eligibility** was claimed "MIGRATED" despite this PR changing zero frontend files (confirmed via `git diff --stat`); independently verified `ImportReviewPage.tsx` already only *displays* the backend's `safety_result`/`safety_key` (`<DetailRow label="Backend status" value={displayCandidate.safety_result || displayCandidate.safety_key} />`) rather than recomputing eligibility client-side, so the accurate label is "VERIFIED EXISTING", not "MIGRATED" -- corrected in the PR body rather than fixed in code, since there was nothing to fix.
- **Verified correct, no fix needed**: `_album_cleanup_merge_plan`'s real (not just contract-level) Release Group ID guard for Duplicate Cleanup -- confirmed via direct code read it genuinely calls `build_album_matching_decision` and refuses to merge on a genuine RGID conflict; added a real production-path test (`test_album_cleanup_merge_plan_real_path_rejects_different_rgid`) since the existing suite, like all its siblings, only exercised the standalone builder. AI Batch (`ai_suggest`) and the manual-MBID-entry validation path are display/candidate-building only -- their actual mutation happens through the pre-existing, correctly-gated `/api/items/<int:iid>/attach-recording` endpoint (`action_eligibility.attach_without_review`, `review_required`, `requires_confirmation`, `conflicts`, plus a `decision_version` audit trail requiring the exact displayed decision to match before either a "safe" auto-attach or an explicit human-confirmed override), which this pass independently traced end to end and found already sound; "Replacement Workflow" is the same endpoint's `mode=confirmed_review` path. MusicBrainz Submit (`item_mbsubmit`/`album_mbsubmit`) is a read-only `beet mbsubmit` diagnostic passthrough with no library mutation at all, so it needs no identity gate. Playlist Placement is Wave 13's engine-owned recording/AcoustID matching, untouched by and unrelated to this PR.
- **AcoustID claim corrected rather than backfilled.** The original PR summary claimed album-family `identity_verified` could be established via "verified Recording IDs / AcoustID audio matches", but `build_album_matching_decision`'s actual implementation only ever computes `deterministic_track_proof` from exact Recording ID equality -- there is no AcoustID/fingerprint consumption anywhere in the album builder. `_folder_release_preflight()` does independently compute `acoustid_target_hits`/`acoustid_mismatch` (via a pre-existing, unrelated-to-this-PR local `_acoustid_multi_file()` call, itself only reachable when `audio_files` is locally non-empty -- effectively dead code in the true no-`/data` topology, same class of debt as other pre-existing local-fallback code left out of narrow-correction scope this pass), but that evidence was never wired into the album identity decision, and wiring an unverified, non-engine-sourced fingerprint computation into the authoritative identity gate would have made the trust model *worse*, not better. Elected not to implement new AcoustID consumption in the album builder this pass (would require a genuinely new engine-side AcoustID-for-folder-preflight capability, appropriately out of narrow-correction scope) and corrected the PR's summary claim to describe what the code actually does: RGID equality and exact Recording ID matches only.
- **Partial-album policy made explicit.** `deterministic_track_proof` requires every *local* item to have an exact Recording ID match against the candidate's tracklist, but deliberately does not require full tracklist coverage -- a 2-track local folder with both tracks exactly matched against an 18-track candidate release counts as sufficient deterministic proof. This was already the code's behavior but was an implicit side effect of the match-count loop bounds rather than a documented decision; added an explaining comment plus `test_partial_album_full_deterministic_match_is_identity_verified` to lock it in as an intentional policy.
- Tests: 10 new in `tests/test_sec002_wave14_mb_identity_matching.py`, including a new `TestSEC002Wave14ProductionCallerWiring` class specifically to hold real production-path tests (as opposed to the rest of the file, which -- both before and after this pass -- tests `matching_contract.py`'s builders standalone): the Import Review gate fix, both compaction-layer preservation checks, the `_folder_release_preflight` gate_ok differential test, the oversized-subset bypass fix, the real `_album_cleanup_merge_plan` RGID guard, and the real `_album_mb_completeness` repair-workflow guard. 1 existing test fixed (was asserting the pre-fix Release-ID-only-as-conflict bug as correct behavior). Full suite: 2207 tests, 0 failures, 128 skipped (POSIX-only), 3 consecutive clean runs; frontend `npm audit`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`test`/`build` all clean.
- CodeQL: 0 open PR-scoped alerts at the final head; no dismissals needed this pass.
- **Merge result**: PR #87 approved and squash-merged. Reviewed branch `fix/sec002-mb-identity-matching-wave14` (preserved, not deleted), final head `040c37ffa22a06b965bed7601c1bccd6f831cc65`. Squash SHA `88517a7d9344e9ee5aa10ef8d04fb21f417e1073`, now `main`. All 19 CI checks green. 0 open CodeQL alerts. No production deployment, no release published, `v0.1.13` unmoved, no new wave started. SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 17: Track Replacement Controlled Mutation Boundary (PR #90) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

(Waves 15-16, which established the Import Review cleanup and album cleanup mutation boundaries this wave builds on, are not separately logged in this file -- a pre-existing documentation gap not backfilled here, out of this entry's narrow scope. See `docs/operations/wave17_track_replacement_design.md` for the corrected design and `tests/test_sec002_wave17_track_replacement.py` for the full test suite this wave added.)

AGY reported Wave 17 (a new `track_replacement_v1` mutation family, migrating track-replacement file/DB mutation into the engine boundary) "complete, verified, pushed, and ready for review." Per this review's explicit mandate not to accept that claim at face value, independently re-verified the actual diff against the starting head and found the implementation did not match the report on nearly every point: no human Review step actually existed between Plan and Apply in the production caller (Plan and Apply were invoked back-to-back inside the same function); the claimed 4-workflow caller matrix was really one workflow (`_music_format_remove_original_after_replacement`) -- `_import_and_tag_disk`/`repair_album_mb_tracks`/`attach-recording` were never touched; production Plan calls carried no Recording ID/AcoustID/matching evidence at all despite the design doc's claim that AcoustID verification gated every replacement; the client could set `quarantine_root` in the Plan payload; `_new_id()` was called once for the quarantine path and a second, different ID was returned as `operation_id` by `store.create()`; the original file's path was accepted from the client with no verification it actually belonged to the given item ID; TOCTOU re-verification at Apply covered only inode/device, not size/mtime; symlink checks only inspected the leaf path, not parent components; `allowed_roots` was accepted but not meaningfully re-checked at Apply; one combined root set allowed an "original" to be satisfied by a download-root file; a DB update failure was caught by a bare `except Exception: pass` and the transaction still reported `Completed`; rollback `unlink()`d the current file before confirming the original could be restored; rollback did not check `mutation_family`; a dead `if not tx` check after `store.get()` left a `KeyError` uncaught; and there was no frontend Review UI at all (0 new frontend files, frontend suite unchanged at 38 tests) despite the report's "ready for review" claim implying an operator-facing flow existed.
- **FIXED -- rebuilt Plan/Apply/Rollback for `track_replacement_v1` from the ground up** in `backend/transaction_engine.py`: item-ID-bound original resolution (client `original_path` is expected-state evidence only, cross-checked against a DB read -- `item_path_mismatch` on disagreement, never independently trusted); role-specific root sets (`original_allowed_roots` = music-library root only, `candidate_allowed_roots` = approved staging roots only -- no combined set); server-derived quarantine root only (`REPLACEMENT_QUARANTINE_DIR`, read engine-side; a client-supplied `quarantine_root` is ignored); the transaction's own `store.create()`-issued ID is used for both the quarantine path and the returned `operation_id` (no second, discarded ID); full TOCTOU stat re-verification (inode, device, size, `mtime_ns`) for **both** the original and the candidate immediately before Apply mutates anything; a hardened symlink check that walks every path component on a normalized-but-not-yet-`resolve()`d path (an ordering bug found in this wave's own new code during test-writing -- `.resolve()` transparently follows and erases symlinks before a walk over the resolved path could ever see them); `allowed_roots` re-validated immediately before each mutation, not only at Plan time.
- **FIXED -- corrected destination semantics (a real behavioral regression in AGY's implementation, not merely an incompleteness)**: traced the pre-Wave-17 `_music_format_remove_original_after_replacement`'s actual behavior and confirmed it never moved the replacement file -- it only quarantined/deleted the original, relying on the replacement already being separately tracked as its own library item via the normal import pipeline. AGY's Apply moved the replacement candidate onto the original's path, silently changing this contract. Removed; Apply now only quarantines the original and removes its DB row, and a production-path regression test (`RealEndToEndRouteTests`) asserts the candidate file is left untouched.
- **FIXED -- matching authority made mandatory, reusing existing infrastructure rather than building a second fingerprint stack.** `create_track_replacement_plan` now requires a `matching_contract` (`identity_source`, `replacement_recording_id` required; original/replacement Recording ID and Release Group ID cross-checked when known; AI-only evidence explicitly rejected via `ai_only_evidence_rejected`). The automatic background job (`_music_format_replace_rows`) already computed a real AcoustID fingerprint match via existing `_acoustid_fingerprint_match`/`_acoustid_fingerprint_ids` helpers before this wave; the fix threads that already-computed evidence into the contract (`_music_format_replacement_matching_contract()`) instead of re-implementing fingerprinting. The new manual entry point (`POST /api/items/<iid>/replacement/plan`) runs the same real, server-side AcoustID check against the operator-supplied candidate and refuses (`candidate_not_verified`) if it does not match -- "a higher-quality file is not sufficient evidence" is enforced structurally, not just documented.
- **FIXED -- DB failure can no longer report success.** DB row removal is verified by rowcount (`db_rowcount_mismatch` on anything other than exactly 1 row), any exception during the DB step returns a structured `db_update_failed` with `mutated: true` (never swallowed), and a post-mutation DB re-read confirms the row is actually gone before the transaction is allowed to reach `Completed`.
- **FIXED -- rollback ordering, family binding, and DB honesty.** The quarantined original is moved to a `.rollback-holding` sibling first, then to the original path, only reporting `Rolled Back` if both steps genuinely succeed; a failure at the second step returns the file to its quarantine slot and reports `Failed`, never a false `Rolled Back`. `rollback_track_replacement` now requires `mutation_family == "track_replacement_v1"` explicitly. A full-row DB capture taken at Plan time is restored on rollback with a rowcount/identity check; if the item ID has been reused or the DB restore fails, the transaction reports `Partially Rolled Back` (filesystem restored, DB not) instead of overclaiming.
- **FIXED -- crash recovery and concurrency.** `apply_steps` progress is persisted after each meaningful stage so a retry after a crash resumes correctly (including a physical existence check before trusting a "already quarantined" flag). Item-ID-keyed locking (`_get_resource_lock`) serializes two transactions racing on the same Beets item while leaving different items' transactions fully parallel -- proven by a real two-thread concurrency test, not asserted from the presence of a per-operation lock alone.
- **FIXED -- malformed transaction IDs and generic rollback dispatch.** The reintroduced dead `if not tx` check after a `store.get()` call that can raise `KeyError` is fixed (safe structured refusal, no 500). The shared `/api/transactions/<id>/rollback` route now looks up the transaction's real `mutation_family` via `beets_client.get_transaction()` before dispatching, replacing the old hardcoded "always assume Import Review cleanup" fallback -- verified with a regression test that a `track_replacement_v1` transaction routes to `rollback_track_replacement` and not `rollback_import_review_cleanup`.
- **FIXED -- built the actual Review UI.** `TrackReplacementModal.tsx` (new) implements Plan -> Review (existing vs. candidate identity, why-authorized evidence, planned changes, rollback availability) -> explicit "Apply Replacement" click -> Completed/Partial/Failed, wired into `Library.tsx`'s per-track album detail view. Apply is never triggered automatically after Plan.
- **Caller matrix corrected, not padded to match the original claim.** Of the four workflows AGY's design doc claimed as migrated, only `_music_format_remove_original_after_replacement` (plus the new manual entry point, added this wave) actually uses `track_replacement_v1`. `_import_and_tag_disk`/`replace_existing_item_ids` (bulk import-time replacement, a different mutation shape) and `repair_album_mb_tracks` (tag repair with no original/candidate file pair) remain unmigrated, out-of-scope ARCH-003 debt -- not silently forced through this contract to look complete. `/api/items/<iid>/attach-recording` was never actually touched by the diff and is confirmed to already have its own, separately reviewed (Wave 14) MusicBrainz attachment transaction semantics; it is not a track-replacement operation and does not belong in this family. Full detail in `docs/operations/wave17_track_replacement_design.md` section 2.
- Tests: `tests/test_sec002_wave17_track_replacement.py` rewritten from scratch, 52 tests (Plan validation ~20, Apply ~16 including full TOCTOU/DB-failure/crash-recovery coverage, concurrency 2, rollback 7, AST structural 3, real end-to-end Flask-route integration 4). 2 pre-existing Wave 16 tests (`test_generic_rollback_route_rejects_album_cleanup_transaction`, `test_generic_rollback_route_still_works_for_import_review_transaction`) needed updated mocks after the generic-rollback-dispatch fix above (they predated the new `beets_client.get_transaction()` pre-lookup call); fixed, not skipped. Combined regression run (Wave 15 + Wave 16 + Import Review cleanup + Wave 17): 121 tests, 0 failures. Full-repository suite run, frontend gate, Docker builds, and CodeQL disposition against the final pushed head are tracked separately below pending completion.
- Status: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. `track_replacement_v1` covers original-file quarantine + DB row removal for single-track replacement only; `_import_and_tag_disk`/`repair_album_mb_tracks` remain separate, undated debt (see matrix above). SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).
- **Merge result**: PR #90 approved and squash-merged. Reviewed branch `feat/sec002-replacement-transactions-wave17` (preserved, not deleted), final head `881a35e669f0ac80de360fdff62476ff0653ed4b`. Squash SHA `155466b2c75a540594406af70903cf8e549a4486`, now `main`. All 19 CI checks green including `security` (red at the starting head due to a stale endpoint inventory) and `CodeQL` (22 new alerts on the corrected head; 8 genuine path-injection/stack-trace-exposure findings fixed, 14 traced individually and dismissed as false positives; 0 open on the final head). No production deployment, no release published, no new wave started at merge time.

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 18: Bulk Import-Time Replacement Controlled Mutation Boundary (PR #91) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

AGY reported Wave 18 (a new `bulk_import_replacement_v1` mutation family covering bulk import-time track replacement) "PASS -- READY FOR CLAUDE REVIEW." Per this review's explicit mandate not to accept that claim at face value, independently re-verified the actual diff against the starting head and found the implementation did not hold up on nearly every point, the most severe being a genuine, previously-unreported regression far beyond what was even suspected going in:

- **CRITICAL, FIXED -- `_merge_imported_album_into_existing` (the mutation family's only real production caller) was a complete, silent no-op on every single call.** The diff deleted the block that fetched the existing/imported album rows and computed the disc/track matching loop (`existing`, `existing_rows`, `imported_rows`, `move_ids`, `dup_rows`, `replace_rows`), while leaving the code that consumes those names untouched -- but at the SAME indentation level as the body of a sibling helper function (`_row_matches_target`), positioned lexically after that helper's own `return` statement. Confirmed via direct AST inspection: the outer function's `try` block contained nothing but the three nested helper `def`s and fell out the bottom with no `return`, implicitly returning `None` every time, having executed zero merge logic, zero engine delegation, zero database changes, zero file quarantine -- not merely "skips the import step" as originally suspected, but the entire function doing nothing at all. Restored the correct block structure (verified via `ast.walk` that the `with _db(...)` block is now a direct statement inside the `try` block, not nested dead code) and added a real end-to-end production-path regression test (`RealProductionPathTests`) that calls the actual function with a patched local DB and asserts real mutation occurred -- this is exactly the class of test gap the review brief called for (something that would have caught a NameError or, as it turned out, silent non-execution).
- **FIXED -- import ownership resolved as Model B, not Model A.** The original design assumed the transaction should trigger Beets import itself via an `import_executor` callback; independently traced the real caller and confirmed Beets always finishes importing the replacement media *before* `_merge_imported_album_into_existing` ever runs -- its job is reconciling an already-imported result against a pre-existing album. Removed the `import_executor` parameter entirely (it was never wired by the real control-agent endpoint anyway, so that code path was already dead) and required the caller to supply an explicit, already-imported `new_item_id` for every `old_item_id`, independently re-verified from the database at both Plan and Apply time.
- **FIXED -- explicit old_item_id -> new_item_id mapping replaces "whatever else is in the album."** The original Plan only captured a flat list of old item IDs; "new item" identity was implicit (any row satisfying `album_id == existing_album_id` and not an old ID). `create_bulk_import_replacement_plan` now requires a `mappings` list, with no "resolve every row currently in the album" fallback -- confirmed with a regression test that an unrelated, real, pre-existing row in the same album is never usable as an old item's replacement merely by existing.
- **FIXED -- per-mapping matching authority, not just an optional album-level Release Group ID check.** Every mapping requires a non-AI-only `identity_source`; RGID is cross-checked between old and new items; the new item must carry its own Recording ID unless the mapping's evidence was already independently verified upstream (`identity_source == "forced_replacement_verified"`).
- **FIXED, and a second genuine architectural correction found during this review** -- initially wired `replace_existing_item_ids` (the automatic music-format retry pipeline's explicit override) through `bulk_import_replacement_v1` too, using `identity_source="forced_replacement_verified"`. Tracing the real caller (`_music_format_replacement_payload` -> `_music_format_replace_rows`) further showed that pipeline already retires its one specific old item through its own, separate, SEC-002 Wave 17 `track_replacement_v1` transaction (`_music_format_remove_original_after_replacement`), called independently *after* the import this function reconciles completes -- at the point `_merge_imported_album_into_existing` runs, that Wave 17 retirement has not happened yet, which is exactly why `forced_replace_rows` still finds the old row present.
