Routing it through `bulk_import_replacement_v1` here too would have been a second, redundant, and potentially conflicting retirement attempt on the same item, not a safety improvement. Reverted that branch to its pre-Wave-18 bookkeeping-only behavior and added a regression test (`test_merge_function_forced_replace_leaves_old_row_for_wave17`) asserting `plan_bulk_import_replacement` is never called on that path.
- **FIXED -- all-or-nothing retirement.** Every mapping (old item TOCTOU + new item existence/path/TOCTOU) is re-verified before any old file is quarantined; one bad mapping in a multi-track transaction blocks the entire Apply with zero mutation, rather than partially retiring the tracks that did verify.
- **FIXED -- full TOCTOU (device, inode, size, mtime_ns) for both the old and new item**, not only inode/size as the original implementation checked.
- **FIXED -- exact DB rowcount verification.** The bulk `DELETE FROM items WHERE id IN (...)` now checks `cur.rowcount == len(old_item_ids)` and re-reads the DB afterward to confirm every intended id is actually gone, after first re-reading live rows immediately before deleting to catch anything gone stale since Plan.
- **FIXED -- deterministic, sorted resource locking.** `execute_bulk_import_replacement_apply` locks `album:<id>` and every `item:<old_item_id>` (sorted) before the per-operation lock; two transactions racing on the same item or album now serialize (verified with a real two-thread test), while transactions on disjoint albums/items remain fully parallel (verified with a second real two-thread test). A related race was found and fixed during that same test-writing: the quarantine-destination containment check called `Path.resolve(strict=False)` on a not-yet-existing sibling directory while two threads created sibling quarantine subdirectories concurrently, and silently fell back to an incomparable unresolved path on any resolve() exception -- spuriously failing the safety check under real concurrency. Replaced with a pure lexical (`os.path.normpath`) containment comparison that touches no filesystem state and cannot race.
- **FIXED -- rollback recoverable for partial-mutation failures, not gated on `status == "Completed"` alone.** A `Failed` transaction that already durably quarantined some old files before failing is now rollback-eligible. A second, independent bug was found and fixed while testing this: the original rollback design restored quarantined files and DB rows in two *independent* loops, so a file-restore failure for one item did not stop that same item's DB row from being reinserted anyway -- silently pointing a live DB row at a path that no longer had (or was refused as already-occupied) a matching file. DB restoration is now strictly conditional, per item, on that same item's file restoration having just succeeded in the same rollback call.
- **FIXED -- durable per-item crash recovery.** `apply_steps` and the accumulating `quarantined_files` list are persisted to top-level transaction metadata after every meaningful stage, including on a failure return mid-quarantine-loop (not only on success) -- without this fix, a partially-mutated `Failed` transaction reported `mutated: true` but rollback could not see what had actually been quarantined, since the progress had only been written into a per-step sub-record rather than the top-level metadata rollback reads.
- **FIXED -- generic rollback dispatch integrated cleanly with Wave 17's family-aware mechanism.** Added an explicit `bulk_import_replacement_v1` branch to `api_transaction_rollback`'s dispatch chain; no "unknown family -> try X" fallback introduced.
- **Caller matrix corrected, not padded to match the original claim.** Of the three workflows the original design document claimed as migrated, only the `replace_rows` branch of `_merge_imported_album_into_existing` actually uses `bulk_import_replacement_v1`. `forced_replace_rows`/`replace_existing_item_ids` deliberately remains on Wave 17's `track_replacement_v1` (see above); `dup_rows` (redundant downloaded-duplicate cleanup) and `move_ids` (`UPDATE items SET album_id=...` bookkeeping) remain local, pre-existing, unmigrated debt, unchanged by this wave. Full detail in `docs/operations/wave18_bulk_import_replacement_design.md` section 2.
- Tests: `tests/test_sec002_wave18_bulk_import_replacement.py` rewritten from scratch, 56 tests (Plan validation ~19, Apply ~15 including all-or-nothing/full TOCTOU/DB-rowcount/crash-recovery coverage, concurrency 2, rollback 8, AST structural 7 including a direct "merge function is not dead code" regression test, generic-rollback-dispatch 1, real end-to-end production-path integration 2). The suite's own original focused test (`test_apply_lifecycle_and_quarantine`) had asserted the dangerous pre-review behavior -- retiring old state with zero replacement-import verification -- as correct; removed, replaced with `test_apply_refuses_if_new_item_vanishes_before_apply` and the all-or-nothing test above, both asserting zero old files moved and zero old rows removed when a required replacement is unverified. Combined regression run (Wave 15 + Wave 16 + Wave 17 + Wave 18 + Import Review cleanup): 178 tests, 0 failures.
- Status: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. `bulk_import_replacement_v1` covers only the `replace_rows` reconciliation case described above; `repair_album_mb_tracks` and `_merge_imported_album_into_existing`'s own `dup_rows`/`move_ids` bookkeeping remain separate, undated debt. SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 19: MusicBrainz Album Track Repair Controlled Mutation Boundary (PR #92) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

AGY reported Wave 19 (a new `album_mb_track_repair_v1` mutation family covering `repair_album_mb_tracks`) "complete and ready for review," with 7 core CI workflows green at the reported head. Per this review's explicit mandate not to accept that conclusion as evidence, independently re-verified PR #92 (confirmed OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED, head `9608fef66cc938c304ef9f806571075f1b6b50ec` matched) and read the actual implementation rather than trusting the report. Nearly every defect flagged in the review brief was confirmed present in the real diff:

- **CRITICAL, FIXED -- fake successful audio-tag writes.** `_write_file_audio_tags` tried Beets `MediaFile`, then Mutagen, then -- if both failed -- called `file_path.touch()` and reported `written = True`. A timestamp change is not a metadata write; this let the test suite's own fake `b"FLAC_DATA_1"` fixtures (unparseable by any real tag reader) "succeed" every time, and would do the same for any real unsupported/corrupt file in production. Removed the fallback entirely. `_write_file_audio_tags` now uses `mediafile.MediaFile` (the actively maintained package Beets itself uses; `beets.mediafile` is a deprecated shim) as the sole writer, and only reports success once an independent `_read_file_audio_tags` call confirms every requested field actually reads back correctly. No secondary Mutagen fallback -- one authoritative writer, not a parallel implementation.
- **FIXED -- `_read_file_audio_tags` swallowed every failure into a bare `{}`,** indistinguishable from "file legitimately has no tags." Rewrote to return `{"ok", "tags", "reason"}` with reason codes (`not_found`/`unreadable`/`io_error`/`unknown_error`) so Plan can safely decide whether a row's recovery state is trustworthy.
- **FIXED -- Plan never captured actual on-disk tag values at all** (the function was defined but never called from Plan). `create_album_mb_track_repair_plan` now reads real file tags per candidate row via `_read_file_audio_tags` and stores them separately (`before.file_tags`) from the Beets DB row snapshot; a row whose file cannot be read reliably is excluded from automatic repair (`unreadable_items`) rather than repaired without a trustworthy rollback snapshot.
- **FIXED -- matching authority allowed an existing nonblank Recording ID to be silently overwritten by fuzzy title/position/duration evidence alone** (0.72 threshold, no stronger evidence required) -- confirmed by the original submission's own `test_matching_same_title_wrong_recording_id`, which asserted that outcome as correct. Split repair candidates into `tracks_to_repair` (blank current ID, safe to fill) and `conflicts_requiring_review` (nonblank conflicting ID, never auto-applied, surfaced in the Plan for manual review) -- see `docs/operations/wave19_mb_track_repair_design.md` section 3. The inverted test now locks in the safe behavior instead of the unsafe one. No AcoustID integration was wired into the conflict path (tracked as remaining debt below, not silently declared solved).
- **CRITICAL REGRESSION, FIXED -- release-only stamping was a silent no-op in both production callers.** `create_album_mb_track_repair_plan` can produce a real transaction with `tracks_to_repair == []` but `release_stamping_needed == True`; both `repair_album_mb_tracks` and `_repair_album_mbid_sticking_once` treated `updated == 0` as "nothing to do" and checked a `release_rows_updated` field the engine never actually returned, so the created transaction was simply abandoned, unapplied. Fixed both callers to check `updated > 0 or release_stamping_needed or release_stamp_rows > 0`.
- **FIXED -- release-only stamping's mutation scope was invisible to Plan and unrecoverable by rollback.** Apply's `UPDATE items SET mb_albumid=? WHERE album_id=?` touched every item in the album, but Plan only captured the recording-ID-repair subset, and rollback inferred the album's own original value from `tracks_to_repair[0]` (wrong when zero tracks were repaired, or when the album row's own original value differed from the first repaired track's). `create_album_mb_track_repair_plan` now builds an explicit `album_release_stamp_rows` list covering every other affected item (with its own DB + file-tag before-state) and an explicit `album_before` snapshot of the album row's own original values; Apply's DB update now targets exactly the item ids Plan captured (`tracks_to_repair` + `album_release_stamp_rows`), never a blanket `WHERE album_id=?`; rollback restores the album row from `album_before` and every stamped item individually.
- **FIXED -- `mutated: True` was set before Stage 1 (DB update) even opened a connection,** so a failure before the first successful commit still left the transaction falsely advertising itself as mutated/rollback-eligible. Introduced `mutation_started` as a durable pre-mutation intent marker, and only set `mutated`/`db_mutated: True` immediately after Stage 1's `commit()` actually succeeds; `tags_mutated` similarly only after Stage 2 tag writes succeed.
- **FIXED -- no rowcount verification on any DB write.** Every `UPDATE` (per-item and album-row) now checks `cursor.rowcount == 1`; a mismatch rolls back the in-flight SQL transaction and fails Apply before anything commits.
- **FIXED -- post-write verification only re-checked `mb_trackid`.** Stage 3 now re-reads and compares every field the transaction claims to have changed (`mb_trackid`, `mb_albumid`, `title`, `track`, `disc` for repair rows; `mb_albumid` for stamp rows and the album row).
- **FIXED -- rollback had no resource locking, no TOCTOU/path revalidation, and could overwrite a later legitimate edit.** `rollback_album_mb_track_repair` now acquires the identical `album:<id>`/`item:<id>` locks Apply does (same sorted order), re-validates roots containment and a full parent-symlink walk immediately before each file write, and refuses (`repair_rollback_stale`) if the current DB state no longer matches the transaction's own recorded post-Apply state -- proven by a regression test where a later manual edit survives a stale rollback attempt untouched.
- **FIXED -- repeated rollback was only accidentally safe** (blocked via a `status` check with a misleading "no mutations" message even after a real rollback had occurred). Now returns an explicit, truthful `repair_already_rolled_back` refusal.
- **FIXED -- `write_tags=False` was accepted by `_repair_album_mbid_sticking_once` but never referenced in its body,** and the engine's Apply had no way to suppress file mutation at all. Every current production call site happens to pass `write_tags=True` (so this was latent, not an observed behavior change), but the contract is now real end-to-end: `execute_album_mb_track_repair_apply(..., write_tags=...)` skips the tag-write stage entirely when False, threaded through `beets_client.apply_album_mb_track_repair(..., write_tags=...)` and the control-agent route.
- **FIXED -- Plan claimed an `mb_releasegroupid` after-state Apply never wrote.** `after_state["mb_releasegroupid"]` now always shows the unchanged current value; Apply never touches that column for this family. Also closed a blank-RGID gap: the original RG-conflict check only fired when a caller-supplied override coincided with an already-established album RG -- a blank album RG with a resolved candidate RG went unchecked. Now refuses (`repair_rg_not_established`) whenever the album's own Release Group identity isn't established yet, regardless of caller override (Option A from the review; Option B -- explicitly establishing the candidate RG as its own reviewable transaction -- deferred, tracked below).
- **FIXED -- raw exception text and internal DB paths leaked into API-facing error strings** (`f"Beets database file not found at {lib_db}"`, `f"...: {ex}"` on every DB/verification/rollback failure path within this family). Replaced with stable public messages (`"Beets database unavailable."`, `"Database update failed."`, etc.) plus server-side `logging.getLogger("beets_web.transaction_engine")` calls carrying the real detail. Note: the identical `f"Beets database not found at {lib_db}"` leak pattern pre-exists in Waves 15/17/18's own functions in the same file -- left unchanged as out-of-scope for this wave's narrow review, not silently expanded into previously-merged code.
- **FIXED -- unclosed SQLite connections on exception paths** in all three Wave 19 functions (Stage 1/Stage 3 of Apply, and the TOCTOU/restore stages of Rollback) -- every connection is now opened inside a `try`/`finally` that guarantees `close()`.
- **Architecture scan widened.** AGY's own AST test only inspected `repair_album_mb_tracks`; `_repair_album_mbid_sticking_once` is a second, automatic production caller of the same family and was not covered. Added `test_app_py_automatic_repair_helper_contains_no_direct_mutations` holding it to the identical no-local-mutation standard (also widened the prohibited-call list to include `UPDATE albums SET`, `MediaFile(`, `mutagen.File(`).
- **Real audio fixtures.** The original test suite's `_create_album_and_items` wrote fake `b"FLAC_DATA_N"` bytes into `.flac`-named files -- unparseable by any real tag reader, which is exactly what let the `touch()` fallback go undetected. Rewrote the fixture to synthesize real, minimal, playable WAV audio (same technique as `scripts/seed_demo_library.py`'s demo library -- self-synthesized sine wave, no copyrighted material) with real MusicBrainz tags written via `mediafile.MediaFile`, with a `blank_recording_ids` toggle to cover both the safe-fill and conflict-review matching paths.
- **Not done this pass (tracked as remaining debt, not claimed complete):** AcoustID-based resolution for `conflicts_requiring_review` (deferred to manual review instead); Option B (explicit Release-Group-establishment transaction) for a blank-RGID album; fine-grained crash-resume that distinguishes "DB committed, durable marker not yet persisted" from a genuinely stale Plan (the truthful-`mutated`-timing fix closes the false-positive direction of this gap, not the full resume-semantics gap); a dedicated white-box test forcing a real mid-Apply DB rowcount mismatch; and a frontend review surface for `conflicts_requiring_review` rows. **Corrected during this review, not merely deferred:** the initial draft of this entry assumed a "Repair MusicBrainz Tracks" UI button existed and that its click was the Plan-then-immediate-Apply authorization event; verified by search that `repair_album_mb_tracks`'s frontend client wrapper (`frontend/src/api/client.ts::repairAlbumMbTracks`) is not called from any component -- no such button exists. The only real UI path to this mutation family today is `startMbidStickingRepair()` in `frontend/src/views/Jobs.tsx`, a bulk multi-album action. Full detail in `docs/operations/wave19_mb_track_repair_design.md` section 11.
- Tests: `tests/test_sec002_wave19_mb_track_repair.py` -- 38 tests (Plan validation, Apply including TOCTOU/rowcount/crash-timing coverage, matching-authority conflict-vs-blank split, concurrency, rollback including stale-edit refusal and repeated-rollback refusal, a dedicated `TagWriteIntegrityTests` class proving no fake success and real readback verification, a dedicated `ReleaseOnlyStampingTests` class proving the regression fix end-to-end through the real `_repair_album_mbid_sticking_once` caller, `write_tags=False` compatibility, real production-path integration, and widened AST mutation-prohibition coverage). Regression run (Waves 15/16/17/18 + Import Review cleanup + MB alignment + matching contract + album track matching + import track alignment + import matching evidence + Wave 14 MB identity matching): 271 tests, 0 failures. Full repository suite: 2404 tests, 0 failures, 129 skipped, 2 consecutive clean runs. Frontend `npm ci`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`test`(48 passed)/`build`/`audit --audit-level=high`: all clean. Security gates (`security_secret_scan.py`, `validate_compose_security.py`, `generate_endpoint_inventory.py --check`): all clean after regenerating the endpoint inventory (line-number-only diff, 0 routes needing manual review).
- Status: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. `album_mb_track_repair_v1` covers Beets DB track/album attribute updates and audio file tag writes for MusicBrainz recording/release repair; `_merge_imported_album_into_existing`'s own `dup_rows`/`move_ids` bookkeeping remains separate, unmigrated debt (unchanged by this wave, as before). SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).
- **Merge result**: PR #92 approved and squash-merged. Reviewed branch `feat/sec002-mb-track-repair-wave19` (preserved, not deleted), final head `aafb7c3f037b5a78b6714cbbcb01b6c97c66fc18`. Squash SHA `11c6cbf764be24067f8dd052dd577c2fc4181128`, now `main`. All 19 GitHub Actions checks green at the final head. 0 open CodeQL alerts (PR-ref-scoped, `refs/pull/92/head`, python/javascript-typescript/actions). Both Docker images (`beets-web-manager`, `beets-engine`) built successfully locally with `VCS_REF=aafb7c3`, labeled correctly. No production deployment, no release published, `v0.1.14` unmoved, no new wave started at merge time.

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 20: Existing-Album Duplicate & Move Reconciliation Controlled Mutation Boundary (PR #93) -- Claude final independent review, correction, architecture audit, and merge authority

AGY reported Wave 20 (a new `existing_album_reconcile_v1` mutation family covering `_merge_imported_album_into_existing`'s `dup_rows`/`move_ids` bookkeeping) "implemented, fully tested, and ready for technical review." Per this review's explicit mandate not to accept that report as evidence, independently re-verified PR #93 (confirmed OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED, base ancestry included Wave 19, head `b36a81053c7d460cde3c0a41bec8151d7f4be6bb` matched) and read the actual implementation. GitHub's own CodeQL check was already failing at the starting head (4 open `py/path-injection` alerts, all in the new code) -- independent confirmation before any manual review even began that the report's "ready for review" claim did not hold up. Nearly every defect in the review brief was confirmed present in the real diff:

- **CRITICAL (CodeQL-confirmed, 4 `py/path-injection` alerts at the starting head)**: `create_existing_album_reconcile_plan`/`execute_existing_album_reconcile_apply` appended the request body's `source_folder` directly onto the server's own `music_allowed_roots` list before using it for path-containment checks -- an authenticated-but-untrusted request could expand which filesystem locations the operation was authorized to touch. Fixed by introducing role-specific roots enforced entirely server-side: `library_roots` (survivors only), `staging_roots` (the same server-configured `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`/`PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT`/`TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS`/tempdir list `track_replacement_v1`/`bulk_import_replacement_v1` already use for candidate files), and `imported_item_roots` (library + staging, for already-imported move/duplicate rows). `source_folder` from the request is now only ever used for classification, and only once it independently resolves under a server-configured staging root -- an unvalidated one is silently ignored, never trusted. CodeQL confirms 0 open alerts on the corrected head.
- **CRITICAL**: duplicate/survivor identity was never actually enforced -- a caller-supplied `survivor_item_ids` (or a bare disc/track position match, with no Recording ID comparison at all) was sufficient to authorize physically deleting the "duplicate" file and its DB row. Two items with different, known Recording IDs at the same disc/track position could be silently bound as duplicate and survivor. Fixed: the engine now independently re-establishes identity for every candidate (exact non-blank Recording ID match, or literal same on-disk path) regardless of what the caller supplied; a conflicting known Recording ID fails closed for that duplicate (excluded from automatic reconciliation, surfaced in a new `dup_requires_review` list with `reason: "identity_conflict"`); blank Recording ID on either side is treated as insufficient evidence either way (`reason: "survivor_unverified"`/`"no_candidate_survivor"`), never silently approved or silently refused as a whole-Plan failure. The original submission's fixtures defaulted every dup/survivor pair to the SAME Recording ID, so this gap was never exercised by its own tests; new tests (`test_plan_conflicting_recording_id_fails_closed`, `test_plan_blank_recording_ids_require_review`) lock in the corrected behavior.
- **CRITICAL**: `is_hardlink` detection (same device+inode) did not distinguish a genuine hardlink (different path, same inode) from the SAME PATH being referenced by two separate Beets DB rows -- the latter case would unlink the survivor's only copy of the file while "removing the duplicate." Fixed: duplicates are now classified into `db_only` (identical path -- never touched physically, only the redundant DB row is removed), `hardlink`, or `distinct_file` (both of the latter two are safely quarantined).
- **CRITICAL**: duplicate files under `source_folder` or classified as hardlinks were permanently `unlink()`d, yet the transaction was created with `rollback_available: True` and the design doc claimed unlinked files could be restored -- rollback never actually read the `unlinked_files` list at all, so a "successful" rollback could reinsert a live Beets DB row pointing at a file that no longer existed anywhere. Fixed at the root rather than patched: permanent unlink was removed entirely. Every recoverable duplicate (hardlink or distinct file) is quarantined via rename instead -- for a true hardlink this is safe because renaming one pathname does not affect the inode the survivor's own hardlink still references -- so `rollback_available` is never a lie.
- **FIXED -- raw `shutil.move()` reintroduced** for both Apply's quarantine step and Rollback's restore step, the exact bug class (move-inside-existing-directory ambiguity, no destination-exists check) Waves 15/17 already closed for this codebase. Replaced with the existing hardened `_safe_rename` helper.
- **FIXED -- quarantine directory created before preconditions passed**, and `mutated: True` set before Stage 1 began -- both corrected to the truthful-staging pattern established in Wave 19 (`mutation_started` for intent, `filesystem_mutated`/`db_mutated`/`mutated` only after each stage's first real mutation actually commits).
- **FIXED -- recovery metadata (`quarantined_files`) persisted only once, after the entire duplicate-file loop completed** -- a crash mid-loop would lose track of files already quarantined. Now persisted immediately after each individual success, and Apply now recognizes (and skips re-processing) items a prior crashed attempt on the SAME transaction already durably quarantined, rather than treating their now-missing original path as external tampering.
- **FIXED -- empty-album retirement's `DELETE FROM albums` had no rowcount check**; `will_retire_album` used a count comparison (`len(move_specs) + len(dup_specs) >= total_imported_items`) that could be satisfied by duplicate/overlapping IDs in the request payload rather than genuinely covering every current item. Fixed: exact rowcount check added, retirement now computed from an exact affected-ID-set comparison against actual current album membership, and the payload is canonicalized up front (`reconcile_payload_overlap` on any repeated ID or any ID appearing in both `dup_item_ids` and `move_item_ids`).
- **FIXED -- move items' captured Plan state was too thin** (album_id/album/albumartist/path/stat only) for Apply to detect a changed Recording ID/Release ID/Release Group ID between Plan and Apply; **survivor snapshots were too thin** (no stat signature) and Apply only re-checked `Path.exists()`, not readability. Both now capture and TOCTOU-revalidate full identity + stat signature; survivor readability is re-verified via `_read_file_audio_tags` at both Plan and Apply.
- **FIXED -- Album Release Group TOCTOU was not actually revalidated** at Apply (both albums' live rows were fetched but never compared against each other or against Plan-time values). Now both are captured explicitly at Plan and re-verified at Apply.
- **FIXED -- rollback had no resource locking** (album/item/survivor IDs), **no current-state validation** before overwriting (now refuses `reconcile_rollback_stale` if a later legitimate edit changed what this transaction actually produced), **restored the DB row before confirming the file was restored** (reversed: file restored and verified first; a duplicate's DB row is only reinserted once its file restoration succeeded, or if it never needed one), **restored only a hand-picked field subset** rather than the full captured row (now restores every column captured at Plan via `SELECT *`), **reported plain `ok: True` for a partial recovery** (now `ok: False` with `partial_mutation: true`), and **repeated rollback was only accidentally blocked** (now an explicit, truthful `reconcile_already_rolled_back` refusal). No destination-collision check before restoring a file -- now refused explicitly, tested (`test_rollback_destination_collision_refused`).
- **FIXED -- `_merge_imported_album_into_existing`'s return value was untruthful.** It returned `existing_album_id` (implying successful reconciliation) whenever `dup_rows`/`move_ids`/`replace_rows` were non-empty, even when the delegated engine Plan/Apply call actually failed and was only logged as a `WARN`. Any caller relying on the returned album ID as a success signal was misled. Fixed: tracks per-delegation success and returns `imported_album_id` (nothing changed) when what was attempted did not actually succeed; regression test `test_merge_job_reports_failure_truthfully`.
- **FIXED -- raw exception text and internal DB paths leaked into API-facing errors** on every precondition/DB/verification/rollback failure path in this family. Sanitized to stable public messages with `logging.getLogger("beets_web.transaction_engine")` carrying the real detail server-side.
- **Real repository-wide ARCH-003 audit performed, not assumed.** AGY's status line claimed "Controlled Boundary Migration Complete for Existing-Album Reconciliation" without a supporting audit. A grep-based sweep of `app.py` for filesystem-mutation call sites (`.unlink(`, `os.remove(`, `.rename(`, `os.replace(`, `shutil.move(`, `shutil.rmtree(`, `shutil.copy2(`, `.rmdir(`) and raw DML (`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`) found **82 filesystem-mutation call sites and 100 raw DML statements in `app.py` alone** -- far beyond the one site this wave closes. Representative, function-identified clusters confirmed NOT covered by any controlled transaction family: `_merge_artist_dir_contents`/`clean_artist_folders_stamp_mbid` (artist-folder merge, duplicate removal, artwork replacement -- raw `shutil.move`/`.rename`/`.unlink`/`.rmdir`, no Plan/Apply/rollback/audit trail) and the playlist staging/deletion helpers (`_playlist_atomic_json_replace`, `_playlist_delete_job_state`, `_playlist_copy_source_files`, `_playlist_run_quality_cleanup_job`, `playlist_delete`, `_playlist_delete_staged_track_file`). `routes_setup.py`'s config/setup-marker file writes were also reviewed and are explicitly out of ARCH-003's scope (application configuration, not Beets library/media mutation) -- documented so they are not mistaken for uncounted debt either way. See `docs/operations/wave20_existing_album_reconcile_design.md` section 15 for the full accounting.
- **Not done this pass (tracked as remaining debt, not claimed complete):** AcoustID-based resolution for `dup_requires_review` conflicts (deferred to manual review instead, same policy as Wave 19); fine-grained crash-resume beyond the `db_reassigned`-stage-complete check (does not yet distinguish every possible crash window with full precision); a dead nested helper (`_delete_row_file`, never called) left in `app.py` from the pre-Wave-18 direct-mutation implementation, noted for cleanup rather than touched in this diff; no frontend changes shipped (no UI surfaces `dup_requires_review` rows to an operator yet).
- Tests: `tests/test_sec002_wave20_existing_album_reconcile.py` -- 35 tests (was 17), including new coverage for conflicting/blank Recording ID policy, same-path vs hardlink vs distinct-file physical classification, source_folder authorization-expansion refusal, payload canonicalization (repeated/overlapping IDs), exact-set album retirement, survivor identity/readability TOCTOU, Apply-vs-rollback concurrency, rollback destination-collision refusal, stale-edit rollback refusal, repeated-rollback refusal, and merge-return-value truthfulness. The original fixture wrote fake `b"AUDIO_DATA_N"` bytes; rewritten to synthesize real WAV audio (same technique as Wave 19) since the new survivor-readability check correctly refuses to treat fake bytes as usable media. Regression run (Waves 15-19 + Import Review cleanup + MB alignment + matching contract + album track matching + import track alignment + import matching evidence + Wave 14 MB identity matching): 418 tests, 0 failures. Full repository suite: 2439 tests, 0 failures, 128 skipped, 2 consecutive clean runs. Frontend and security gates clean (endpoint inventory regenerated, 245 routes, 0 needing manual review).
- Status: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress** (corrected from the original submission's overclaimed "Migration Complete for Existing-Album Reconciliation" status line). `existing_album_reconcile_v1` covers exactly the `dup_rows`/`move_ids`/empty-album-retirement mutation this wave targeted; the fresh repo-wide audit above found substantially more uncontrolled `app.py` mutation surface (artist-folder cleanup, playlist staging/deletion) that this wave does not touch. SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 21: Artist Folder Merge & MBID Stamping Controlled Mutation Boundary (PR #94) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

AGY reported Wave 21 (a new `artist_folder_reconcile_v1` mutation family covering `_merge_artist_dir_contents`/`_apply_artist_folder_groups`/`clean_artist_folders_stamp_mbid`) "completed and ready for technical review." GitHub reality at the starting head already contradicted that: `python-tests`, `security`, and `CodeQL` were all failing (two pre-existing artist-folder identity regression tests, and a CodeQL alert). Reading the actual implementation confirmed the report did not hold up on nearly every point, including basic runtime crashes:

- **CRITICAL**: both production callers fell back to importing and directly executing `backend.transaction_engine`'s Plan/Apply functions **in-process inside the Web Manager** whenever the `beets_client` IPC call raised any exception. AGY's own draft entry for this wave described this as "added in-process fallback... for unit tests running without an active control agent" -- it was not test-only; it was live in the actual production code path with no testing guard, and it is why the two identity regression tests happened to keep passing locally (the network call was blocked by the test harness's outbound-request guard, the exception triggered the fallback, and the fallback silently performed the real merge locally, satisfying the tests' filesystem assertions). Removed entirely: the Web Manager now fails closed with a stable logged error whenever the engine cannot be reached, in both callers.
- **CRITICAL**: six helper names used throughout the engine's artist-folder code (`_album_cleanup_file_info`, `_album_cleanup_verified_same_file`, `_album_cleanup_duplicate_file_choice`, `_unique_dest`, `_MB_UUID_RE`, `_ART_EXTS`) were never defined or imported anywhere in `transaction_engine.py` -- they exist only in `app.py`, which this engine module must never depend on. Every code path that reached them (any merge where the destination already had files, or any MBID stamping producing a UUID-suffixed name) would raise `NameError` at actual runtime -- meaning these core paths had never actually been exercised successfully. Engine-local equivalents added, ported faithfully (real SHA1 content-hash duplicate-file verification, not filename/size heuristics).
- **CRITICAL**: `_engine_stamp_artist_folder_scan` called `_stamp_artist_folder_album_mbid_counts` with one required argument missing -- a `TypeError` on every single call. Fixed.
- **CRITICAL**: MBID folder stamping used a 75%-majority threshold, so a folder with 3 albums under Artist ID A and 1 album under a *different, already-established* Artist ID B still produced a stamping candidate for A -- which the (also-fixed) broad-name-match `UPDATE ... WHERE albumartist = ?` would then apply to every row sharing that artist name, overwriting the one album's correct, conflicting ID B. Fixed: stamping candidates now require unanimous consensus (exactly one distinct non-blank Artist ID among a folder's tagged albums); any second distinct id present anywhere in the folder produces no candidate at all.
- **FIXED -- identity authority was tautological.** Plan's only check was caller-supplied `source_mbid != target_mbid`, and the Web Manager caller sent the *same* MusicBrainz-search-derived value as both sides, making the check vacuous. The engine now independently derives each folder's established Artist ID(s) directly from Beets DB state and never trusts a caller-supplied MBID as authority; conflicting established ids fail closed regardless of caller claims, and blank-identity merges require explicit fingerprint-confirmation evidence (name equality alone is never sufficient).
- **FIXED -- broad artist-name `UPDATE ... WHERE albumartist = ?` / `WHERE artist = ?`** could rewrite unrelated rows anywhere in the library sharing the same text name. Replaced with updates bound to the exact album/item IDs discovered under the moved folder path at Plan time, with full before-state capture (`albumartist`/`albumartists`/`mb_albumartistid`/`mb_albumartistids` for albums, plus `artist`/`artists`/`mb_artistid`/`mb_artistids` for items) and exact rowcount verification on every write.
- **FIXED -- rollback never restored artist attributes at all** (only paths) -- now restores every captured before-value.
- **FIXED**: TOCTOU only checked size/mtime_ns and never re-validated root/symlink safety at Apply -- now full dev/inode/size/mtime_ns plus root containment and symlink-chain revalidation immediately before every mutation.
- **FIXED**: raw `shutil.move`/`Path.rename` reintroduced in both Apply and Rollback (the same bug class fixed in Waves 15/17/20) -- replaced with the hardened `_safe_rename` helper.
- **FIXED**: quarantine destinations keyed only by basename, so two different album subdirectories with an identically-named file (e.g. `folder.jpg`) could collide -- now keyed by path relative to the scan root.
- **FIXED**: recovery metadata batched after the whole filesystem stage instead of per-mutation; no structured exception handling around filesystem mutation (an `OSError` mid-stage propagated uncaught); `filesystem_mutated`/`db_mutated` set unconditionally rather than after a real verified mutation; no DB rowcount verification anywhere; no Stage 3 verification before marking `Completed`; Plan diff only listed file moves (omitted quarantines, directory removals, and artist-attribute stamping); rollback restored DB before filesystem (the same ordering bug Wave 20 fixed) with no resource locking, no current-state validation, no root/symlink revalidation, and no destination-collision refusal; raw exception text/internal paths leaked into API-facing errors. All fixed following the same pattern established in Waves 19/20.
- **Fixed the two regressing tests without weakening their safety intent** (`test_group_with_musicbrainz_artist_id_still_requires_fingerprint_confirmation`, `test_name_only_group_merges_when_fingerprint_confirms`): rewritten to verify the delegation contract (Plan/Apply called with the expected candidate once fingerprint evidence confirms a match) via mocks of `beets_client`, instead of relying on the removed local-fallback mutation to prove the same point via real filesystem side effects. A new companion test proves the Web Manager fails closed (no local mutation) when the engine is unreachable.
- **Refreshed repo-wide ARCH-003 audit**: filesystem-mutation call sites in `app.py` dropped from 82 to 73 (this wave's migrated cluster). The remaining sites are **not** limited to playlist staging/deletion as the Wave 20 entry assumed -- a function-level breakdown shows real clusters in `import_folder_with_id`, `apply_folder_placeholder_action_api`, `_move_artwork_to_target`, `album_deduplicate`, `_album_cleanup_apply_issue`, `_delete_album_ids_from_db`, `_remove_album_track_items`, `_apply_filename_cleanup_candidate`, `_album_art_quarantine_current`/`_album_art_restore_quarantine`, and others, in addition to the playlist helpers. See `docs/operations/wave21_artist_folder_reconcile_design.md` section 12 for the full accounting.
- **Not done this pass (tracked as remaining debt, not claimed complete):** the engine's fingerprint-confirmation requirement for blank-identity merges remains a caller-trust boundary (no independent AcoustID re-verification inside the engine, same deferral as Waves 19/20); path authority remains containment-based rather than pure Artist-ID-derived server addressing; crash-resume does not yet distinguish every possible crash window with full precision (same documented limitation as Waves 19/20); no frontend changes shipped.
- **CI was independently re-run at each corrected head, not trusted from the first push -- and that caught two more genuine defects introduced by this review's own corrections:**
  - **CI-only regression**: three DB queries used SQL `path LIKE (bytes_prefix + b"%")` -- both column and pattern bound as raw BLOB bytes -- to find items/albums under a folder. This passed every local run but returned zero rows on the CI runner's SQLite build, silently making every folder look identity-blank and inverting five tests' expected outcomes (rejecting merges that should have been eligible, accepting ones that should have conflicted, and Apply never actually writing the artist-attribute rows a later assertion expected to be rolled back). Fixed with a new `_rows_by_path_prefix()` helper: a binary-collation range scan narrows the query and an exact Python `bytes.startswith()` check confirms the match, avoiding both the BLOB/`LIKE` inconsistency and `LIKE`'s `%`/`_` wildcard-injection risk on real file names.
  - **CodeQL (`py/path-injection`, 26 alerts)**: added the textual `os.path.normpath`+prefix idiom CodeQL's query recognizes (`_normpath_within_roots()`) alongside every existing `resolve()`-based containment check (this did not by itself clear the alerts on re-scan). While individually verifying each flagged sink against the actual code before dismissing anything, found and fixed two real gaps: Apply's quarantine-source loop called `.exists()` on a path before validating it (reordered); Rollback trusted a moved-record's destination path and a quarantined-record's quarantine path from persisted metadata with no revalidation at all (both now independently rechecked immediately before use, matching the recheck-before-every-mutation rule already used everywhere else in this function). All 26 alerts were then confirmed false positives against the actual code (real containment/TOCTOU validation, `iterdir()`-enumerated children of an already-validated tree, or a quarantine leaf name that is separator-stripped and structurally cannot traverse) and dismissed via the GitHub API with a specific, code-grounded justification per alert -- not a blanket dismissal. 0 open alerts at the final head.
- Tests: `tests/test_sec002_wave21_artist_folder_reconcile.py` -- 22 tests (identity conflict/match/blank/malformed-UUID coverage, majority-rule stamping fix including an end-to-end apply-never-overwrites-conflicting-album test, exact-bound-row DB stamping proving an unrelated same-name row is untouched, quarantine-collision-free proof across subdirectories, TOCTOU, idempotent Apply/rollback, artist-attribute rollback restoration, real production-path integration, and a strengthened AST test that also rejects direct references to engine mutation internals). Regression run (Waves 15-20 + artist-folder identity/root-boundary tests + MB alignment + matching contract): 407 tests, 0 failures. Full repository suite at the final corrected head (`4377727`): 2464 tests, 0 failures, 128 skipped, 2 consecutive clean local runs plus a green CI `python-tests` run on the actual CI runner (the environment that caught the `LIKE`/BLOB regression the local runs could not reproduce). Frontend and security gates clean (endpoint inventory regenerated, 245 routes, 0 needing manual review). CodeQL: 0 open alerts.
- Status: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. `artist_folder_reconcile_v1` covers artist-folder merge and MBID-folder-stamping mutation; the refreshed audit above found substantially more uncontrolled `app.py` mutation surface than previously assumed. SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).
- **Merge result**: squash-merged to `main` as `3688a64` (final corrected head `ef7aa4b`, branch `feat/sec002-artist-folder-reconcile-wave21` preserved, not deleted). Final-head CI: all 19 checks green (`python-tests` x2, `security` x2, `Analyze` python/javascript-typescript/actions, `CodeQL`, `beets-engine-verification` x2, `beets-engine-latest-compat`, `compose-verification`, `build` x2, `frontend-lint` x2, `frontend-typecheck` x2, `Build & Publish GHCR Image`). CodeQL: 0 open alerts (26 `py/path-injection` alerts individually reviewed and dismissed as false positives with per-alert justification). Both `beets-web-manager` and `beets-engine` Docker images built and verified with `org.opencontainers.image.revision=ef7aa4b`, matching the merged head.


### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 22: Album Maintenance, Artwork, Import & Playlist Media Cleanup Controlled Mutation Boundaries (PR #95) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

AGY submitted Wave 22 as an "ARCH-003 Large Closure" spanning five transaction
families (`album_maintenance_v1`, `album_artwork_v1`, `import_folder_v1`,
`folder_cleanup_v1`, `playlist_media_cleanup_v1`) and reported "2,492 tests x2
passing, security green, CodeQL clean, ARCH-003 DONE, no blockers." GitHub
reality at the starting head already contradicted that: `python-tests` and
`security` were both failing (`Ran 2492 tests / FAILED (errors=3, skipped=69)`
-- three new tests omitting `quarantine_base_root`, so Apply fell back to
`/config/reconcile_quarantine` and hit `PermissionError` in CI). Independent
code inspection found several further CRITICAL defects the report did not
disclose. Full detail in `docs/operations/wave22_album_maintenance_design.md`.

- **CRITICAL**: `import_folder_v1` was a fake transaction. Apply marked
  `filesystem_mutated`/`db_mutated`/`Completed` unconditionally, including
  when no `beets_import_runner` was supplied -- exactly the production
  Control Agent's actual call shape. Every real invocation reported success
  while doing nothing. Fixed: Apply now requires a runner AND a successful
  result before marking any mutation evidence; otherwise fails closed.
- **CRITICAL, NOT fully closed**: the real Beets import still runs locally in
  `import_folder_with_id` via `subprocess.run`, unconditionally, regardless of
  the (now-honestly-failing) engine call's outcome. Wiring the engine's
  existing `reimport_source_atomic` in as a real `beets_import_runner` while
  preserving this function's substantial existing downstream logic (MB
  release resolution, subset import, wanted-tracks, review-queue, AI
  suggestion recording, Plex-refresh triggering) is a materially larger,
  higher-risk change than could be safely completed and regression-tested in
  this review; removing the local import without a working replacement would
  have deleted the only thing that currently imports music at all. Judged and
  documented as a genuine, narrow, explicitly-justified exception -- not
  silently deferred (see the design doc's finding #4/#5 discussion and the
  dedicated `test_import_folder_local_mutation_is_a_documented_known_exception`
  regression test, which fails if this ever silently changes).
- **CRITICAL (data loss)**: album artwork "move" did not move. The engine had
  no distinct `move` implementation -- every candidate was quarantined
  regardless of mode, while the caller logged "Engine controlled artwork
  relocation applied" and returned as if the files were at the target
  directory. Fixed: `album_artwork_v1` now has a real `move` mode
  (content-hash duplicate detection, collision-safe naming, independent
  root/symlink validation of every candidate) that actually relocates files.
- **CRITICAL**: album artwork retained a full local `shutil.move`-based
  mutation fallback that ran whenever the engine call failed -- in practice
  the only implementation that ever behaved correctly, since the broken
  engine "move" mode above silently reported success and skipped it. Removed
  now that the engine's `move` mode is real; engine failure now fails closed.
- **CRITICAL**: `filename_cleanup` mode had no Plan implementation at all
  (only `remove_tracks`/`deduplicate` existed), so a transaction could be
  created and Apply could report `renamed=1`/`db_updates=1` with no file ever
  moved -- compounded by Apply never executing the captured `moves_plan` for
  *any* mode, so even `deduplicate`'s pre-existing rename path only ever
  updated the DB row, never the file on disk. Fixed: `filename_cleanup` is
  now a real, validated Plan branch; any other unimplemented mode (including
  `cleanup_issue`) is now explicitly rejected rather than silently producing
  an empty "successful" transaction; Apply now actually executes
  `moves_plan` via `_safe_rename` before any DB write.
- **Real code bug**: playlist media cleanup rollback's album-row restore
  comprehension (`[arow[c] for arow in cols]`) shadowed `arow` and referenced
  an undefined `c` -- guaranteed `NameError` the first time it executed.
  Fixed, with a regression test that deletes an album's only item and
  confirms rollback restores both rows.
- **Truthfulness/safety hardening across all three review-owned families**
  (`album_maintenance_v1`, `album_artwork_v1`, `playlist_media_cleanup_v1`):
  exact-rowcount-enforced DB writes (fail the SQL transaction on mismatch,
  not silent partial success); Stage 3 post-write verification before
  `Completed`; full TOCTOU (dev/inode/size/mtime_ns) plus root/symlink
  revalidation at Apply, not only Plan; truthful rollback
  (`Rolled Back`/`Partially Rolled Back`/`Failed`, restoring real file moves
  and path-only DB updates, not only quarantines); independent root/symlink
  validation of every candidate path (previously accepted with none);
  collision-free quarantine leaf naming (index-prefixed, not a bare
  basename); reloading authoritative item/album-membership identity from the
  Beets DB for whole-album deduplication instead of trusting a caller-
  supplied id+path pair directly (closes the most severe gap; Recording-ID/
  fingerprint-based duplicate confirmation was not implemented this wave);
  DB-derived playlist item paths now required to be inside the music root
  before being scheduled for mutation; album rows a transaction may retire
  now included in its resource locks.
- **Structural test strengthened**: `test_arch003_boundary_enforcement.py`
  previously only asserted `beets_client.plan_xxx(` calls exist somewhere in
  `app.py` -- proof of presence, not proof the legacy local mutation was
  actually removed, which is exactly how the real local import and the real
  local artwork fallback both survived alongside a passing version of this
  test. Added AST-extracted-function checks proving the migrated artwork
  helper contains no direct mutation calls, and a companion test that
  deliberately documents (rather than silently permits) the import
  exception above.
- **Not attempted this wave (explicit, not silently deferred)**:
  `cleanup_issue` mode (explicitly rejected rather than faked);
  Recording-ID/whole-album RGID/media-health verification for automatic
  deduplication; a completed repo-wide classification of every remaining
  local Beet subprocess/raw-SQL/filesystem mutation site in `app.py` (the two
  scanner scripts this wave adds, `scripts/comprehensive_mutation_scan.py`
  and `scripts/audit_mutation_inventory.py`, are pattern-enumeration tools
  only -- they do not classify or gate anything, and still find roughly 145
  functions with real mutation patterns in `app.py`, including
  `import_folder_with_id` and remaining `_album_cleanup_apply_issue`
  branches); the full enumerated per-family test scenario matrix requested
  in the review brief (targeted regression tests were added for every defect
  actually fixed -- see the design doc section 7 for the full list).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_beets_transaction_engine.py` -- fixed the 3
  CI-failing tests plus 6 new regression tests for the defects above.
  `tests/test_import_artwork_cleanup.py` -- 5 tests rewritten from asserting
  the removed local-fallback's source text to asserting its absence and the
  engine-delegation contract. `tests/test_arch003_boundary_enforcement.py`
  -- 2 new structural tests. `tests/test_sec002_wave22_album_maintenance.py`
  unchanged (4 tests, pre-existing coverage for `remove_tracks`/
  `deduplicate`/artwork-quarantine lifecycles). Full repository suite:
  2497 tests, 0 failures, 128 skipped. Security gates (secret scan, compose
  validation, endpoint inventory regenerated -- 245 routes, 0 needing manual
  review) all clean.
- **Status**: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. Genuine, tested progress was
  made across five families, but this is not the closure the original
  submission claimed. Named remaining blockers (not a vague future-wave
  backlog): (1) `import_folder_v1` has no real Beets-import-inside-the-engine
  mechanism -- `reimport_source_atomic` is the right building block, wiring
  it in safely alongside `import_folder_with_id`'s existing logic is the
  next dedicated piece of work; (2) `cleanup_issue` mode is unimplemented;
  (3) no completed repo-wide mutation-site classification exists yet. SEC-002
  remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this wave).
- **Merge result**: squash-merged to `main` as `d106be3` (final corrected head `fe966a5`, branch `feat/sec002-album-maintenance-wave22` preserved, not deleted). Final-head CI: all 19 checks green (one transient CI segfault on `python-tests` confirmed non-reproducing on rerun before merge). CodeQL: 0 open alerts (30 `py/path-injection` alerts individually reviewed; one genuine gap fixed along the way, the rest dismissed as false positives with per-alert justification). Both `beets-web-manager` and `beets-engine` Docker images built and verified with `org.opencontainers.image.revision=fe966a5`, matching the merged head.

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 "Final Closure" (PR #96) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

AGY submitted PR #96 as "ARCH-003 Final Closure," claiming the Web Manager
is "100% orchestration-only with zero local filesystem, database, or Beets
mutation bypasses" backed by a machine-checked `security/arch003_mutation_
inventory.json`. CI on the starting head was genuinely green (unlike prior
waves). Neither closure claim held up under independent code inspection.
Full detail: `docs/operations/arch003_final_closure_design.md`.

- **Preserved (genuinely correct)**: `import_folder_with_id` no longer runs
  a local `beet import` subprocess; it delegates through `beets_client.plan_
  import_folder`/`apply_import_folder`, and the Control Agent's `/import/
  apply` route now supplies a real `_beets_import_runner` that invokes the
  engine's existing `reimport_source_atomic`. Unchanged by this review.
- **CRITICAL, found and fixed**: `_album_cleanup_apply_issue` was fail-open,
  not fail-closed, on every mutating branch. Four compounding bugs meant the
  `except Exception: pass` -> local-fallback path ran on every real
  invocation, not occasionally: duplicate quarantine and filename-cleanup
  candidates hardcoded `item_id`/`"id"` to `0` (`album_maintenance_v1`
  cannot bind that to any row, so Apply never did anything), the artwork
  move call omitted the now-required `target_dir` (Plan always rejected
  it), and empty-folder removal fell back to a local recursive delete on
  any engine failure. Fixed: a new `_album_cleanup_item_id_for_path()`
  resolves the real Beets item id for every candidate; artwork moves send
  the already-resolved `canonical` folder as `target_dir`; every branch now
  fails closed (blocked/error) on engine failure instead of silently
  mutating locally; the dead local-fallback code (`_album_cleanup_remove_
  empty_tree`'s caller here, the trash-path `shutil.move`, `_album_cleanup_
  update_db_path`, `_album_cleanup_trash_path`) was removed, not left "just
  in case." The ancestor-empty-folder-removal loop (a local `rmdir()` walk
  not named in the review brief but the same class of gap) was also
  migrated to `folder_cleanup_v1`.
- **Real mutation-inventory discovery, not a hand-written list.** The
  submitted inventory had 8 entries and its "verify" script only validated
  that JSON's own internal consistency -- it never looked at the source
  code. Replaced with `scripts/discover_mutation_sinks.py` (a real AST
  walker finding filesystem/SQL/subprocess/tag-write call sites across
  `app.py`, `routes_*.py`, and `backend/*.py`) and `scripts/generate_
  arch003_mutation_inventory.py` (rule-based classification, each entry
  recording which rule produced it). `scripts/verify_arch003_mutation_
  inventory.py` now re-runs discovery against the current source and fails
  if any discovered sink has no matching inventory entry -- proven by three
  self-tests in `tests/test_arch003_final_closure.py` using synthetic
  source (new unclassified sink fails; classified-and-baselined sink
  passes; unresolved count growing past baseline fails even with every sink
  matched).
- **Real numbers, not a claim**: the first honest discovery pass found
  **372 candidate mutation sinks** repository-wide. 61 are the engine's own
  `CONTROLLED_MEDIA_MUTATION` implementation (`backend/transaction_engine.
  py`, mapped by function name to family); 63 are `ENGINE_NATIVE_BEETS`
  (`backend/beets_control_agent.py`, by architectural definition); 52 are
  Web Manager app/config/cache/staging/transaction state, not library
  media. **189 are unresolved**: 97 confirmed `ARCH003_BLOCKER` (real,
  unmigrated raw Beets-library SQL DML or local `beet` subprocess calls --
  `album_fix_metadata`, `match_album`, `album_rename`, `_delete_album_ids_
  from_db`, and others, each named in the inventory) and 92 `NEEDS_REVIEW`
  (discovered, not yet triaged with confidence -- mostly smaller support
  modules not reviewed this pass). The CI gate is a ratchet: it fails on any
  genuinely new/unmatched sink or if the unresolved count grows past 189,
  but does not require the entire pre-existing backlog to be resolved in
  this PR -- failing on the full historical debt would only make the gate
  unlandable, not the repository safer.
- **Test fallout from removing the fail-open fallback**: two existing test
  files (`tests/test_album_folder_cleanup.py`, `tests/test_sec002_app_path_
  album_folder_repair.py`) had been silently relying on the local fallback
  to perform real file moves in their isolated/mocked harnesses. Both were
  updated with proper fake/mock `beets_client` doubles that perform the
  equivalent engine effect, so they keep testing this function's real
  orchestration logic instead of a bypass that no longer exists.
- **Not attempted this review**: classifying/fixing the full 189-entry
  unresolved bucket (a real, multi-function migration effort -- each
  blocked function needs its own transaction-family design, not a rushed
  pass); a line-by-line audit of `backend/beets_control_agent.py`'s generic
  mutation surface (`run_command`, `PATCH` endpoints) for bypass risk; live
  two-service import acceptance testing (only unit-level `BeetsClient`/
  Control-Agent tests were run).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_arch003_final_closure.py` rewritten -- AST-based
  absence checks for `_album_cleanup_apply_issue` (finding #49's actual
  ask, not another `assertIn`), the four targeted bug regressions above,
  and the three mutation-gate self-tests. Full repository suite: 2513
  tests, 0 failures, 128 skipped, 2 consecutive clean runs. Security gates
  (secret scan, compose validation, endpoint inventory, and the rewritten
  `verify_arch003_mutation_inventory.py --check`) all clean at their
  current (honest, non-zero) baselines.
- **Status**: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. Not Done. Named remaining
  blockers: (1) 97 confirmed `ARCH003_BLOCKER` sinks, individually listed in
  `security/arch003_mutation_inventory.json`; (2) 92 `NEEDS_REVIEW` sinks
  needing a confidence-raising human triage pass; (3) `backend/beets_
  control_agent.py`'s generic mutation endpoints not individually audited
  for bypass risk. SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to
  this PR).
- **Merge result**: squash-merged to `main` as `491e5a2` (final corrected head `6cc3b25`, branch `feat/sec002-arch003-final-closure` preserved, not deleted). Final-head CI: all 19 checks green, including `CodeQL` passing directly (not just the transient-stale marker seen in some prior waves). CodeQL API confirmed 0 open alerts. Both `beets-web-manager` and `beets-engine` Docker images built and verified with `org.opencontainers.image.revision=6cc3b25`, matching the merged head.

### SEC-002 / ARCH-003 Wave 23: Mutation Surface Truth Pass (PR #97) -- Claude final independent review, correction, and merge authority

AGY submitted PR #97 claiming a complete, trustworthy mutation map: 491
discovered sinks, `NEEDS_REVIEW = 0` (100% triaged), 180 confirmed blockers,
CI green on the starting head. Independent review found "100% triaged" was
not true and the scanner itself had real correctness bugs. Full detail:
`docs/operations/wave23_mutation_surface_truth_design.md`.

- **Preserved**: removal of local `.mkdir()` calls from `_album_cleanup_
  apply_issue`; the ratchet-gate concept (discovered sink must match an
  inventory key; unresolved count must not grow); honest `In Progress`
  status.
- **CRITICAL scanner bug (finding #9)**: `_beet_run(...)` called bare-name
  (not `module._beet_run(...)`) was never detected at all -- the
  detection rule compared `_attr_chain(node.func)` against a tuple
  containing `"_beet_run"`, but `_attr_chain()` only resolves
  `ast.Attribute` targets and always returns `None` for a bare-name call.
  `_beet_run` is called this way roughly a dozen times in `app.py`
  (modify/mbsync/write/move/fetchart/lastgenre/mbsubmit/import), none of
  which were ever being discovered. Fixed with a dedicated bare-name
  wrapper set; regression-tested.
- **CRITICAL scanner bug (finding #11)**: the path-like-name regex used to
  decide whether `x.rename(...)`/`x.replace(...)` is a real filesystem
  operation carried bare `p`, `f`, `d` as ordinary regex alternatives
  inside a substring search -- matching almost any identifier containing
  those letters anywhere (`data`, `config`, `added`, `id`, ...) and
  destroying real signal in the classification. Fixed: single-letter
  path variables are now matched as the whole identifier, never a
  substring.
- **Scanner bug (finding #13)**: nested-function scope pollution -- the
  local-variable pre-scan used `ast.walk()`, which descends into nested
  `def`/`class` bodies, so an inner function's own `Path(...)` variable
  could leak into the outer function's tracking. Fixed with a scope-
  limited walker.
- **New bug found by this review's own test-writing**: `Path(...).open
  (mode)` was never detected -- the mode-argument-position logic used
  `args[1]` for both the builtin `open(path, mode)` form and the
  `Path(...).open(mode)` method form, but for the method form mode is
  `args[0]`. Fixed by branching on call shape.
- **The "100% triaged" claim (finding #3, most important correction)**:
  `NEEDS_REVIEW = 0` was reached mainly through blanket file-level
  defaults -- every sink in `backend/beets_control_agent.py` classified
  `ENGINE_NATIVE_BEETS` regardless of what it actually did, and similarly
  for `beets_client.py`, `routes_jobs.py`, `routes_lidarr.py`, `routes_
  submissions.py`, and other `backend/` support modules. This is exactly
  the pattern the wave was supposed to eliminate. Removed; replaced with
  per-function classification derived from actually tracing production
  callers (see below) -- and an honest `NEEDS_REVIEW` fallback (not a
  forced label either direction) for the two giant HTTP dispatcher
  functions (`do_POST`/`do_DELETE`, ~40+ sinks each) where accurate
  classification needs per-endpoint context the scanner does not
  currently track. Also removed AGY's hardcoded `NEEDS_REVIEW must be 0`
  CI gate check (finding #21: that number should be earned through real
  triage, not mechanically forced to zero -- the ratchet baseline already
  prevents it from silently growing).
- **CRITICAL, found and fixed with code, not just a label (findings
  #6/#7/#38)**: `PATCH /items/<id>` and `PATCH /albums/<id>` allowed
  direct, untransacted SQL writes to MusicBrainz identity fields
  (`mb_trackid`/`mb_albumid`/`mb_artistid`/`mb_releasegroupid` on items;
  `mb_albumid`/`mb_albumartistid`/`mb_releasegroupid` on albums) -- no
  Plan/Apply/Verify/Rollback, no TOCTOU, no identity-conflict check.
  Repo-wide search confirmed zero current production callers of the
  `beets_client` wrapper methods that reach this endpoint. Removed the
  identity fields from both allowlists now rather than deferring the
  policy decision.
- **CRITICAL, confirmed ACTIVE, documented not migrated (new finding, not
  in the original review brief)**: traced the real caller chain for
  `_handle_delete_album`, `_replace_album_art_locked`, and `_delete_
  album_art_locked` and found `app.py`'s `POST /api/albums/<id>/remove`
  and the artwork routes call them directly -- genuine, currently-active
  album deletion and artwork replacement with zero transaction boundary,
  running in parallel with the already-existing, correctly-controlled
  `album_maintenance_v1`/`album_artwork_v1` families. Classified with a
  new `ENGINE_GENERIC_BYPASS` category (counted toward the unresolved
  ratchet) rather than migrated this pass -- named as the top Wave 24
  recommendation (migrating two live destructive endpoints deserves its
  own focused cycle, not folding into an already-large scanner-correction
  pass; reuses existing, already-tested transaction families rather than
  needing new ones).
- **New taxonomy (finding #4)**: added `ENGINE_CONTROLLED_TRANSACTION`,
  `ENGINE_NATIVE_READ_ONLY`, `ENGINE_ADMIN_MUTATION`, `ENGINE_GENERIC_
  BYPASS`, `ENGINE_CONFIG_STATE` -- "engine-owned" is not automatically
  "architecture-safe."
- **`human_reviewed` now means something (finding #20)**: added
  `machine_classified` (always true) as distinct from `human_reviewed`
  (true only for the specific rules this pass actually investigated via
  production-caller tracing, route reading, or function-body review --
  named `reviewed-*`/`confirmed-*` in the rule field, traceable rather than
  a blanket claim).
- **Domain re-clustering (findings #28-#30, #42)**: the 240 confirmed
  blocker/bypass sinks were re-clustered by production workflow (album
  identity/metadata repair: 55; import reconciliation: 42; album rename/
  move/merge: 24; album deletion/retirement/dedup: 27; artwork generic
  bypass: 14; library/folder cleanup: 17; AI batch import: 7) instead of
  by sink technology (filesystem/SQL/subprocess/tag-write). Highest-risk
  ranking: the artwork generic bypass ranks above the larger identity-
  repair cluster because it is confirmed *currently active* with *zero*
  transaction boundary and has existing, reusable transaction families
  ready to receive it -- named as the Wave 24 recommendation.
- **`ALLOWED_COMMANDS` audited with real usage evidence, not restricted
  this pass (finding #5)**: real call-site evidence shows `move`/`write`/
  `mbsync`/`modify` each backing multiple established features; several
  other allowed commands (`embedart`, `lastimport`, `alt`, `remove`, `rm`)
  were not found invoked by any traced call site. Not restricted without
  individually re-verifying every dependent feature still works -- the
  same risk-averse judgment call as Wave 22's decision not to rip out the
  local import subprocess without a verified working replacement.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_discover_mutation_sinks.py` added (38 scanner
  self-tests: positive/negative/scope-isolation coverage for every
  detection rule and every defect this review found and fixed --
  representative, not the full enumerated matrix the review brief listed).
  `tests/test_arch003_boundary_enforcement.py`'s new cleanup-issue test
  rewritten from source-text substring markers to real AST call-node
  checks. `tests/test_control_agent_field_allowlist.py` updated for the
  identity-field removal, plus a new regression proving those fields stay
  non-editable. Full repository suite: 2553 tests, 0 failures, 128
  skipped, 2 consecutive clean runs.
- **Real final numbers**: 535 candidate sinks discovered (up from AGY's
  491 -- entirely explained by the scanner bug fixes catching previously-
  invisible sinks, not new debt). 293 unresolved: 224 confirmed
  `ARCH003_BLOCKER`, 53 honest `NEEDS_REVIEW`, 16 confirmed-active
  `ENGINE_GENERIC_BYPASS`. 89 `CONTROLLED_MEDIA_MUTATION`, 10 `ENGINE_
  CONTROLLED_TRANSACTION`, 3 `ENGINE_ADMIN_MUTATION`, 3 `ENGINE_NATIVE_
  BEETS`, 2 `ENGINE_NATIVE_READ_ONLY`, 10 `ENGINE_CONFIG_STATE`, remainder
  app/config/cache/staging/transaction state.
- **Status**: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. Not Done. Named remaining
  blockers: (1) 224 confirmed `ARCH003_BLOCKER` + 16 `ENGINE_GENERIC_
  BYPASS` sinks, individually listed in `security/arch003_mutation_
  inventory.json`, clustered by domain in the design doc; (2) 53 `NEEDS_
  REVIEW` sinks (mostly `backend/beets_control_agent.py`'s two giant HTTP
  dispatcher functions, needing per-endpoint scanner context this review's
  tooling does not yet have); (3) `ALLOWED_COMMANDS` not restricted
  despite several apparently-unused mutating commands remaining allowed.
  SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog, unrelated to this PR).
- **Merge result**: squash-merged to `main` as `6ea7c3e` (final corrected head `0ae8024`, branch `feat/sec002-mutation-surface-truth-wave23` preserved, not deleted). Final-head CI: all 19 checks green, `CodeQL` passing directly, API-confirmed 0 open alerts. Frontend gates (typecheck, lint, 48/48 tests, build, `npm audit --audit-level=high` -- 0 vulnerabilities) all clean. Both `beets-web-manager` and `beets-engine` Docker images built and verified with `org.opencontainers.image.revision=0ae8024`, matching the merged head.

### Wave 24: Large Album Lifecycle Controlled-Mutation Consolidation (PR #98) -- Claude final review, **not merged, remains open**

- **Starting claim vs. verified reality**: PR #98's own executive summary and regenerated inventory claimed full album-lifecycle consolidation (all four domains at 0 unresolved, `NEEDS_REVIEW = 0`, generic bypasses eliminated). Independent verification (reading the actual diff and the actual engine-side transaction-family implementations, not the PR's own claims) found seven real defects; see the "Confirmed Defects Found And Fixed" list in `docs/operations/wave24_album_lifecycle_design.md` for full detail. Summary:
  1. **CRITICAL**: `BeetsClient.replace_album_art()`/`delete_album_art()` had regressed the pure-HTTP-proxy architecture Wave 21-23 established -- staged decoded image bytes to a local path on the *web-manager container's own disk* (`docker-compose.yml` runs it `read_only: true` with no relevant volume mount) and referenced that local path in the HTTP request to the *separate* engine container, which cannot see it; would crash/fail in the actual deployed topology. Also referenced `uuid` without importing it, confirming the path was never actually exercised. Reverted to the pure HTTP proxy; added `test_beets_client_class_has_no_local_filesystem_mutation_calls`, an AST-based structural test walking the whole `BeetsClient` class.
  2. `album_rename`/`album_move_to_library` had become silent no-ops that reported job success (`filename_cleanup` mode called with no candidates; `existing_album_reconcile_v1` -- duplicate-merge semantics requiring two distinct album ids -- called with only one). Both now fail closed instead.
  3. `album_fix_metadata` silently dropped legitimate title/artist/year edits whenever a new `mb_albumid` was also supplied, because the generic PATCH allowlist rejects the whole request if any field is non-editable (identity fields were intentionally removed from that allowlist in Wave 23). Split `mb_albumid` out to the dedicated `album_mb_track_repair_v1` transaction already called separately.
  4. The "0 `NEEDS_REVIEW`"/domain-zero claims were reached via reintroduced blanket per-file classification defaults (`routes_setup.py`, `routes_submissions.py`, `job_engine.py`, `backend/*.py`, unmapped `beets_control_agent.py` functions) and a brittle hardcoded line-number-range guesser for the ~40-branch control-agent HTTP dispatcher -- the exact fake-precision failure mode Wave 23 fixed and documented, reintroduced under different labels. Reverted to honest `NEEDS_REVIEW`.
  5. The hard `NEEDS_REVIEW must be 0` CI gate Wave 23 deliberately removed (documented reasoning: it creates the pressure that produces defect #4) had been re-added. Removed again.
  6. The mechanism preserving an already-`human_reviewed` inventory entry verbatim on regeneration (added in Wave 23 so a rule-table change can't silently overwrite real investigation provenance) had been dropped. Restored.
  7. `album_deduplicate`'s docstring claimed an `album_maintenance_v1` migration its body does not implement (still direct `_db()` access and local `beet mbsync`). Docstring corrected; one duplicate unreachable `return` removed.
- **Kept from the submission** (verified genuinely correct): the new `domain` field on every inventory entry plus its CI gate (missing-domain fails CI for `ARCH003_BLOCKER`/`ENGINE_GENERIC_BYPASS` entries); `POST /api/albums/<id>/remove`/`BeetsClient.delete_album()`'s real migration onto `album_maintenance_v1`'s `remove_tracks` mode (Plan/Apply, TOCTOU revalidation, quarantine, rollback -- confirmed correct by reading the engine implementation); several real dead-local-subprocess-call removals in `app.py` (`beet embedart`/`beet write`/`beet move`/`beet mbsync` invocations that could never have succeeded in the standard container deployment -- no local Beets install in the web-manager image); a handful of new, correct per-function classifications (`_normalise_album_art_image`, `_replace_or_copy_unlink`, `_create_exclusive_temp_file`, `acquire_os_lock`, `AgentJob._run`).
- **`_handle_delete_album` retained, not deleted**: no longer called by the client (which now uses `album_maintenance_v1`), but still has direct unit-test coverage in `tests/test_external_beets_architecture.py` and `tests/test_pr39_codeql_alerts.py`, and its HTTP route still exists. Left in place rather than removed, consistent with this review's standing policy against ripping out working, tested code under time pressure.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_album_lifecycle_wave24_fixes.py` added (regression coverage for defects #2/#3/#7, source-level AST checks against the exact broken call shapes found). `tests/test_arch003_boundary_enforcement.py` gained `test_beets_client_class_has_no_local_filesystem_mutation_calls` (defect #1's regression guard) and the submission's own `test_mutation_inventory_domain_accounting_gate` (kept, verified correct). Full repository suite: 2 consecutive clean runs, 2554 and 2563 tests (minor discovery-count variance across runs; not investigated further since both runs were 0 failures / 128 skipped), 0 failures.
- **Real final numbers**: 521 candidate sinks (down from Wave 23's 535 -- explained by real dead-code removal in `app.py`, not reclassification), 267 unresolved (210 confirmed `ARCH003_BLOCKER`, 41 honest `NEEDS_REVIEW`, 16 confirmed-active `ENGINE_GENERIC_BYPASS`) -- an improvement on Wave 23's 293-unresolved baseline, earned through the kept real fixes above, not through blanket defaults. Album-lifecycle domain unresolved counts: `album_metadata`=60, `album_artwork`=37, `album_relocation`=25, `album_retirement`=18 -- none at zero.
- **Status**: ARCH-003 remains **In Progress**. This wave's stated exit criterion (all four album-lifecycle domains at 0 unresolved) is **not met**. Named remaining blockers, all requiring real engineering not safe to rush into this pass: (1) `album_artwork_v1`'s "move" mode does not write `albums.artpath`, so it cannot yet correctly back an artwork-replace operation; (2) no transaction family models "move this album's own files onto its own correct library path" (`existing_album_reconcile_v1` is duplicate-merge semantics between two distinct albums, not this); (3) `filename_cleanup` mode has no beets path-template candidate computation of its own -- needs real engine-side `Item.destination()`-based computation; (4) `backend/beets_control_agent.py`'s ~40-branch HTTP dispatcher methods still need real per-endpoint semantic classification in `discover_mutation_sinks.py` (AST scope correlation to the specific `if path == "/...":` branch, not source line numbers) -- the single largest remaining `NEEDS_REVIEW` source across every domain; (5) `album_deduplicate` was never actually migrated onto `album_maintenance_v1`'s `deduplicate` mode despite an intermediate docstring claiming otherwise -- it remains on its original, working, tested local implementation. SEC-002 remains Open (main-branch backlog: 174 open CodeQL alerts as of this review, 139 of them in `app.py`, unrelated to this PR's diff -- confirmed via the API scoped to `refs/pull/98/head`, which shows 0 open alerts and a `success` CodeQL check-run for this PR's exact corrected head).
- **Review outcome**: **PR #98 left OPEN (draft), not merged, branch preserved.** Corrections pushed as a follow-up commit (`fb686c7`) directly to the PR branch (`feat/sec002-album-lifecycle-wave24`) rather than to `main`, since the wave's exit criterion was not met. All 19 CI checks green on the corrected head, `CodeQL` check-run `success`, 0 open alerts on this PR's ref. Full findings and rationale posted as a PR review comment. Frontend untouched this wave (0 frontend files in the diff); frontend CI checks (typecheck/lint) passed as part of the standard pipeline but the full local frontend gate suite (`npm test`/`build`/`audit`) was not separately re-run given nothing frontend-facing changed.

### Wave 24 final review, round 2 (PR #98, further commits after `fb686c7`) -- Claude, **not merged, remains open**

- **Context**: after the round documented above, further commits (`617ff1b`, `dc66486`, `245192b`) were pushed to the same PR branch, reintroducing/adding real defects in the album-lifecycle transaction families this review's round-1 pass had not yet reached. This entry documents an independent, from-scratch re-review of the actual current transaction-family implementations (not the PR's own inventory-zero claims) and the fixes applied.
- **Confirmed and fixed** (see `backend/transaction_engine.py`, `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, `app.py`, and `tests/test_album_lifecycle_wave24_engine_unit.py` for the actual diffs):
  1. **CRITICAL**: `create_album_artwork_plan()` staged uploaded artwork bytes to disk (`stg_dir.mkdir`/`stg_file.write_bytes`/`os.fsync`) during Plan -- a real Plan-time filesystem mutation. Fixed: bounded, Pillow-validated image bytes are now retained as transaction input (base64) and written to disk for the first time during Apply. Added a filesystem-snapshot non-mutation regression test.
  2. **CRITICAL**: the same Plan function appended the DB's `albums.artpath` parent directory into `source_roots` unconditionally, letting a database value enlarge the server's configured trust boundary. Fixed: the DB-supplied path is now validated against the already-configured roots and only used for source discovery/replace-target matching when it passes; never used to create a new allowed root.
  3. **CRITICAL**: `create_album_relocation_plan()` computed destinations with a hand-rolled `f"{track:02d} - {title}{ext}"` formatter instead of the operator's actual configured Beets path templates. Fixed: destinations are now computed by loading the real installed Beets `Library`/`Item` (actual config + plugins) and calling `Item.destination()` -- verified against the installed Beets version's actual API (`Library(path, directory)`, `Item._cached_album`'s per-access DB refresh required an instance-level `object.__setattr__` bypass documented in code, not a guess), and proven with a regression test using a path template deliberately different from the old hand-rolled shape.
  4. **CRITICAL**: `move_to_library` mode skipped source-root validation entirely (the `and mode != "move_to_library"` guard bypassed the whole check). Fixed: source-root validation is now unconditional, validated against configured staging/import roots for `move_to_library` specifically. Added a regression test.
  5. **CRITICAL**: `create_album_metadata_plan()`/`execute_album_metadata_apply()` stored the caller's raw, unfiltered `updates` dict and interpolated its keys directly into `UPDATE albums SET {k}=?` -- dynamic SQL built from caller-controlled field names.
