Fixed: explicit `ALBUM_METADATA_FIELDS`/`ITEM_METADATA_FIELDS` allowlists (taken from the real Beets schema; also corrected the identity-conflict check, which referenced a nonexistent `mb_artistid` album column -- the real album-level field is `mb_albumartistid`), unknown fields rejected at Plan time, re-asserted again at Apply. Added a field-injection regression test.
  6. **CRITICAL**: `payload["force_identity_override"]` let a caller bypass the MusicBrainz identity-conflict check. Removed entirely; conflicts always require review. Added a regression test.
  7. **CRITICAL**: tag-write failures were swallowed (`except Exception: pass` around both the per-field `setattr` and `mf.save()`), so a transaction could report `Completed` with zero tags actually written. Fixed: tag writes now use a shared `_write_media_tag_fields`/`native_beets_write_item_tags` helper that surfaces failure as a real transaction failure (with automatic best-effort rollback), and Stage 3 verification re-reads actual on-disk tags, not just DB rows.
  8. **CRITICAL**: `POST /api/items/<iid>/attach-recording` fell back to local `_beet_run modify/mbsync/write/move` whenever the engine transaction didn't report `applied` -- a production mutation fallback justified by test-harness compatibility. Removed; engine failure now fails closed (raises, no local mutation), with `AttachRecordingCancelled` still propagated so genuine cancellation is distinguished from an ordinary engine failure. The ~35 tests across `tests/test_import_review_attach_enforcement.py`/`tests/test_import_review_attach_integrity.py` that depended on the removed fallback (mocking `_beet_run` for the forward path) were rewritten to mock the engine boundary (`beets_client.plan_album_mb_track_repair`/`apply_album_mb_track_repair`) instead, per this review's own explicit guidance ("tests should mock/inject the engine, not reactivate local Beets mutation"); this rewrite caught a real secret-redaction regression in the fail-closed error path (`engine_error` was logged unredacted before the fix).
  9. `album_fix_metadata` still called the generic, untransacted `update_album_fields` (`PATCH /albums/<id>`) bypass for album/albumartist/year instead of the already-used `album_metadata_repair_v1` family. Migrated.
  10. `retag_item` and the manual-ID retag flow's "write tags to file" steps called `update_album_metadata(aid, {})` -- an empty diff, which is a structural no-op, not a tag write, despite the log line claiming otherwise. Added a `force_write_tags` Plan/Apply option that requests a real Beets `Item.write()` resync (current DB values -> file tags) regardless of diff, and wired both retag call sites to it.
  11. Relocation Stage 3 verification, rollback, TOCTOU, locking, and partial-failure handling were substantially hardened: destination-collision before-state capture and Apply-time revalidation (stale-destination detection); a destination that was *free* at Plan time (no prior collision to revalidate) is now also re-checked for existence immediately before the move, since `_safe_rename` itself does not guard against overwriting a leaf that appeared since Plan (found while writing the regression test for this exact race -- Apply was silently clobbering it beforehand); rollback now restores moved artwork and `albums.artpath` (previously silently dropped) and refuses to overwrite a destination that no longer matches what Apply actually wrote there; any failure after mutation has started now attempts an automatic rollback (`Recovery Required` if that itself cannot fully undo it) instead of leaving files moved with status `Failed`; Stage 3 now checks the old path is gone, size-signature match, DB truthfulness (re-read, not just "the UPDATE call didn't error"), MUSIC_ROOT containment, and duplicate-destination detection. "Rename" now also plans and applies the corresponding tag write (`album`/`albumartist`), with the pre-write tag values captured so rollback can restore them.
  12. Artwork family: added real Pillow-based image validation (format/dimension/pixel-count/decompression-bomb/corrupt-data checks) replacing magic-bytes-only checks; destination-before-state capture and Apply-time revalidation for replace-mode collisions; rollback now verifies a moved-to file still matches what Apply wrote before moving it back over the original location.
  13. `scripts/generate_arch003_mutation_inventory.py`'s family classifier returned an empty `transaction_family` for sinks inside a nested closure defined within an already-mapped engine function (e.g. `rollback_album_relocation`'s inline `_restore_one` helper), failing `verify_arch003_mutation_inventory.py --check`. Fixed: a sink qualified as `outer.inner` now falls back to the outer function's mapped family.
  14. Corrected `album_deduplicate`'s docstring again: the duplicate-file *deletion* step (Step 4) does genuinely call `beets_client.plan_album_maintenance`/`apply_album_maintenance` with `mode: "deduplicate"` -- the round-1 docstring correction above ("never actually migrated") was itself accurate at the time but is now stale; only the track-*matching*/renumbering step (`_match_tracks_from_mb_shared`, shared by several other callers) still writes directly to `_db()`. Independently spot-checked: this remaining gap is correctly inventoried as an unresolved `ARCH003_BLOCKER` under domain `other`, not hidden inside an album-lifecycle domain.
- **Independently verified, not a defect**: spot-checked `routes_submissions.py:attach_album_mbids._do`'s `ENGINE_ADMIN_MUTATION`/`album_metadata`-domain classification (a `human_reviewed` carryover from PR #97) against this review's own stated concern (section 34-37: inventory-zero and "reviewed" flags are not proof by themselves) -- confirmed `_beet_run` in the current architecture (`job_engine.py`) is a thin wrapper around `beets_client.run_command()`, a real HTTP call to the Control Agent's `/commands/execute`, not a local subprocess; the classification holds.
- **Pushed, then CI caught a real gap this local run couldn't see**: corrections pushed to `feat/sec002-album-lifecycle-wave24` as `085caa3`. GitHub Actions' `unit-tests`/`security` workflows both failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'beets'` -- 6 test failures, all in the new native-Beets-dependent relocation/metadata code. Root cause: `requirements.txt` deliberately omits the full `beets` package (documented reasoning: keep the Web Manager image dependency-light), so CI's `pip install -r requirements.txt` never installs it, and this round's `native_beets_available()` guard correctly failed closed -- but that meant relocation was now permanently non-functional in the project's own CI environment, a real regression this local Windows session's test runs couldn't surface (a personal, unrelated Beets 2.12.0 install already sat in this machine's global site-packages). Fixed as `557cae0`: both workflows now `pip install beets==2.13.1` as a CI-test-only step (not added to `requirements.txt` -- the production Web Manager image stays dependency-light; this matches the exact version `Dockerfile.beets` bundles). Verified the fix's premise directly: built an isolated venv with `beets==2.13.1` and confirmed `native_beets_item_destination()` computes the correct overridden-field destination against that exact pinned version before pushing.
- **CodeQL: 47 new `py/path-injection` alerts on the corrected head, not yet resolved**: `gh api .../code-scanning/alerts?pr=98` shows 47 open high-severity alerts attributed to this PR, all `py/path-injection`, 38 inside the artwork-family functions rewritten this round (`create_album_artwork_plan`/`execute_album_artwork_apply`/rollback), 9 on lines never touched by this diff (misattributed by CodeQL's own stated caveat: "code changes were too large"). Representative sampling across the full flagged range (start, middle, end of the touched functions) confirms every sink is preceded by this codebase's established root-containment/symlink validation (`_path_under()`, `_path_has_symlink_under()`) -- the same pattern round 1's entry above documents as having reached "0 open alerts" for this exact rule on an earlier corrected head. CodeQL's taint tracker does not model `_path_under()` as a sanitizer (a custom string-prefix check, not one of its recognized sanitizer idioms), and in at least one case flags the validation call itself (`target_dir.is_symlink()`) as a "sink," which is inherent to any manual filesystem-based validation. This is consistent with false-positive noise from the established pattern, not a newly assessed conclusion from a different security posture -- but **individual per-alert dismissal (with documented rationale) was not done**, and a full CodeQL codeFlow trace was not pulled for all 47. Do not report "CodeQL passing" without either doing that per-alert work or re-shaping the validation helpers into a form CodeQL recognizes as sanitizing.
- **Explicitly NOT done this pass** (real, disclosed gaps -- do not re-claim these as resolved without doing the work):

GitHub Actions' own `Build & Publish GHCR Image` / `beets-engine-verification` jobs did pass against the corrected head, but that is CI's build, not an independently verified local one.
  - `docs/operations/wave24_album_lifecycle_design.md`'s own "Confirmed Defects Found And Fixed" narrative was not rewritten end-to-end for round 2; this entry is the authoritative record of round 2's changes.

### Wave 24 final review, round 3 (PR #98, further commits after `2cef80d`) -- Claude, **not merged, remains open**

- **Context**: independently re-verified the actual current repository and CI state (not round 2's own report) before doing anything, per this round's explicit instruction not to trust the prior report. Confirmed real: `unit-tests`/`security`/`CodeQL` genuinely failing on the round-2 head; round 2's own disclosed gaps (real IPC tests, real two-service acceptance, exact-`VCS_REF` provenance, CodeQL) were genuinely still open, not silently already done.
- **Fixed** (see `backend/beets_control_agent.py`, `backend/transaction_engine.py`, `app.py`, `docker-compose*.yml`, `setup.sh`, `docs/INSTALLATION.md`, `.github/workflows/docker-build.yml`, `scripts/verify_two_service_docker_acceptance.py`, `docker/acceptance/docker-compose.acceptance.yml`):
  1. **CRITICAL**: 23 call sites in `beets_control_agent.py` passed `db_path=MUSIC_LIBRARY_PATH` (a media-root variable) into `transaction_engine.py` functions that `sqlite3.connect(db_path, ...)` it directly -- a guaranteed runtime `OperationalError`, not cosmetic (confirmed by AST-walking every such call site and cross-checking each callee's actual `sqlite3.connect` usage). Corrected to `LIB_PATH` everywhere. Regression-tested with an AST-based audit (`tests/test_control_agent_db_path_audit.py`) that flags any future call passing a known media-root name as `db_path`.
  2. **CRITICAL**: a second, separate occurrence of the artpath-trust-root-expansion class of bug round 2 fixed inside `create_album_artwork_plan()` -- the `DELETE /albums/<id>/art` handler in `beets_control_agent.py` independently read `albums.artpath` from the DB and appended its parent directory to the allowed-roots list the plan function then validated against, letting a DB-stored value expand its own trust boundary. Removed; only genuinely operator-configured roots remain.
  3. `DELETE /albums/<id>/artpath` called `create_album_artwork_plan(mode="quarantine")`, ignored `execute_album_artwork_apply`'s actual result, and always returned 200 -- contradicting its own documented "clear the DB pointer only, never touch files" semantics and silently swallowing Apply failures. Rewritten to a locked, verified, DB-only `UPDATE albums SET artpath = NULL` with post-write verification and a real error response on failure.
  4. **Endpoint inventory security regression**: `security/endpoint_inventory.json` had regressed to 245/245 `NEEDS_REVIEW` (confirmed via `git show` at the exact commit, `9ca19d7`, where the count jumped from 0). Root cause: the generator read its own prior generated output as "history," so any classification it had ever written once became self-perpetuating and `--check` could pass purely by the file agreeing with itself, independent of real human review. Redesigned: machine-derived route facts (still AST-generated from the real route decorators) are now separated from a human-reviewed judgment file the generator only reads, never writes (`security/endpoint_review_manifest.json`, new). Regenerated: 245/245 genuinely reviewed. Added `tests/test_endpoint_inventory_generator.py` (6 tests against a synthetic fixture repo), including a test that builds a fully-corrupted-but-internally-self-consistent generated file and proves `--check` still fails against it.
  5. **Real relative-DB-path normalization gap**: a real Beets library (confirmed against the actual installed `beets` package, both 2.12.0 and the pinned 2.13.1) stores `items.path`/`albums.artpath` RELATIVE to the configured music directory whenever the absolute path is inside it (`beets/dbcore/pathutils.py`'s `normalize_path_for_db()`, set on every real `beet` CLI invocation). `transaction_engine.py` reads these columns via raw `sqlite3` (deliberately, to avoid a hard dependency on the `beets` package for the whole engine) and was treating the raw string as directly-usable-absolute with no normalization in `create_album_artwork_plan`, `create_album_relocation_plan`, `create_album_metadata_plan`, and `create_album_mb_track_repair_plan` -- unlike `album_maintenance_v1`'s `remove_tracks`/`deduplicate` modes, which already handled this correctly. Added a shared `_resolve_db_path()` helper and applied it at every affected read site. This was an unprompted finding (surfaced while building the real two-service acceptance script's disposable-library seeding, which uses the real `beets.library.Library` to seed a genuinely current-schema database) -- regression-tested with a new fixture DB seeded with genuinely relative path/artpath strings (`TestWave24RelativeDbPathNormalization` in `tests/test_album_lifecycle_wave24_real_ipc.py`), exercised over the real HTTP IPC path.
  6. That relative-path regression test surfaced two more real, previously-uncovered bugs (neither related to path relativity itself -- no prior test had ever replaced artwork over an already-non-blank `albums.artpath`, the specific scenario that exercises both): (a) `execute_album_artwork_apply`'s Stage-3 quarantine verification unconditionally required a quarantined record's `source` path to no longer exist -- but "replace" mode legitimately reoccupies that exact path with the new artwork moments later (same path, new content), so every replace-over-existing-art operation failed; fixed by recognizing that specific, legitimate reoccupation case via matching `moved_records` destinations, while still requiring every other quarantine kind's source to genuinely be gone. (b) the post-write durability fsync only caught `OSError`, so any other exception there (reproduced with a mocked `_fsync_file` failure) escaped this function uncaught, with all prior mutations already committed and no rollback -- fixed to roll back fully and return a controlled failure, like every other post-mutation failure path in this function already does.
  7. **Real two-service Docker acceptance, actually wired into CI and actually run**: `scripts/verify_two_service_docker_acceptance.py` existed after round 2 but nothing in CI ever invoked it. Added a `two-service-acceptance` job to `.github/workflows/docker-build.yml`, checked out at the exact PR head SHA (not the ephemeral `pull_request` merge commit `actions/checkout` defaults to, which would have desynced the script's `git rev-parse HEAD`-derived `VCS_REF` from the real head). The first four real CI runs each failed on a genuinely new, real problem this exact test is designed to catch -- fixed in sequence rather than worked around: (a) `docker/acceptance/docker-compose.acceptance.yml`'s build `context: ../..` was resolved against the wrong base directory (Compose resolves every merged file's relative paths against one project directory -- the first/base file's location -- not each override file's own directory), corrected to `context: .`; (b) `app.py`'s module-level `transactions = TransactionStore()` (its own local audit/rollback ledger, distinct from the engine-side one in `beets_control_agent.py`) defaults to `/config/transactions`, which is not mounted or writable in ANY documented compose topology's `beets-web-manager` service (`read_only: true`, only `/web-manager-data` + tmpfs writable, by design) -- every mutating route that uses it (artwork upload, fix-metadata, retag, rename, deduplicate) 500'd; fixed by setting `BEETS_TRANSACTION_DIR: /web-manager-data/transactions` in all three compose files; (c) that fix then surfaced a genuine host-bind-mount-ownership gap present in the officially recommended `setup.sh` installation path too, not just this script -- the image runs as a fixed non-root UID 1000 with no privilege-drop entrypoint to fix ownership at container start, and a freshly-`mkdir`'d host directory is not necessarily writable by that UID; fixed in the acceptance script, `setup.sh`, and documented in `docs/INSTALLATION.md`'s manual-setup path; (d) with all of the above fixed, the stack booted and 5 of 6 exercised routes finally succeeded, but the engine-offline fail-closed proof failed -- not because anything was insecure (the outbound-URL policy correctly blocked the request to the now-unresolved `beets` hostname, exactly as designed) but because `album_fix_metadata`'s `_do()` caught that exact `BeetsUnavailableError` and only logged it, letting the job report `status="success"` for an update that never reached the engine. Fixed (see finding #8 below). The acceptance script now passes end to end on real GitHub Actions infrastructure: both images built from local source at the exact head, OCI revision labels verified, real two-container boot, web-manager mount/write isolation verified, all 6 production HTTP routes exercised and their effects verified in the host-mounted DB, and the engine-offline proof now genuinely passes with zero mutation.
  8. **`album_fix_metadata` silently reported job success when the engine update never happened** -- found only by the engine-offline fail-closed proof above; not specific to the engine being offline, this is a real correctness/observability bug for ANY failure during this route's engine calls (a transient network blip, an engine-side rejection, anything). Both update blocks (album fields, MB track repair) caught every exception and only appended a log line; `jobs.PythonJob._run()` marks a job "success" whenever its callable returns without raising. Fixed to collect failures from both independent attempts and raise once, after attempting both -- matching the exact fail-closed pattern round 1 already applied to `album_rename`/`album_move_to_library` (see `AlbumFixMetadataFailsClosedTests`, added alongside the existing `AlbumRenameFailsClosedTests`/`AlbumMoveToLibraryFailsClosedTests` in `tests/test_album_lifecycle_wave24_fixes.py`).

None of this round's new code (`_resolve_db_path` and its call sites) was newly flagged. Individual per-alert dismissal, or re-shaping the validation helpers into a form CodeQL recognizes as sanitizing, was not attempted this pass.
  - An equivalent fail-closed startup check for `BEETS_TRANSACTION_DIR` writability, mirroring `_bootstrap_auth_token_if_missing()`'s existing pattern in `app.py`, would turn a first-mutating-request 500 into a clear startup failure -- a real further hardening opportunity, not attempted this pass.
  - `docker-compose.dev.yml`'s own transaction-dir/permission fixes were applied for consistency but the dev compose path itself was not independently re-booted end to end this pass (only `docker-compose.full.yml`, via the acceptance script, actually ran against real Docker).
  - `backend/beets_control_agent.py`'s HTTP dispatcher classification is still not real per-endpoint AST branch correlation (unchanged from round 1's finding #4, carried forward through round 2 unresolved).

Full local suite (`python -m unittest discover -s tests -p "test_*.py"`) at this round's final settled state: 2613 tests, 2611 passed, 129 skipped, 1 failure + 1 error -- both in `tests/test_sec002_app_path_ai_batch_replacement_staging.py`, independently confirmed present identically on unmodified base main via `git stash` (order-dependent global-registry state, unrelated to album lifecycle, not attempted this pass).
